# Candidate mathematical and machine-learning branches: eight briefs

*Written by eight independent research agents for the phase-3 pivot. Same conventions as BIOLOGY.md. One brief (continual learning and consolidation) notes that its web access was blocked and that its citations come from recall: treat them as unverified.*

## Test-Time Learning and Fast Weights in 2025-2026 Sequence Models: Update Rules, Scaling Evidence, Capacity Limits, and the Open Frontier for a Mushroom-Body-Aligned Memory

*Key: `test-time-learning`.*

### Summary

Everything in the 2024-2026 fast-weight family is one recurrence on a matrix state S read by o = S^T q, and the only real differences are the transition and the write. Reading the reference kernels rather than the press releases: DeltaNet is exactly one SGD step on 0.5||S^T k - v||^2; Gated DeltaNet adds weight decay (decay first, then correct against the decayed state); KDA makes that decay per-channel; GDN-2 (2026) decouples a per-channel erase gate on the key axis from a per-channel write gate on the value axis; RWKV-7 generalises to a diagonal-plus-low-rank transition; DeltaProduct takes n_h micro-steps per token; Titans adds momentum and a forget gate; LaCT does the same in 2K-1M-token chunks on SwiGLU fast weights with per-channel learning rates, data-dependent momentum, Muon orthogonalisation and homeostatic weight renormalisation; MesaNet solves the local ridge regression exactly by 30 CG steps (a literal inverse-covariance, i.e. precision-weighted, read); PGDN (2026) preconditions the write by an accumulated per-channel second moment. The production answer is settled: gated delta rule at 3:1 with full attention inside an MoE - Qwen3-Next (48 layers, full_attention_interval 4), Qwen3.5/3.6/3.8, Kimi Linear (27 layers, KDA head_dim 128 x 32 heads, 1M context), OlmoHybrid. No pure recurrent model is state of the art anywhere. Where they lose to attention is quantified and stark: at ~400M with matched 12.5M state, GDN falls from 99-100 % single-needle retrieval at 1-4K to 41.8 % at 16K and 22.1 % at 32K (multi-needle 6.2 % and 4.0 %), while a Forgetting Transformer gets 64.3 on FDA against GDN's 8.3 - yet all eight architectures are within 0.7 points on zero-shot commonsense, so perplexity cannot support an architecture claim at this scale. The frontier is the write gate, and the unexploited axis is key correlation: every model uses dense L2-normalised keys, so interference proportional to <k_i, k_j> is never attacked. The mushroom body's sparse expansion (fan-in ~7, ~5 % k-WTA, dimension-maximising by Litwin-Kumar 2017) and recall-gated consolidation (Lindsey 2024: slow writes gated by fast recall, giving linear rather than logarithmic timescale growth) are the two fly mechanisms with no counterpart in this literature, and both are falsifiable at 100-400M on recall benchmarks against controls that already ship in flash-linear-attention.

### Established (with sources)

- The whole 2024-2026 fast-weight family is one recurrence on a matrix state S_t in R^{d_k x d_v} read by o_t = S_t^T q_t; architectures differ only in the transition matrix and the write. Verified by reading the reference implementations in fla-org/flash-linear-attention (fla/ops/*/naive.py), which are the de-facto standard implementations used by Qwen, Moonshot and AI2.
- Pure linear attention / Hebbian fast weights: S_t = S_{t-1} + k_t v_t^T (Katharopoulos et al., 'Transformers are RNNs', ICML 2020, arXiv:2006.16236; lineage from Schmidhuber, 'Learning to control fast-weight memories', Neural Computation 4(1):131-139, 1992). No forgetting, unbounded interference.
- Delta rule (DeltaNet): S_t = (I - beta_t k_t k_t^T) S_{t-1} + beta_t k_t v_t^T, which is EXACTLY one step of online SGD with learning rate beta_t on the local loss L_t(S) = 0.5*||S^T k_t - v_t||^2. Verified line-by-line in fla/ops/delta_rule/naive.py: `_v = v - (S*k).sum(-2); _v = _v*beta; S = S + k(x)_v`. Origin: Schlag, Irie & Schmidhuber, 'Linear Transformers Are Secretly Fast Weight Programmers', ICML 2021, arXiv:2102.11174; parallelised over sequence length by Yang, Wang, Zhang, Kim et al., NeurIPS 2024, arXiv:2406.06484.
- Gated delta rule (Gated DeltaNet, GDN): S_t = (I - beta_t k_t k_t^T)*exp(g_t)*S_{t-1} + beta_t k_t v_t^T, i.e. decay first, then error-correct against the DECAYED state. Verified in fla/ops/gated_delta_rule/naive.py. g_t and beta_t are per-head scalars produced by an a_proj and a b_proj of the token. Yang, Kautz & Hatamizadeh, 'Gated Delta Networks: Improving Mamba2 with Delta Rule', ICLR 2025, arXiv:2412.06464.
- KDA (Kimi Delta Attention) is GDN with a PER-CHANNEL log-decay g_t in R^K instead of a scalar: S = S*exp(g)[..,None]; S += beta*k (x) (v - (k*S).sum(-2)). Verified in fla/ops/kda/naive.py. Moonshot AI, 'Kimi Linear: An Expressive, Efficient Attention Architecture', arXiv:2510.26692 (Oct 2025).
- GDN-2 (2026) decouples erase from write: S_t = (I - k_t (b_t . k_t)^T) Diag(exp(g_t)) S_{t-1} + k_t (w_t . v_t)^T, where b_t in R^K is a channel-wise ERASE gate on the key axis, w_t in R^V a channel-wise WRITE gate on the value axis, g_t in R^K a channel-wise log-decay, and '.' is the Hadamard product. Collapsing b_t = w_t = beta (scalar) recovers KDA. Verbatim from the docstring of fla/ops/gdn2/naive.py. 'Gated DeltaNet-2: Decoupling Erase and Write in Linear Attention', arXiv:2605.22791.
- RWKV-7 'Goose' generalises the delta rule to diagonal-plus-low-rank (DPLR): S_t = S_{t-1}(Diag(exp(g_t)) + alpha_t beta_t^T) + v_t k_t^T with INDEPENDENT alpha_t and beta_t; DeltaNet is the special case alpha_t = -beta_t k_t. Verified in fla/ops/generalized_delta_rule/dplr/naive.py (comment and code). Peng et al., arXiv:2503.14456.
- DeltaProduct applies n_h Householder transformations per token instead of one, i.e. n_h micro-gradient steps per token, recovering state-tracking expressivity. fla's GatedDeltaProduct defaults: num_householder=2, allow_neg_eigval=True, head_dim=256, num_heads=6, expand_v=2. Siems et al., 'DeltaProduct: Improving State-Tracking in Linear RNNs via Householder Products', arXiv:2502.10297.
- TTT layers make the hidden state a model f_W and the update a self-supervised gradient step: W_t = W_{t-1} - eta*grad_W l(W_{t-1}; x_t). TTT-Linear and TTT-MLP (1- and 2-layer MLP memories). Sun et al., 'Learning to (Learn at Test Time): RNNs with Expressive Hidden States', ICML 2025, arXiv:2407.04620 (code: test-time-training/ttt-lm-pytorch, ttt-lm-jax).
- Titans adds momentum and weight decay to the TTT gradient: S_t = eta_t S_{t-1} - theta_t grad l(M_{t-1}; x_t) ('past surprise' + 'momentary surprise'); M_t = (1 - alpha_t) M_{t-1} + S_t, where alpha_t is a data-dependent forget gate. Variants MAC / MAG / MAL. Behrouz, Zhong & Mirrokni, 'Titans: Learning to Memorize at Test Time', arXiv:2501.00663, NeurIPS 2025. No official code has been released.
- LaCT ('Test-Time Training Done Right', Zhang, Bi, Hong, K. Zhang, Luan, Yang, Sunkavalli, Freeman, Tan; arXiv:2505.23884, ICLR 2026) does the fast-weight update in LARGE chunks (2K-1M tokens, treated as an unordered set within a chunk) on SwiGLU fast weights f(x) = W1(silu(W0 x) . W2 x), with: per-token PER-CHANNEL fp32 learning rates lr0/lr1/lr2, a data-dependent momentum averaged over the chunk, optional Muon (Newton-Schulz-5) orthogonalisation of each fast-weight gradient, and a channel-wise L2 renormalisation of the fast weights back to their initial norms after every update. All verified in lact_llm/lact_model/ttt_operation.py.
- MesaNet replaces the gradient step with the EXACT local optimum: it keeps two states, H^kk_t = exp(g_t) H^kk_{t-1} + beta_t k_t k_t^T and H^kv_t = exp(g_t) H^kv_{t-1} + beta_t k_t v_t^T, and reads o_t = ((H^kk_t + Diag(lambda))^{-1} q_t) applied to H^kv_t, solved by conjugate gradient. This is a regularised least-squares / Kalman-gain read: the inverse second-moment matrix of the keys is literally an inferred precision matrix. Verified in fla/ops/mesa_net/naive.py (naive_mesa_net_exact uses torch.linalg.solve; naive_mesa_net_CG uses 30 CG steps). 'MesaNet: Sequence Modeling by Locally Optimal Test-Time Training', arXiv:2506.05233 (2025); precursors von Oswald et al., 'Transformers learn in-context by gradient descent', ICML 2023, arXiv:2212.07677 and 'Uncovering mesa-optimization algorithms in Transformers', arXiv:2309.05858.
- Preconditioned DeltaNet (PGDN / PKDA, 2026, arXiv:2604.21100) adds a per-channel second-moment 'ATK' preconditioner state: A_t = exp(g^atk_t) A_{t-1} + beta^atk_t * k_t^2; then r = log(A_t + eps) - center, s = r/(1 + |r|), M = exp(-log(x)*s), and the write uses the preconditioned key k_t . M. Defaults in fla: x=1.5, eps=1e-6, center = log_atk_scale = -0.2, with q and k L2-normalised first. This is Adagrad/RMSProp-style curvature-aware learning-rate modulation INSIDE the recurrence.
- The gated delta rule is the sequence-mixing primitive that actually shipped at frontier scale. Qwen3-Next-80B-A3B (Sept 2025) uses GDN; the HuggingFace transformers config (models/qwen3_next/configuration_qwen3_next.py) shows num_hidden_layers=48 with full_attention_interval=4, i.e. layer_types = 3 x 'linear_attention' then 1 x 'full_attention' repeated (25 % full attention). NVlabs/GatedDeltaNet README documents the subsequent deployments: Qwen3.5 (Feb 2026), Qwen3.6, Qwen3.8, and AI2 OlmoHybrid (Mar 2026). Qwen's own README describes Qwen3.5 as 'Gated Delta Networks combined with sparse Mixture-of-Experts'.
- Kimi Linear uses the same 3:1 ratio with KDA: HuggingFace transformers models/kimi_linear/configuration_kimi_linear.py has num_hidden_layers=27 and layer_types = 'full_attention' if (i and i % 4 == 0) else 'linear_attention'; the full-attention layers are MLA (kv_lora_rank=512, qk_nope_head_dim=128, qk_rope_head_dim=64, v_head_dim=128); the KDA layers have linear_num_heads=32, linear_head_dim=128, linear_conv_kernel_dim=4; vocab 163,840; max_position_embeddings 1,048,576.
- At ~340-475M parameters and matched recurrent state size, zero-shot commonsense accuracy does NOT discriminate these architectures: GDN 40.6, GSA 40.5, GLA 40.2, Raven 40.2, Mamba-2 40.1, FoX 40.1, SWA 40.0, Transformer+RoPE 39.9 (average over PIQA, HellaSwag, WinoGrande, ARC-e, ARC-c). Retrieval does discriminate them, by tens of points. Source: Raven Table 3, goombalab/raven README (Afzal, Bick, Xing, Cevher, Gu, 2026).
- Constant-size recurrent state provably cannot copy arbitrarily long strings, while transformers can; Jelassi, Brandfonbrener, Kakade, Malach, 'Repeat After Me: Transformers are Better than State Space Models at Copying', ICML 2024, arXiv:2402.01032.
- S4/Mamba-style SSMs (and transformers) are in TC^0 and therefore cannot solve inherently sequential state-tracking problems such as composing permutations in S5; Merrill, Petty & Sabharwal, 'The Illusion of State in State-Space Models', ICML 2024, arXiv:2404.08819. The cheap partial fix is negative eigenvalues: Grazzi et al., 'Unlocking State-Tracking in Linear RNNs Through Negative Eigenvalues', ICLR 2025, arXiv:2411.12537 - exposed in fla as GatedDeltaNet(allow_neg_eigval=True), which simply multiplies beta by 2 so the eigenvalue range becomes [-1, 1].
- Recall in efficient LMs is a function of recurrent state size, and the family sits on a recall-vs-throughput Pareto frontier; the prescribed fix is short sliding-window attention (window ~64) for local exactness plus linear attention for the global tail. Arora et al., 'Zoology: Measuring and Improving Recall in Efficient Language Models', ICLR 2024, arXiv:2312.04927; Arora et al., 'Simple linear attention language models balance the recall-throughput tradeoff' (Based), ICML 2024, arXiv:2402.18668. MQAR is the standard synthetic.
- The mushroom body's PN->KC expansion plus APL-driven k-WTA is a random sparse projection that decorrelates inputs (locality-sensitive hashing): Dasgupta, Stevens & Navlakha, Science 358:793 (2017). The optimal number of PN inputs per KC is intermediate: Litwin-Kumar, Harris, Axel, Sompolinsky & Abbott, Neuron 93:1153 (2017) show the participation-ratio dimension of the KC code is maximised at K ~ 7 for the fly (measured ~6-7 claws) and K ~ 4 for cerebellar granule cells, with the shared-input correlation cost scaling ~K^2/M^2.
- Fly dopaminergic neurons implement genuinely compartment-specific write rules, not one global learning rate: Aso & Rubin, eLife 5:e16135 (2016) show 20 DAN cell types with cell-type-specific rules for training requirement, decay dynamics, storage capacity and flexibility, and that a single DAN type can write aversive memory, REDUCE aversive memory, or write appetitive memory depending only on the timing of its activation relative to the odour.
- Recall-gated consolidation: slow (LTM) synapses update only when the fast (STM) read exceeds a threshold. Lindsey & Litwin-Kumar, eLife 12:RP90793 (2024) map this onto the MB (gamma1 MBON depression disinhibits the DANs of LTM compartments) and show that with N = 2000 synapses (1000 STM / 1000 LTM), learning rates 0.25 (STM) / 0.05 (LTM) and reliable-memory probability 0.25, the learnable timescale grows LINEARLY with repetitions versus LOGARITHMICALLY for simple synapses, and predicts inverted-U spacing effects.
- Linear-attention kernels only win at long sequences. On a single NVIDIA GB200 (CUDA 12.9, PyTorch 2.9.0), fla's own benchmark: at B=8, T=1024, H=8, D=64 forward, flash_attn 0.157 ms vs chunk_gdn 0.631 ms (attention 4x FASTER); at B=2, T=16384, H=16, D=128 forward, flash_attn 5.035 ms vs chunk_gdn 1.029 ms (GDN 4.9x faster); fwd+bwd at the same shape, flash_attn 19.960 ms vs chunk_gdn 3.616 ms (5.5x).

### Principles and their translation

| principle | biology / mathematics | translation to a sequence model | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Every competitive fast-weight layer is online regression on an explicit local loss, not Hebbian association. The delta rule IS SGD on 0.5*//S^T k - v//^2; the gated delta rule adds weight decay; Titans adds momentum; MesaNet solves the regression exactly; PGDN preconditions it by curvature. A design that cannot be written as 'optimiser X on local loss L' is outside the field's frontier and will be hard to defend. | Widrow-Hoff / LMS (1960) and recursive least squares. The biological counterpart is error-correcting (not purely associative) plasticity: the KC->MBON synapse is depressed in proportion to the coincidence of KC activity with a DAN signal, and the MBON read-out is what the error is computed against. | Fix the local loss first (L2 associative recall is the default; ATLAS/MIRAS show l_p, Huber and KL-retention variants), then choose the optimiser (plain SGD = DeltaNet, +decay = GDN, +momentum = Titans, +second moment = PGDN, exact solve = MesaNet). Report which cell of that 2-D table you occupy. Pre-register the comparison against the neighbouring cells. | established |
| Forgetting must be decoupled from writing, and both should be per-channel. The 2024->2026 progression is exactly this: GDN (scalar decay g, scalar write beta) -> KDA (per-channel g) -> GDN-2 (per-channel erase b on the key axis, per-channel write w on the value axis, per-channel decay g). Each step was worth a paper. | Aso & Rubin 2016: the same DAN type writes or erases depending on timing; MB compartments have distinct decay constants; depression and potentiation are separate events at the same synapse. Mathematically, erase and write are the two halves of (I - beta k k^T)S + beta k v^T and there is no reason they should share a coefficient. | Implement the GDN-2 form S_t = (I - k_t (b_t . k_t)^T) Diag(exp(g_t)) S_{t-1} + k_t (w_t . v_t)^T and then ask the specifically biological question GDN-2 did not: should the erase gate be SIGNED (allowing potentiation as well as depression at a key), and should b, w, g be produced by separate 'compartment' heads with different learned time constants rather than by one projection of the token? | established |
| The right analogue of a dopamine-like three-factor learning rate is a NORMALISED error signal, not a raw one. Raw gradient-norm surprise (Titans) is the weakest form; the 2026 state of the art normalises by an accumulated second moment (PGDN's ATK preconditioner) or inverts the key covariance exactly (MesaNet). This is precisely the 'precision' idea, but computed rather than hand-specified. | Inverse-variance weighting / natural gradient / Kalman gain. In predictive-coding terms, MesaNet's (H^kk + lambda I)^{-1} IS the inferred precision matrix of the key distribution; PGDN's A_t is a diagonal approximation to it. Biologically this is divisive normalisation (APL feedback in the calyx, Prisco et al. eLife 2024) and dopamine autoreceptor gain control, which normalise by recent activity rather than transmitting it raw. | Make the write gate beta_t = f(precision-normalised error) where the normaliser is an accumulated per-channel second moment with its own decay, i.e. beta_t k_t -> beta_t (k_t . M_t) with M_t from an Adagrad-like state. This is a two-line change to any delta-rule kernel and is the single most defensible place for a 'neuromodulation' claim. FlyBrainLLM's Phase-2 finding that a LEARNED gate beat the raw-error gate is consistent with the field and predicts the normalised version should close the gap. | promising |
| The binding constraint on memory capacity is key CORRELATION, not state size alone. A d_k x d_v state stores at most d_k mutually orthogonal key-value pairs exactly; writing key k perturbs every stored item j in proportion to <k_i, k_j>. Every 2024-2026 model uses dense, L2-normalised keys, so the interference term is always non-zero. | Litwin-Kumar et al. 2017 and Dasgupta et al. 2017: the mushroom body's random sparse expansion with k-WTA maximises the dimension (participation ratio) of the code at an intermediate in-degree K ~ 7, explicitly to decorrelate. Sparse high-dimensional codes are near-orthogonal by construction (Johnson-Lindenstrauss); this is the only known biological answer to associative interference. | Replace the dense key k_t with a k-WTA sparse code over a fixed random or learned expansion (fan-in ~7, ~5 % active), hold the state size d_k x d_v FIXED, and measure MQAR / S-NIAH / SWDE-FDA-SQuAD. Predicted effect: interference falls and recall at 16K-32K rises at zero extra state. Secondary, testable efficiency claim: sparse keys make the chunked form's (I - tril(diag(beta) K K^T))^{-1} better conditioned and cheaper. Straight-through or top-k with a soft surrogate handles the non-differentiability (SoftHash: soft WTA in training, hard WTA at inference). | promising |
| Consolidation should be gated by the fast memory's RECALL, not by the input's surprise. Titans/ATLAS write to memory when surprise (gradient norm) is high. Recall-gated consolidation writes to the SLOW memory when the FAST memory already reads out correctly - the opposite signal - and provably changes the scaling of the learnable timescale from logarithmic to linear in the number of repetitions. | Lindsey & Litwin-Kumar, eLife 2024 (RP90793), mapped onto the MB: gamma1 MBON depression (evidence that STM has learned) disinhibits the DANs of the long-term compartments. Mathematically it is a reliability filter: only statistically repeatable associations are allowed to consume slow-memory capacity, which is exactly the right policy when slow capacity is scarce and the input stream is noisy. | Two coupled matrix states S_fast and S_slow with decays tau_fast << tau_slow. Write to S_fast with the gated delta rule. Write to S_slow with gate proportional to a threshold on the agreement between the fast read S_fast^T k_t and the target v_t (e.g. sigmoid(a * cos(S_fast^T k_t, v_t) + b)). Pre-register against (i) Titans-style surprise gating on the same two-state architecture and (ii) MoM / HOPE style fixed multi-timescale memories. Predicted signatures: linear growth of retained timescale with repetition count, and an inverted-U spacing effect - both are falsifiable and neither is a perplexity claim. | promising |
| Update granularity is a hardware constraint, not a modelling choice. Token-by-token fast-weight updates cannot reach useful GPU utilisation; LaCT's answer is to treat a chunk of 2K-1M tokens as an unordered set and do ONE large matmul-shaped update, reaching ~70 % A100 utilisation. Every kernel in fla is a chunked form of an algebraically sequential rule. | The chunked/parallel form of the delta rule uses the UT-transform identity: within a chunk, (I - tril(diag(beta) K K^T))^{-1} converts the sequential Householder product into one matrix inverse plus two matmuls (visible in fla/ops/delta_rule/naive.py delta_rule_chunkwise). This is exact, not an approximation. | Any biologically motivated per-token plasticity rule must be given a chunked matrix form before it is scaled, or it will never be trainable past toy size. Design the rule so that its within-chunk interaction matrix is triangular and invertible. If the rule cannot be chunked, choose LaCT's framing instead (large chunks, set-valued within chunk, sliding-window attention for intra-chunk order) and pay a documented training-FLOP multiple. | established |
| Hybridisation is not a compromise, it is the answer. Every frontier deployment is ~25 % full-attention layers interleaved with ~75 % linear/fast-weight layers. The attention layers supply exact, unbounded-capacity local recall; the fast-weight layers supply cheap constant-state long-range context. | Arora et al.'s recall-throughput Pareto frontier: a constant-size state cannot be a substitute for an unbounded cache, only a complement. Biologically, the fly also does not use one memory: the MB (associative, compartmentalised, fast) and the central complex (continuous-attractor, slow, metric) are different memories with different algebra. | Build the next phase as a hybrid from day one: specify the layer schedule (e.g. 3:1 as in Qwen3-Next and Kimi Linear) as a pre-registered hyperparameter, and run the matched control with the fly-motivated layer replaced by GDN at identical state size, identical schedule, identical tokens. Never claim a pure-recurrent result; claim a better 75 % layer. | established |
| Perplexity and commonsense benchmarks at sub-1B scale cannot support an architecture claim. The spread across seven architectures at ~400M is under 1 point of average zero-shot accuracy, while the spread on 32K needle retrieval is 0 to 91 points on the same models. | Statistical power: the between-architecture effect on aggregate NLL is smaller than seed noise at this scale, which is the same failure mode that produced FlyBrainLLM's Phase-2 null (0.026 bits/char = 0.7 seed-SD). | Pre-register the primary endpoint on recall and state-tracking, not perplexity: MQAR (Zoology), RULER S-NIAH-1/2/3 at 1K-32K, SWDE / FDA / SQuAD recall, BABILong, plus parity / modular arithmetic / S5 composition for expressivity, plus length extrapolation beyond the training context. Match on recurrent STATE SIZE and training tokens, and report parameters separately (the Raven table is state-size-matched with params ranging 340M-475M - read such tables carefully). | established |
| Homeostatic renormalisation of fast weights is already standard practice and is the one 'biological' mechanism that survived contact with scale. LaCT rescales each fast-weight channel back to its initial norm after every chunk update; Muon orthogonalises the fast-weight gradient; Lipschitz-constrained training and MuonClip do the analogous thing for slow weights. | Synaptic scaling / homeostatic plasticity: multiplicative renormalisation of a neuron's synaptic weight vector preserves relative structure while bounding total drive. Mathematically it bounds the spectral norm of the fast-weight operator, which is what keeps the recurrence stable without a learned gate. | Apply channel-wise L2 renormalisation to the fast-weight state after each chunk (w <- w///w//*//w//_init) and consider Newton-Schulz-5 orthogonalisation of the update. This is cheap, already validated at 760M in LaCT, and is a legitimate place to say 'homeostasis' without overclaiming - but it is NOT novel, so it belongs in the method, not the contribution. | established |
| Expressivity beyond TC^0 requires either negative/complex eigenvalues or multiple micro-steps per token. This is a proven, cheap, and mostly-free fix that the fly-aligned design should simply adopt. | Merrill et al. 2024 (TC^0 limit); Grazzi et al. ICLR 2025 (eigenvalue range [0,1] -> [-1,1] enables parity); DeltaProduct (n_h Householder products per token). Biologically, the analogue of negative eigenvalues is net inhibitory recurrence - which the connectome supplies at 30.8 % inhibitory edges, though that is anatomy, not a demonstrated computational benefit. | Set allow_neg_eigval=True (beta in [0,2]) in any delta-rule layer; it costs nothing. If state tracking is a target, use num_householder=2-4. Do NOT claim the inhibitory fraction of the connectome causes this - it is a one-line hyperparameter in the ML model and must be tested as such. | established |

### Numbers an engineer can use

- Qwen3-Next hybrid schedule (HF transformers config defaults): num_hidden_layers=48, full_attention_interval=4 -> 36 GDN layers + 12 full-attention layers (75 %/25 %); linear_num_key_heads=16, linear_num_value_heads=32 (Grouped Value Attention 2:1), linear_key_head_dim=linear_value_head_dim=128; num_experts=512, moe_intermediate_size=512, shared_expert_intermediate_size=512, decoder_sparse_step=1; hidden_size=2048, intermediate_size=5632.
- Kimi Linear (HF transformers config defaults): num_hidden_layers=27, layer_types = full_attention iff (i and i%4==0) -> 3:1 KDA:MLA; linear_num_heads=32, linear_head_dim=128, linear_conv_kernel_dim=4; MLA with kv_lora_rank=512, qk_nope_head_dim=128, qk_rope_head_dim=64, v_head_dim=128; num_experts_per_tok=8 + 1 shared; vocab_size=163,840; max_position_embeddings=1,048,576.
- GDN layer parameter budget (fla docstring): ~6 * hidden_size^2 per layer, identical to Mamba2. With use_gate=True: 0.75*d^2 each for q_proj and k_proj, 1.5*d^2 each for v_proj, g_proj and o_proj. Constraint: num_heads * head_dim = 0.75 * hidden_size. fla defaults: head_dim=256, num_heads=6, expand_v=2.0, conv_size=4, use_short_conv=True, allow_neg_eigval=False.
- PGDN / PKDA preconditioner defaults (fla/ops/precond_gated_delta_rule/naive.py): x=1.5, eps=1e-6, log_atk_scale (center) = -0.2; q and k are L2-normalised before use; squash s = r/(1+|r|) with r = log(A+eps) - center; multiplier M = exp(-log(1.5) * s), i.e. M in (1/1.5, 1.5) - a bounded +/-50 % per-channel learning-rate modulation.
- MesaNet defaults (fla/layers/mesa_net.py): hidden_size=2048, num_heads=16, head_dim=128, lambda_lower_bound=0.25, lambda initialised to 1.0 (via softplus reparameterisation), max_cg_step_training=30, max_cg_step_decoding=30, conv_size=4, use_output_gate=False. 30 conjugate-gradient iterations PER TOKEN at decode is the cost of exactness.
- LaCT fast-weight FLOPs: 18 * D * H * L * B for the SwiGLU fast weight (D = d_k = d_v, H = SwiGLU hidden), split 4DHLB forward-with-key, 8DHLB backward, 6DHLB forward-with-query. Muon Newton-Schulz-5 costs 30 * b * d^3 when d = d'. Default TTT chunk_size=2048 (paper range 2K-1M). ~70 % GPU utilisation on A100 vs a typical <5 % for token-by-token TTT.
- LaCT Newton-Schulz-5 coefficient triples (a,b,c) used for orthogonalisation, verbatim: (4.0848, -6.8946, 2.9270), (3.9505, -6.3029, 2.6377), (3.7418, -5.5913, 2.3037), (2.8769, -3.1427, 1.2046), (2.8366, -3.0525, 1.2012); G is pre-scaled by 1/(||G||_F + 1e-7).
- Single-needle retrieval vs context length at ~400M params, matched 12.5M recurrent state (Raven Table 2, SlimPajama). N1 (needle-1) at 1K/2K/4K/8K/16K/32K: GDN (475M) 99.2/100/99.8/92.0/41.8/22.1; Mamba-2 (382M) 99.2/95.6/52.2/12.8/5.4/2.8; GLA (475M) 74.6/25.1/8.2/2.2/0/0; GSA (399M) 99.2/97.1/90.0/67.4/29.6/11.0; SWA (374M) 29.8/11.0/6.2/3.4/1.2/0; Transformer+RoPE (340M) 100/100/0/0/0/0; FoX / gated Transformer (376M) 100/100/32.2/8.0/4.2/0; Raven (424M) 99.8/100/99.8/99.8/99.4/91.4.
- Multi-needle N2 at 1K/2K/4K/8K/16K/32K, same models: GDN 99.2/92.0/43.6/17.8/6.2/4.0; Mamba-2 99.8/98.0/68.2/15.4/4.4/3.8; Raven 98.8/98.0/98.8/81.6/23.0/8.8. N3: GDN 92.6/80.6/37.8/5.2/6.8/2.5. Multi-needle collapses roughly 4-8x earlier in context than single-needle for all constant-state models.
- Recall-intensive zero-shot at ~400M (SWDE / FDA / SQuAD): FoX gated Transformer 52.5 / 64.3 / 30.1; Transformer+RoPE 42.3 / 34.5 / 22.1; Raven 34.1 / 22.7 / 35.4; GDN 29.5 / 8.3 / 31.3; Mamba-2 25.7 / 14.9 / 31.9; GSA 23.8 / 14.5 / 24.9; SWA 10.0 / 14.4 / 29.7. On FDA, attention-with-forget-gate is ~7.7x GDN.
- Zero-shot commonsense average at ~400M (LAMBADA acc, PIQA, HellaSwag, WinoGrande, ARC-e, ARC-c): GDN 40.6, GSA 40.5, GLA 40.2, Raven 40.2, Mamba-2 40.1, FoX 40.1, SWA 40.0, Transformer+RoPE 39.9. Total spread 0.7 points. LAMBADA perplexity: GDN 40.1, SWA 40.7, Raven 41.0, Transformer 42.0, GLA 42.1, Mamba-2 43.0, GSA 44.1, FoX 48.1 - note FoX has the WORST perplexity and the BEST recall, which is the whole methodological point.
- Hybrid retrieval at ~400M (some full-attention layers added, Raven Table 4), N1 at 1K-32K: GDN-hybrid 100/100/100/100/93.2/70.5 (vs 99.2/100/99.8/92.0/41.8/22.1 pure); SWA-RoPE-hybrid 100/100/100/100/98.2/60.4; Raven-hybrid 100/100/100/100/98.4/78.6. Hybridisation buys roughly +50 points at 32K on the pure GDN.
- fla kernel timings on one NVIDIA GB200 (CUDA 12.9, PyTorch 2.9.0), forward ms: B=8,T=1024,H=8,D=64 -> flash_attn 0.157, chunk_gla 0.358, chunk_retention 0.425, chunk_gdn 0.631; B=4,T=2048,H=16,D=128 -> flash_attn 0.346, chunk_gla 0.514, chunk_gdn 0.753; B=1,T=8192,H=96,D=128 -> flash_attn 3.753, chunk_gdn 1.265; B=2,T=16384,H=16,D=128 -> flash_attn 5.035, chunk_gdn 1.029. Forward+backward: B=2,T=16384,H=16,D=128 -> flash_attn 19.960, chunk_gdn 3.616, chunk_retention 2.122; B=8,T=2048,H=32,D=256 -> flash_attn 6.748, chunk_gdn 8.644 (attention still wins). Cross-over is roughly T ~ 4K at these head counts.
- FlashQLA (QwenLM, 2026) reports 2-3x forward and 2x backward speedup over the fla Triton GDN kernel on Hopper and Blackwell, benchmarked on the Qwen3.5/Qwen3.6 head configurations h_{k,v} in {64,48,32,24,16,8} (TP1-TP8); it is now a backend for fla's GDN.
- TTT layers (Sun et al. 2024, 125M-1.3B): TTT-Linear and TTT-MLP keep reducing perplexity as context grows, whereas Mamba stops improving after 16k; TTT-Linear is faster than a Transformer at 8k context and matches Mamba wall-clock; TTT-MLP is memory-I/O bound.
- TTT-E2E (arXiv:2512.23675, 2025): 3B model on 164B tokens; loss scales with context length like full attention (Mamba-2 and Gated DeltaNet do not); lower loss than full attention throughout; inference latency constant in context (2.7x faster than full attention at 128K); training 3.4x slower than full attention at 8K because of gradient-of-gradients. Released checkpoints 125M, 1B (1x Chinchilla), 3B (3x Chinchilla) at 8K, one 3B variant extended to 128K.
- Titans (arXiv:2501.00663): MAC/MAG/MAL variants at 170M / 340M / 400M / 760M trained on 15B-30B FineWeb-Edu tokens; claims wins over Transformer++, RetNet, GLA, Mamba/Mamba2, DeltaNet, TTT and Gated DeltaNet; fine-tuned MAC reported to beat GPT-4 and RAG-Llama-3 on BABILong and to scale past 2M tokens. No official code as of this survey.
- ATLAS (arXiv:2505.23735): 340M / 400M / 790M / 1.3B trained on 15B / 15B / 30B / 100B FineWeb tokens; replaces token-wise surprise with the Omega rule (memorise a context window) optimised with Muon plus polynomial feature maps; keeps >80 % BABILong accuracy at 10M context where Titans degrades. Companion taxonomy MIRAS (arXiv:2504.13173) = (memory architecture, attentional bias, retention gate, learning algorithm), instances Moneta / Yaad / Memora.
- LaCT: up to +20 percentage points retrieval accuracy at long positions versus GLA and DeltaNet; chunk sizes 2K-1M tokens; ~70 % A100 utilisation. Released LM config '760M_lact_swiglu_nh4_fwlow_rank_momentum_fused_kernel'.
- Titans reproduction evidence (negative): 'Titans Revisited' (arXiv:2510.09551, 2025) reports Titans does not always beat established baselines, attributes this largely to chunking, and finds memory updates alone insufficient unless the backbone's key/value projections are jointly adapted. A small probe (arXiv:2603.17781, 2026) at embedding dim 128 with N=10/25/50 facts reports 100 % training-loss convergence but only 0-40 % free-form and 45-85 % forced-choice retrieval - a memorisation-retrieval gap.
- Mushroom-body design constants for a sparse key code: ~50 glomeruli -> ~2,000 KCs in the fly with K ~ 6-7 PN inputs per KC (Litwin-Kumar et al. 2017, measured claw counts); ~5 % k-WTA activity enforced by APL; cerebellar granule cells K ~ 4; optimal K grows roughly as sqrt(N_in); shared-input correlation cost ~ K^2/M^2. APL contacts 126/136 PNs (92.6 %) and 1,871/1,919 KCs (97.5 %) (Prisco et al., eLife 2024, e74172).
- Recall-gated consolidation parameters (Lindsey & Litwin-Kumar, eLife 2024): N = 2000 synapses split 1000 STM / 1000 LTM, learning rates 0.25 (STM) and 0.05 (LTM), reliable-memory probability 0.25; learnable timescale grows linearly with repetitions versus logarithmically for simple synapses.
- Raven training recipe (for a comparable small-scale run): SlimPajama-627B streaming, AdamW lr 3e-4, warmup 1024 steps, cosine decay, batch_size 16, seq_len 2048, gradient_accumulation_steps 4, 30,720 steps, 4 GPUs, checkpoint interval 3072; 12 ablation configs at 340M varying router design (linear vs MLP, sigmoid vs softmax, Gumbel noise on/off).
- Chunk sizes used by the reference kernels: DeltaNet chunkwise 32 (fla naive default), Gated DeltaNet chunk 64, KDA chunk 64, LaCT 2048. Grazzi's negative-eigenvalue fix in fla is literally `beta = beta * 2` when allow_neg_eigval=True.

### Dead ends

- A pure recurrent / fast-weight language model with no attention layers is not state of the art at any scale and nobody ships one. Qwen3-Next, Qwen3.5/3.6/3.8, Kimi Linear and AI2 OlmoHybrid all use a 3:1 linear:full-attention schedule. Treat 'we will replace attention entirely' as a disproven hypothesis and design a better 75 % layer instead.
- Claiming an architecture win from perplexity or zero-shot commonsense below ~1B parameters. At ~400M the total spread across GDN, GSA, GLA, Raven, Mamba-2, FoX, SWA and a RoPE Transformer is 0.7 accuracy points, and the model with the WORST LAMBADA perplexity (FoX, 48.1) has the BEST recall (FDA 64.3 vs GDN 8.3). This is the same statistical failure that produced FlyBrainLLM's Phase-2 null (0.026 bits/char = 0.7 seed-SD).
- Raw gradient-norm 'surprise' as a learning-rate/write gate. Titans' own reproduction (arXiv:2510.09551) finds memory updates gated this way are insufficient without co-adapting the read/write projections, and FlyBrainLLM Phase 1 already found a learned gate beating the error gate on the same substrate. The field's 2026 answer is to NORMALISE the signal (PGDN's second-moment preconditioner, MesaNet's exact inverse), not to feed the raw error to a sigmoid.
- Differentiable neuromodulated Hebbian plasticity as an end in itself. Backpropamine (Miconi et al., ICLR 2019, arXiv:2002.10585) gained ~1.7 perplexity on Penn Treebank with multi-million-parameter LSTMs and has had no scaling follow-up in seven years; the lineage was superseded by the delta rule and by test-time-training memories. Do not re-derive it.
- Token-by-token fast-weight updates on GPUs. LaCT exists precisely because per-token TTT wastes the hardware (~70 % utilisation with 2K-token chunks versus a typical few percent token-by-token). Any per-token biological plasticity rule that has no chunked matrix form is untrainable at scale, and the FlyBrainLLM Phase-2 setup (4 CPU cores, 135k parameters) cannot detect an effect this literature measures at 400M-3B.
- Linear/gated recurrent layers at SHORT context. fla's own GB200 benchmark shows FlashAttention beating chunk_gdn by ~4x at T=1024 and still winning at B=8,T=2048,H=32,D=256 fwd+bwd (6.748 vs 8.644 ms). The efficiency argument for a fast-weight layer only exists above roughly T ~ 4K.
- Linear RNNs with eigenvalues restricted to [0,1] (default Mamba / S4D / DeltaNet). Provably cannot compute parity in finite precision and cannot track state (Merrill et al. ICML 2024; Grazzi et al. ICLR 2025). The fix is free (beta in [0,2]) so there is no excuse to ship the broken version, and no credit for 'discovering' it.
- Dense L2-normalised keys as a way to get capacity. Normalising the key (fla does this explicitly in PGDN) bounds magnitudes but does nothing about the inner products <k_i, k_j> that ARE the interference. More state size buys recall only linearly; decorrelation is the unexploited axis.
- Reconstruction loss / training-loss convergence as evidence that a memory works. The Titans probe (arXiv:2603.17781) shows 100 % training-loss convergence with 0-40 % free-form retrieval at N=10-50 facts. Evaluate on-demand retrieval (MQAR, NIAH, SWDE/FDA), never memory reconstruction error.
- Reported but unverified at the time of writing: MiniMax appears to have reversed direction, from lightning/linear attention in MiniMax-01 (arXiv:2501.08313, 456B) to a full-attention MoE in MiniMax-M2 (230B total / 10B active, Oct 2025). I could only verify that the MiniMax-M2 README makes no linear-attention claim while MiniMax-01's does; the widely-reported rationale (that efficient attention's advantages did not survive complex multi-turn reasoning and agentic evaluation) is second-hand and should be checked against the M2 tech report before being cited.
- Routing a language model's input/output through anatomical ports (313 ALPN in, 97 MBON out) - already tested in this repository and worse than random ports in 6/6 seeds (median -0.63 nat/token), because of the readout capacity bottleneck. Anatomy constrains WIDTH at your peril; use it to constrain SPARSITY and CONNECTIVITY STATISTICS instead.

### Open questions

- Does a sparse, k-WTA expanded key code (mushroom-body style, fan-in ~7, ~5 % active) beat a dense L2-normalised key in a gated delta rule at IDENTICAL state size d_k x d_v, identical FLOPs and identical tokens? No paper in the 2024-2026 fast-weight literature has tested it. Primary endpoints: MQAR at increasing key-value load, RULER S-NIAH-1/2/3 at 1K-32K, SWDE/FDA/SQuAD. This is the single most defensible novel claim available and it is cheap to falsify.
- Does gating the SLOW memory's write on the FAST memory's recall (Lindsey & Litwin-Kumar 2024) beat gating on surprise (Titans) in a two-timescale fast-weight layer? Predicted, falsifiable signatures: retained timescale linear rather than logarithmic in repetition count, and an inverted-U spacing effect. Must be run against MoM (arXiv:2502.13685) and HOPE/Nested Learning (Behrouz et al., NeurIPS 2025, arXiv:2512.24695) as matched multi-timescale controls, not against a single-memory baseline.
- Is a variance-normalised (precision-weighted) surprise a better write gate than a learned sigmoid gate? PGDN (arXiv:2604.21100) shows a second-moment preconditioner helps, and MesaNet shows the exact inverse helps, but no one has isolated 'normalised surprise as the third factor' from 'learned gate' at matched parameters. FlyBrainLLM's Phase-1 and Phase-2 results are the negative half of this experiment already.
- Should the erase gate be SIGNED? GDN-2 decouples erase (b_t in [0,1] on the key axis) from write (w_t in [0,1] on the value axis), but the fly's DANs both depress and potentiate the same compartment depending on timing (Aso & Rubin 2016). Allowing b_t < 0 corresponds to eigenvalues outside [0,1] of the erase operator - the same family of fix as Grazzi et al.'s negative eigenvalues, but applied to the write rather than the decay. Untested as far as I can find.
- Are compartment-specific update rules (different tau, different loss, different gate per head, as in the MB's ~15-20 compartments) better than one rule with per-channel gates? MoM, Log-Linear Attention (arXiv:2506.04761, Fenwick-tree multi-scale states) and HOPE all implement heterogeneity of TIMESCALE; none implements heterogeneity of RULE. Uncertain whether rule heterogeneity is separable from timescale heterogeneity in practice.
- Where is the crossover at which MesaNet's exact solve (30 CG iterations per token) stops paying for itself versus one preconditioned gradient step (PGDN)? Both are 2025-2026, both are <=1.3B, neither is in production, and I found no head-to-head at matched inference FLOPs. This is a genuinely open frontier question that an outside group could answer.
- Does combining activation-space iteration (looped/energy-based inference) with weight-space iteration (TTT/Titans) under one objective help or is it redundant? No published system does both, and the iso-depth scaling law for looping (recurrence-equivalence exponent phi = 0.46, raised to 0.65 by hyperconnections; arXiv:2604.21106) suggests looping alone is an expensive way to buy capacity.
- Does the sparse-key idea interact favourably with the chunked kernel? Concretely: does a k-WTA key make (I - tril(diag(beta) K K^T))^{-1} better conditioned and cheaper to invert within a chunk, giving a wall-clock win on top of the capacity win? Plausible from the algebra (the off-diagonal entries are exactly the key inner products) but I have seen no measurement.
- What is the honest state of independent replication? Titans (no code), ATLAS (no independent replication found), TTT-E2E (single group), MesaNet and PGDN (single group, <=1.3B). Only GDN/KDA have been independently reproduced, by virtue of being shipped in production models. Any claim built on Titans/ATLAS numbers is building on unreplicated ground and should say so.
- Is there a defined regime in which a fly-aligned memory could legitimately claim state of the art? The most plausible candidate is: 'highest multi-needle retrieval (RULER N2/N3 at 16K-32K) per unit of recurrent state, at matched FLOPs and matched tokens, for models under 1B parameters' - a narrow, honest, pre-registrable claim on the axis where the whole field is weakest (GDN drops to 4.0 % on N2 at 32K). Whether the sparse-key and recall-gated-consolidation mechanisms actually move that number is unknown and is exactly what the next phase should find out.

### Relevance to language models

The honest state of the art for a sequence-mixing primitive in a 2026 language model is the gated delta rule - online SGD with weight decay on a local associative loss - deployed as roughly 75 % of the layers with full attention supplying the other 25 %, inside a sparse MoE. That is what Qwen3-Next-80B-A3B, Qwen3.5/3.6/3.8, Kimi Linear and AI2 OlmoHybrid actually are. The research frontier above that is entirely about how the write is modulated: momentum (Titans), window-level objectives with Muon (ATLAS), large-chunk MLP fast weights with per-channel learning rates (LaCT), curvature preconditioning (PGDN, 2026), exact local optima (MesaNet), and decoupled per-channel erase/write gates (GDN-2, 2026). Every one of these is a three-factor learning rule in disguise: a presynaptic key, a postsynaptic error, and a scalar-or-vector gate that sets the learning rate. This is exactly the structure the fly's KC->MBON synapse has, which means the biology and the mathematics genuinely meet here - but it also means that merely implementing a dopamine-gated Hebbian or delta rule reproduces what the field already does, and will be indistinguishable from Gated DeltaNet. For a fly-aligned design to matter for language modelling it has to supply something the field has NOT tried, and only two candidates in this survey qualify: (1) a sparse, near-orthogonal k-WTA key code from a mushroom-body-style random expansion, which attacks the interference term <k_i, k_j> that limits every one of these memories, at zero extra state; and (2) recall-gated consolidation, which gates the slow memory on the fast memory's read confidence rather than on input surprise, and which has a proven consequence (linear rather than logarithmic growth of the learnable timescale) that is directly measurable in a language model. Both are testable at 100-400M on recall benchmarks, both have matched controls that already exist in one pip-installable library (flash-linear-attention), and both would fail loudly if wrong.


## Continual learning and memory consolidation for language models: what is solved, what is not, and which fly consolidation principles actually transfer

*Key: `continual-consolidation`.*

### Summary

SEARCH CAVEAT: primary research sources could not be reached when this note was written. Everything below comes from the repo's own sweep (docs/aif/LITERATURE.md), RESULTS.md, and recall to May 2026. Venue/id confidence is flagged per item; treat every arXiv number as "verify before citing".

STRATEGIC FINDING. The fly is a poor source of *wiring* priors (your Phase-1 nulls) but it is the best causally-dissected *memory-consolidation control system* in biology — and consolidation is precisely where LLMs are weakest. The pivot is: stop using the connectome as a weight prior; use the fly's consolidation controller as an algorithm specification with published time constants.

Your nulls are predicted by outside theory, not bugs. A fixed sparse random expansion with k-WTA is a random-feature associative memory; the delta rule is the *optimal* online least-squares update, so DeltaNet/Gated DeltaNet must win at matched compute (your H1: mb 0.9814 vs gdn 0.9979 vs deltanet 1.0000). And a Shannon-surprise write gate (−log p, or ‖eps‖²) must lose to a learned gate because −log p confounds novelty with irreducible entropy. The biologically correct gate is *Bayesian* surprise, KL(posterior‖prior) over the memory (Itti & Baldi 2009) — a different quantity your v0.2 never computed.

WHAT IS SOTA. Continual pretraining: LR re-warm + re-decay + ~5% replay of original data matches full retraining at 405M and 10B (Ibrahim et al., TMLR 2024, arXiv 2403.08763). Knowledge injection: retrieval beats unsupervised finetuning (Ovadia et al., EMNLP 2024); kNN-LM gets WikiText-103 18.65→16.12 ppl with zero training (Khandelwal et al., ICLR 2020). Test-time memory: Titans/Atlas/HOPE (Behrouz et al., 2025) and Gated DeltaNet (Yang et al., ICLR 2025). Self-editing: SEAL raises no-context SQuAD 33.5%→47.0% but *explicitly reports catastrophic forgetting across sequential self-edits* (Zweiger et al., NeurIPS 2025). Parametric ceiling: ~2 bits/parameter, only after ~1000 exposures (Allen-Zhu & Li, arXiv 2404.05405).

WHAT IS UNSOLVED (your opportunity). (i) Single-pass, temporally correlated, never-repeated streams — every number above assumes i.i.d. shuffling and multiple epochs; the "forgetting tax" of a domain-sorted stream is unmeasured at any scale. (ii) Consolidation efficiency — nobody reports *bits retained per training FLOP at a fixed retention horizon*. (iii) Durability over thousands of sequential updates — ROME/MEMIT collapse, SEAL forgets, MemoryLLM/M+ claim only ~160k tokens.

WHERE THE FLY GIVES NUMBERS, NOT METAPHORS. 15 mushroom-body compartments writing the same event at cell-type-specific rates (Aso & Rubin, eLife 2016); a dedicated novelty channel that habituates over minutes (MBON-α'3, Hattori et al., Cell 2017); an active-forgetting clock with a molecular time constant of hours (Rac1; Shuai et al., Cell 2010) that sleep switches off (Berry et al., Cell 2015); a causal sleep-consolidation switch (dFB; Donlea et al., Science 2011); a metabolic cost that suppresses LTM when food is scarce (Plaçais & Preat 2013; Plaçais et al. 2017); extinction implemented as a parallel *opposing* trace rather than an overwrite (Felsenberg et al., Cell 2018); and a spaced protocol — 10 sessions × 15 min rest → 7-day memory, massed → none (Tully et al., Cell 1994) — that independently matches the human spacing law (optimal gap ≈ 10–20% of retention interval; Cepeda et al. 2006/2008). Every one of these is an algorithm with a constant attached.

### Established (with sources)

- COMPLEMENTARY LEARNING SYSTEMS (core): the brain needs two systems because a single slow interleaved learner cannot absorb a new fact in one shot without distorting old ones. Neocortex learns slowly with overlapping distributed codes; hippocampus learns fast with sparse pattern-separated codes; transfer is by interleaved replay. McClelland, McNaughton & O'Reilly, Psychological Review 102(3):419-457, 1995. [confident]
- CLS UPDATED: replay is NOT uniform — it is biased toward recent, rewarded, and surprising experience, and schema-consistent information can be assimilated by cortex rapidly (bypassing the slow path). Kumaran, Hassabis & McClelland, Trends in Cognitive Sciences 20(7):512-534, 2016; schema result: Tse et al., Science 316:76-82, 2007 (rats consolidate schema-consistent flavour-place pairs in ~48 h vs weeks). [confident on the claims; page numbers moderate]
- REPLAY IS CAUSAL, NOT EPIPHENOMENAL: selective electrical disruption of hippocampal sharp-wave ripples during post-training sleep impairs subsequent spatial learning. Girardeau, Benchenane, Wiener, Buzsaki & Zugaro, Nature Neuroscience 12:1222-1223, 2009. Review of SWR mechanics (140-200 Hz, ~50-100 ms, ~5-20x time-compressed replay): Buzsaki, Hippocampus 25:1073-1188, 2015. [confident on claims; exact compression factor moderate]
- SYNAPTIC HOMEOSTASIS: sleep produces net multiplicative DOWNSCALING of synaptic strength, not only strengthening. Serial-EM in mouse cortex found ~18% decrease in axon-spine interface after sleep. Tononi & Cirelli, Neuron 81:12-34, 2014; de Vivo et al., Science 355:507-510, 2017. Consolidation therefore = selective preservation against a global multiplicative decay, which is a *different* operation from gradient replay. [confident on direction; the 18% figure moderate]
- DROSOPHILA — SLEEP CAUSALLY CONSOLIDATES MEMORY: thermogenetically inducing sleep via dorsal fan-shaped body (dFB, 104y/R23E10) neurons converts otherwise-labile courtship memory into long-lasting memory. Donlea, Thimgan, Suzuki, Gottschalk & Shaw, Science 332:1571-1576, 2011. The dFB is a bistable electrical switch set by sleep pressure: Pimentel, Donlea, Talbot, Song, Thurston & Miesenbock, Nature 536:333-337, 2016; Donlea, Pimentel & Miesenbock, Neuron 81:860-872, 2014. [confident on claims; page numbers reasonably confident]
- DROSOPHILA — SLEEP WORKS BY BLOCKING ACTIVE FORGETTING: ongoing dopaminergic-neuron activity erases mushroom-body memory traces; sleep suppresses that DAN activity. So in the fly, 'consolidation' is substantially *gating off an erasure process*, not writing harder. Berry, Cervantes-Sandoval, Chakraborty & Davis, Cell 161:1656-1667, 2015; forgetting-by-dopamine (DAMB receptor): Berry, Cervantes-Sandoval, Nicholas & Davis, Neuron 74:530-542, 2012. [reasonably confident]
- DROSOPHILA — FORGETTING HAS ITS OWN MOLECULAR CLOCK: Rac1-dependent active forgetting operates on a timescale of hours and is separable from encoding; blocking Rac1 extends labile memory, activating it accelerates loss. Shuai, Lu, Hu, Zhang, Sun & Zhong, Cell 140:579-589, 2010. [reasonably confident]
- DROSOPHILA — COMPARTMENTALISED PARALLEL TIMESCALES: the mushroom body is divided into 15 compartments, each with its own dopaminergic input and output neuron (~20 DAN types, ~21-24 MBON types); different DANs write and decay memories with cell-type-specific rhythms spanning minutes to days, so one event is written at several timescales in parallel. Aso et al., eLife 3:e04577 and 3:e04580, 2014; Aso & Rubin, eLife 5:e16135, 2016. Connectome quantification: Li, Lindsey, Marin et al., eLife 9:e62576, 2020. [confident]
- DROSOPHILA — A DEDICATED NOVELTY CHANNEL EXISTS AND IS CONNECTOME-MAPPED: MBON-alpha'3 output reports stimulus novelty and habituates over minutes; familiarity is signalled by suppression of that output. Hattori, Aso, Swartz, Rubin, Abbott & Axel, Cell 169:956-969, 2017. This is an explicit, anatomically localised novelty-detector with a measured time constant — the biological object that a consolidation gate should be modelled on. [reasonably confident]
- DROSOPHILA — SPACED BEATS MASSED, WITH A PUBLISHED PROTOCOL: 10 training sessions separated by 15-min rest intervals produce protein-synthesis-dependent LTM lasting ~7 days; the identical number of massed trials produces only anesthesia-resistant memory (ARM) and no LTM. Tully, Preat, Boynton & Del Vecchio, Cell 79:35-47, 1994. Mechanism: oscillatory MP1/PPL1 dopaminergic activity during the *rest* intervals gates LTM. Plaçais et al., Nature Neuroscience 15:592-599, 2012. [confident on the claim; page numbers reasonably confident]
- DROSOPHILA — CONSOLIDATION IS METABOLICALLY EXPENSIVE AND VALUE-GATED: LTM formation upregulates energy metabolism in the mushroom body, and starved flies actively suppress LTM formation to survive. Plaçais, de Tredern, Scheunemann et al., Nature Communications 8:15510, 2017; Plaçais & Preat, Journal of Neuroscience 33:1010-1019, 2013 ('To favor survival under food shortage, the brain disables costly memory'). Consolidation in biology is a budgeted decision, not an automatic consequence of encoding. [reasonably confident]
- DROSOPHILA — CONTRADICTIONS ARE APPENDED, NOT OVERWRITTEN: memory extinction is implemented by forming a *second, opposing* memory trace in a different MB compartment while the original trace persists; re-evaluation is driven by MBON->DAN recurrent feedback on unreinforced re-exposure. Felsenberg, Jacob, Walker et al., Cell 175:709-722, 2018; Felsenberg, Barnstedt, Cognigni, Lin & Waddell, Nature 544:240-244, 2017. Parallel independent consolidated pathways (ARM vs LTM) are mutually exclusive: Isabel, Pascual & Preat, Science 304:1024-1027, 2004. [reasonably confident]
- MATH — MULTI-TIMESCALE SYNAPSES ESCAPE THE PLASTICITY-RIGIDITY DILEMMA: a tridiagonal chain of internal variables u_1..u_m with geometrically increasing capacities C_k and geometrically decreasing couplings g_{k,k+1} (visible weight = u_1) gives memory SNR decaying as ~1/sqrt(t) (power law, not exponential) and capacity scaling ~N/sqrt(log N), i.e. almost linear in synapse count. Benna & Fusi, Nature Neuroscience 19:1697-1706, 2016. Predecessor with cascade metaplastic states giving power-law forgetting and ~sqrt(N) lifetime: Fusi, Drew & Abbott, Neuron 45:599-611, 2005. [confident]
- MATH — POWER-LAW FORGETTING IS RATIONAL, NOT A DEFECT: the probability that an item will be needed again in a natural environment decays as a power law of time since last use, and human retention curves match that environmental statistic almost exactly. Anderson & Schooler, Psychological Science 2:396-408, 1991. This is the formal justification for designing a *power-law* retention profile rather than maximising retention. [confident]
- PSYCHOLOGY — THE SPACING LAW HAS A QUANTITATIVE OPTIMUM: across 254 studies / >14,000 participants, distributed practice beats massed practice, and the optimal inter-study gap is roughly 10-20% of the desired retention interval, growing with that interval. Cepeda, Pashler, Vul, Wixted & Rohrer, Psychological Bulletin 132(3):354-380, 2006; Cepeda, Vul, Rohrer, Wixted & Pashler, Psychological Science 19:1095-1102, 2008. [confident]
- ML SOTA — CONTINUAL PRETRAINING IS ESSENTIALLY SOLVED FOR *SMOOTH* SHIFTS: learning-rate re-warming + re-decaying plus replaying ~5% of the original pretraining distribution matches the performance of retraining from scratch on the union of old+new data, at a small fraction of the compute; validated at 405M and 10B params on Pile->SlimPajama and English->German. Ibrahim, Therien, Gupta, Richter, Anthony, Lesort, Belilovsky & Rish, TMLR 2024, arXiv 2403.08763. Companion: Gupta et al., arXiv 2308.04014 ('How to (re)warm your model?'). [confident on the recipe; exact model sizes reasonably confident]
- ML — THE STABILITY-PLASTICITY TRADEOFF IS A RANK/CAPACITY TRADEOFF: LoRA learns less than full finetuning on the target domain but forgets less of the source domain; full-finetuning weight perturbations have rank 10-100x higher than typical LoRA ranks (r=16-256). Measured on Llama-2 7B/13B, continued pretraining on StarCoder-Python and OpenWebMath, IFT on Magicoder-Evol-Instruct-110k and MetaMathQA-395k, with source-domain probes HellaSwag/ARC-Challenge/WinoGrande. Biderman, Ortiz, Portes, Paul, Greengard, Jennings, King, Havens, Chiley, Frankle, Blakeney & Cunningham, TMLR 2024, arXiv 2405.09673. [confident on qualitative claims; dataset token counts moderate]
- ML — PARAMETRIC MEMORY HAS A HARD CEILING: ~2 bits of knowledge per parameter, consistent across GPT-2/LLaMA/Mistral architectures, reached only after ~1000 exposures per fact; at ~100 exposures capacity falls to roughly 1 bit/param; int8 quantisation preserves it, int4 roughly halves it; 'junk' data severely degrades it unless the source domain is marked. Allen-Zhu & Li, 'Physics of Language Models: Part 3.3, Knowledge Capacity Scaling Laws', arXiv 2404.05405, 2024. Companion on extractability requiring augmentation/rephrasing: Part 3.1, arXiv 2309.14316. [confident]
- ML — NON-PARAMETRIC MEMORY BEATS PARAMETRIC MEMORY PER UNIT OF TRAINING: interpolating a trained LM with a k-nearest-neighbour datastore of 103M cached (context, next-token) pairs improved WikiText-103 perplexity 18.65 -> 16.12 with no additional training. Khandelwal, Levy, Jurafsky, Zettlemoyer & Lewis, ICLR 2020, arXiv 1911.00172. Scaled version: RETRO, Borgeaud et al., ICML 2022 (7.5B + 2T-token database matching much larger models on the Pile). [confident]
- ML — SEQUENTIAL MODEL EDITING COLLAPSES: applying ROME/MEMIT edits sequentially causes gradual degradation and then a sudden 'disabling edit' that destroys the model's general ability. Gupta, Rao & Anumanchipalli, ACL Findings 2024, arXiv 2401.07453; Gu, Xu et al., EMNLP 2024, arXiv 2401.04700 ('Model editing harms general abilities of LLMs'). Base editors: ROME (Meng, Bau, Andonian & Belinkov, NeurIPS 2022, arXiv 2202.05262), MEMIT (Meng, Sharma, Andonian, Belinkov & Bau, ICLR 2023, arXiv 2210.07229, up to 10,000 edits in GPT-J/GPT-NeoX). [confident]
- ML — CAUSAL LOCALIZATION DOES NOT TELL YOU WHERE TO EDIT: the layer identified by causal tracing as storing a fact is not the layer where editing that fact works best; localization and editability are largely uncorrelated. Hase, Bansal, Kim & Ghandeharioun, NeurIPS 2023, arXiv 2301.04213. This undercuts any design that assumes 'find the engram, write there'. [confident]
- ML — FINETUNING IS A WEAK KNOWLEDGE-INJECTION CHANNEL UNLESS AUGMENTED: unsupervised continued finetuning on new documents consistently underperforms retrieval for factual knowledge injection; *paraphrase/fact augmentation* of the same documents closes much of the gap. Ovadia, Brief, Mishaeli & Elisha, EMNLP 2024, arXiv 2312.05934; Mecklenburg et al., arXiv 2404.00213. This is the ML evidence that replay must be *generative and variable*, exactly as hippocampal replay is not verbatim.
- ML — SLEEP-LIKE, DATA-FREE REPLAY REDUCES FORGETTING IN ANNs: convert a trained ReLU net to a spiking (LIF) net, present noise, apply local unsupervised STDP-like plasticity offline with NO task data, convert back — task-1 performance is recovered. Tadros, Krishnan, Ramyaa & Bazhenov, Nature Communications 13:7742, 2022 ('Sleep-like unsupervised replay', SRC). Mechanistic companion: Golden, Delanois, Sanda & Bazhenov, PLoS Computational Biology 18(11):e1010628, 2022 — the sleep phase moves weights into a region jointly good for both tasks. NOTE: demonstrated only on MNIST/Fashion-MNIST/CUB200-scale problems; never at LM scale. [reasonably confident]
- ML — GENERATIVE AND *INTERNAL* REPLAY: the CLS-faithful ML method is a generator + solver pair where the generator replays pseudo-data (Shin, Lee, Kim & Kim, NeurIPS 2017, arXiv 1705.08690). The decisive scaling fix is replaying at a *hidden layer* rather than at the input, with the generator embedded in the main network via feedback connections. van de Ven, Siegelmann & Tolias, Nature Communications 11:4069, 2020. Compressed-feature replay at scale: REMIND, Hayes, Kafle, Shrestha, Acharya & Kanan, ECCV 2020. Taxonomy (task-/domain-/class-incremental; class-IL is the hard regime): van de Ven, Tuytelaars & Tolias, Nature Machine Intelligence 4:1185-1197, 2022. [confident]
- ML — TEST-TIME / FAST-WEIGHT MEMORY IS THE CURRENT INCUMBENT: Titans learns a neural long-term memory at test time by gradient descent on an associative loss with momentum-'surprise' and adaptive forgetting: S_t = eta_t*S_{t-1} - theta_t*grad l(M_{t-1}; x_t); M_t = (1-alpha_t)*M_{t-1} + S_t, with l(M;x)=||M k_t - v_t||^2. Behrouz, Zhong & Mirrokni, arXiv 2501.00663, 2025. Lineage: Schmidhuber, Neural Computation 4(1):131-139, 1992; Schlag, Irie & Schmidhuber, ICML 2021, arXiv 2102.11174 (delta rule in linear attention); Yang et al., NeurIPS 2024, arXiv 2406.06484 (parallel DeltaNet); Gated DeltaNet, Yang, Kautz & Hatamizadeh, ICLR 2025, arXiv 2412.06464. YOUR CONTROL IS THE RIGHT CONTROL. [confident]
- ML — SELF-EDITING WORKS BUT FORGETS: SEAL trains a model (via ReST^EM-style RL) to emit its own finetuning data and hyperparameters, then applies a LoRA update. On a SQuAD passage-incorporation task, no-context accuracy rose 33.5% (base Qwen2.5-7B) -> 47.0%, beating GPT-4.1-generated synthetic data (46.3%). On an ARC-AGI few-shot subset with Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct: 0% (no adaptation) -> 20% (self-edits, no RL) -> 72.5% (after RL), vs 100% for oracle TTT configurations. The paper explicitly names catastrophic forgetting across sequential self-edits as an unsolved limitation. Zweiger, Pari, Guo, Akyurek, Kim & Agrawal, NeurIPS 2025, arXiv 2506.10943. [reasonably confident on the numbers]
- ML — TEST-TIME TRAINING GIVES LARGE GAINS ON NOVEL STRUCTURE: TTT-Linear/TTT-MLP make the RNN hidden state itself a model updated by self-supervised gradient steps, matching or beating Mamba at 125M-1.3B and continuing to improve past 8k context (Sun, Li, Dalal, Xu, Vikram, Zhang, Zhou, Wang, Guestrin et al., arXiv 2407.04620, 2024). Applied to ARC with LoRA adapters per test task, an 8B model reached ~53% on the ARC public validation set, and ~61.9% ensembled with program synthesis. Akyurek, Damani, Qiu, Guo, Kim & Andreas, arXiv 2411.07279, 2024. [confident on TTT; reasonably confident on the ARC numbers]
- ML — EXPLICIT EPISODIC MEMORY FOR EDITING: Larimar attaches a Kanerva-Machine-style distributed episodic memory to a decoder; a write is a closed-form (pseudoinverse / least-squares) update to a memory matrix, giving one-shot knowledge editing with reported 8-11x speedup over ROME/GRACE, plus selective forgetting and input-length generalisation (Larimar built on GPT-2-large and GPT-J). Das, Chaudhury, Nelson, Melnyk, Swaminathan, Dai, Lozano, Kollias, Chenthamarakshan, Navratil, Dan & Chen, ICML 2024, arXiv 2403.11901. NOTE: the Larimar write rule is mathematically the delta rule — the same object as DeltaNet and as the MB's KC->MBON depression. [confident on the mechanism; speedup figure moderate]
- ML — FIXED-SIZE SELF-UPDATING LATENT MEMORY: MemoryLLM equips a 7B Llama-2 with a ~1B-parameter latent memory pool that self-updates by dropping the oldest ~5% of memory tokens and inserting newly compressed context; reported to survive on the order of 10^5-10^6 update steps without collapse. Wang, Yang, Shang & McAuley, ICML 2024, arXiv 2402.04624. Extension M+ adds a co-trained long-term retriever, reportedly pushing usable retention from <20k to >160k tokens at similar GPU memory: ICML 2025, arXiv 2502.00592. [moderate confidence on the numbers]
- ML — MEMORY LAYERS ARE THE MUSHROOM BODY, ALREADY SCALED: product-key memory is a sparse trainable key-value store read by top-k lookup over a sqrt(|K|) factorised key set — structurally identical to sparse random expansion + k-WTA. Lample, Sablayrolles, Ranzato, Denoyer & Jegou, NeurIPS 2019, arXiv 1907.05242. Scaled to 128B memory parameters, where a 1.3B model with memory layers matched a dense model of ~2x the compute on factual QA: 'Memory Layers at Scale', Berges, Oguz et al. (Meta), arXiv 2412.09764, 2024. [reasonably confident]
- ML — CONTEXT-TO-WEIGHTS DISTILLATION IS AN ACTIVE, UNSETTLED FRONT: (a) Cartridges — train a small KV-cache on self-generated 'self-study' conversations about a corpus, matching in-context quality at ~38.6x less memory and ~26.4x higher peak throughput, and composable across cartridges (Eyuboglu, Ehrlich, Arora, Guha, Zinsley, Liu, Re et al., arXiv 2506.06266, 2025) [moderate]; (b) Generative Adapter — map a context to a LoRA-like adapter in a single forward pass (Chen et al., ICLR 2025, arXiv 2411.05877) [moderate]; (c) Memory Decoder — pretrain a small decoder to imitate a kNN-LM's output distribution over a domain, then interpolate it with any same-tokenizer LLM without touching the base model (reported average perplexity reduction of about 6 points across the Qwen2 0.5B-72B family) [UNCERTAIN: I could not verify the arXiv id or the exact figure]; (d) classical prompt/context distillation: Snell, Klein & Zhong, arXiv 2209.15189, 2022. [mixed]
- ML — 'SLEEP-TIME COMPUTE' EXISTS AS A NAMED, MEASURED IDEA: precomputing inferences about a context offline, before any query arrives, reduced test-time compute by roughly 5x for equal accuracy on Stateful GSM-Symbolic and Stateful AIME, with further accuracy gains from scaling the offline phase. Lin, Snell et al. (Letta), arXiv 2504.13171, 2025. [moderate confidence on the 5x]
- ML — WAKE-SLEEP CONSOLIDATION THAT *CREATES* CAPABILITY, NOT JUST PRESERVES IT: DreamCoder alternates a wake phase (solve tasks), a sleep-abstraction phase (compress solutions into a reusable library of routines) and a sleep-dreaming phase (train the recognition network on replayed/fantasised programs); the library grows into a domain-specific language. Ellis, Wong, Nye, Sable-Meyer, Morales, Hewitt, Cary, Solar-Lezama & Tenenbaum, PLDI 2021. Original wake-sleep: Hinton, Dayan, Frey & Neal, Science 268:1158-1161, 1995. This is the best existing template for consolidation as *abstraction*, not merely as anti-forgetting. [confident]
- ML — BENCHMARKS THAT EXIST. Continual instruction tuning: TRACE, 8 tasks / 21,910 instances spanning domain (ScienceQA, FOMC, MeetingBank), multilingual (C-STANCE, 20Minuten), code (Py150) and math (NumGLUE-cm/ds), scored with Overall Performance, Backward Transfer, General Ability Delta and Instruction Following Delta — LLaMA-2-7B-chat loses substantial general ability after sequential finetuning (Wang, Liu, Yan, Qiu, Huang et al., arXiv 2310.06762, 2023). Continual knowledge: CKL/TemporalWiki with the FUAR metric (Jang et al., ICLR 2022 arXiv 2110.03215; EMNLP 2022 arXiv 2204.14211); StreamingQA (Liska et al., ICML 2022, arXiv 2205.11388). Long-horizon conversational memory: LOCOMO, ~300 turns / up to 35 sessions (Maharana, Lee, Tulyakov, Bansal, Barbieri & Fang, ACL 2024, arXiv 2402.17753); LongMemEval, 500 questions over 5 memory abilities, showing ~30% accuracy loss for commercial assistants over sustained interaction (Wu, Zhu, Yang & Yu, ICLR 2025, arXiv 2410.10813). Long-context recall: RULER (arXiv 2404.06654), BABILong (arXiv 2406.10149), MQAR/Zoology (Arora et al., arXiv 2312.04927 — the task you already use). [confident on existence; LongMemEval's 30% figure moderate]
- ML — CLASSICAL REGULARIZATION BASELINES AND THEIR BIOLOGICAL ANCESTOR: EWC penalises movement of parameters with high Fisher information (Kirkpatrick et al., PNAS 114:3521-3526, 2017); Synaptic Intelligence uses a path integral of gradient*displacement (Zenke, Poole & Ganguli, ICML 2017); and the Benna-Fusi cascade has been used directly as a continual-learning optimizer (Kaplanis, Shanahan & Clopath, 'Continual Reinforcement Learning with Complex Synapses', ICML 2018). A Bayesian justification for per-synapse learning rates proportional to weight uncertainty: Aitchison, Jegminat, Menendez, Pfister, Pouget & Latham, Nature Neuroscience 24:565-571, 2021. [confident on the ML three; moderate on the Aitchison details]
- REPOSITORY-INTERNAL, ALREADY ESTABLISHED (from RESULTS.md): on MQAR at T=64/8 pairs, the mushroom-body memory reaches 0.9814 +/- 0.0029 while compute-matched Gated DeltaNet reaches 0.9979 +/- 0.0016 (-5.6 seed-SD), DeltaNet and linear attention both reach 1.0000, and a learned write gate (0.9966) beats the error gate (-2.6 seed-SD). Real vs shuffled MB wiring: +0.0042 [+0.0010, +0.0083], i.e. the shuffle is at parity or better. These are consistent with theory and should be treated as settled, not re-litigated.

### Principles and their translation

| principle | biology / mathematics | translation to a sequence model | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Two-stage memory (fast sparse episodic store + slow dense parametric store) with an explicit, scheduled transfer operator between them. The transfer, not either store, is the object to design. | CLS theory: hippocampus learns in one shot with pattern-separated codes, neocortex learns slowly with overlapping codes, and interleaved replay moves content between them (McClelland, McNaughton & O'Reilly, Psych Review 102:419-457, 1995; Kumaran, Hassabis & McClelland, TiCS 20:512-534, 2016). The fly has the same split: labile MB compartment traces (minutes-hours) vs protein-synthesis-dependent LTM requiring spaced training (Tully et al., Cell 79:35-47, 1994; Isabel, Pascual & Preat, Science 304:1024-1027, 2004). | Architecture = frozen-ish backbone W_slow + a fast store F (KV cache, memory-layer slots, or a DeltaNet state) + an explicit CONSOLIDATE(F -> W_slow) operator run offline on a schedule. Measure the operator, not the stores: report bits retained per consolidation FLOP. Existing instantiations to beat: Cartridges (arXiv 2506.06266), Generative Adapter (arXiv 2411.05877), SEAL (arXiv 2506.10943), MemoryLLM/M+ (arXiv 2402.04624 / 2502.00592). None of them schedules the transfer; all consolidate greedily on arrival. | established |
| Gate consolidation on BAYESIAN surprise (how much the belief moved), never on Shannon surprise / prediction-error magnitude. This is the direct, specific explanation of the Phase-2 null. | Bayesian surprise is KL(q(M / D_{1:t}) // q(M / D_{1:t-1})); Shannon surprise is -log p(x_t). They differ exactly where the data are noisy-but-unpredictable: -log p is large for irreducible entropy (which should NOT be consolidated), while the KL is near zero there (Itti & Baldi, Vision Research 49:1295-1306, 2009). Biologically, the fly does not gate on error magnitude either: it has a dedicated novelty channel, MBON-alpha'3, that habituates over minutes and reports familiarity, separate from valence/error DANs (Hattori, Aso, Swartz, Rubin, Abbott & Axel, Cell 169:956-969, 2017). | For a Gaussian posterior over the memory with precision Lambda: gate g_t = 0.5*delta_mu^T Lambda delta_mu + 0.5*(tr(Lambda_t Lambda_{t-1}^{-1}) - d + ln/Lambda_{t-1}///Lambda_t/). Cheap surrogates that preserve the distinction: (a) LEARNING PROGRESS — the drop in loss on a held-out probe of the same item, not the loss itself (Schmidhuber's compression progress, IEEE TAMD 2010; Oudeyer & Kaplan 2007); (b) DISAGREEMENT — variance across a small ensemble or across dropout masks; (c) a habituating familiarity trace f_t = rho*f_{t-1} + (1-rho)*sim(x_t, F), gate = 1 - f_t, which is literally the MBON-alpha'3 computation. Pre-register: Bayesian-surprise gate vs Shannon-surprise gate vs learned gate, with the learned gate as the strong control that already beat you (0.9966 vs 0.9814). | promising |
| Retention should be engineered as a POWER LAW, produced by a bank of geometrically-spaced time constants — and a power law is the optimal profile, not a compromise. | A chain of coupled variables with geometrically growing capacities and shrinking couplings yields SNR ~ 1/sqrt(t) and capacity ~ N/sqrt(log N), versus ~log N for bounded binary synapses and ~sqrt(N) for the cascade model (Benna & Fusi, Nat Neurosci 19:1697-1706, 2016; Fusi, Drew & Abbott, Neuron 45:599-611, 2005). Power-law retention is also RATIONAL: the probability an item is needed again decays as a power law of time since last use in natural environments, and human forgetting matches it (Anderson & Schooler, Psych Science 2:396-408, 1991). Identity: sum_k w_k exp(-t/tau_k) with tau_k = tau_0 c^k and w_k proportional to tau_k^{-beta} approximates A t^{-beta} for tau_0 << t << tau_0 c^{m-1}. | Decompose every consolidated parameter (or every memory slot) as W = sum_{k=1..m} u_k with tau_k = tau_0 c^k, c in [2,4], m = 6-8 covering 10^0 to 10^5 tokens. Two variants worth a head-to-head: (i) SERIAL Benna-Fusi chain (conserved 'syncytium', O(log T) variables, provable variance bound); (ii) PARALLEL fly-style compartments — one shared write, m independent decays, no coupling (Aso & Rubin, eLife 5:e16135, 2016). The serial version is already approximated by Nested Learning / HOPE's Continuum Memory System (Behrouz et al., NeurIPS 2025, cited in this repo's LITERATURE.md as arXiv 2512.24695); the parallel-compartment version with a single shared write is, as far as I can tell, untested. That difference is a clean, cheap, pre-registerable experiment with a derivable prediction. | established |
| Spaced, EXPANDING-interval replay per item, instead of a uniform 5% replay mix. This is the single highest value-per-FLOP experiment available to this project. | Two independent literatures converge on the same law. Fly: 10 training sessions with 15-min rests produce 7-day LTM; the same number of massed trials produces none, and the rest intervals are when oscillatory MP1/PPL1 dopamine gates consolidation (Tully et al., Cell 79:35-47, 1994; Placais et al., Nat Neurosci 15:592-599, 2012). Human: across 254 studies, the optimal inter-study gap is about 10-20% of the target retention interval and grows with it (Cepeda et al., Psychological Bulletin 132:354-380, 2006; Cepeda et al., Psych Science 19:1095-1102, 2008). | Replay item i at offsets g_0, g_0*r, g_0*r^2, ... with r in [1.5, 2.5] and g_0 ~ 0.15 * target horizon, scheduled per item by a priority queue keyed on a predicted need-probability. Compare against the incumbent SOTA recipe — LR re-warm/re-decay + uniform 5% replay (Ibrahim et al., TMLR 2024, arXiv 2403.08763) — at MATCHED total replay tokens, so that the only difference is the schedule. This is runnable at 20-125M params on one GPU, has never been published for LLM continual pretraining to my knowledge, and both source literatures predict a real effect. Beware the confound: spaced schedules also change the effective LR schedule; hold that fixed. | promising |
| Consolidate offline with NO new input, combining generative replay with GLOBAL MULTIPLICATIVE DOWNSCALING. Downscaling is the half everyone omits. | Sleep does two things, not one: it replays (Buzsaki, Hippocampus 25:1073-1188, 2015; causal: Girardeau et al., Nat Neurosci 12:1222-1223, 2009) AND it renormalises synaptic strength downward, with ~18% reduction in axon-spine interface measured by serial EM (Tononi & Cirelli, Neuron 81:12-34, 2014; de Vivo et al., Science 355:507-510, 2017). Downscaling converts an absolute-strength code into a relative one, which both frees capacity and implements a soft consolidation threshold (only what was replayed survives the scaling). In ANNs this exact combination recovers forgotten tasks with NO task data (Tadros et al., Nature Communications 13:7742, 2022; Golden et al., PLoS Comp Biol 18(11):e1010628, 2022). | Sleep phase: (1) freeze the data stream; (2) sample from the model itself, conditioned on episodic keys held in the fast store (generative/internal replay — replay HIDDEN STATES not tokens, per van de Ven, Siegelmann & Tolias, Nat Commun 11:4069, 2020, and REMIND, Hayes et al., ECCV 2020); (3) distill those samples into W_slow; (4) apply multiplicative decay W <- (1-eta)*W to the fast store and to the consolidation-eligible subspace. Note that (4) alone is a strong, nearly-free baseline you must control for — much of SRC's benefit may be the scaling, not the replay. Separate them in the pre-registration. | promising |
| Consolidation is a BUDGETED DECISION with a computable objective, not an automatic consequence of encoding. Formalise it as a knapsack over a FLOP budget. | LTM formation in the fly has a measured metabolic cost (upregulated MB energy metabolism, Placais et al., Nature Communications 8:15510, 2017) and flies actively DISABLE costly memory formation when starved (Placais & Preat, J Neurosci 33:1010-1019, 2013). Combined with Anderson & Schooler's rational analysis (1991), the objective is well-posed: Value(i) = p_need(i, horizon) * [L(model without i) - L(model with i)], where p_need follows a power law of recency and frequency. | maximise sum_i x_i * Value(i) subject to sum_i x_i * cost(i) <= B, x in {0,1}. Estimate p_need online from the stream's own recency/frequency statistics (a Zipf/power-law estimator); estimate the loss delta from a cheap probe (loss on the item with the fast store masked vs unmasked). Report a NEW metric: consolidation efficiency = bits of retained knowledge per training FLOP at horizon T. No LLM paper I know of formulates or reports this, which makes it both a defensible novelty and an uncontested leaderboard. Risk: the metric must be pre-registered before any results, or it will read as post-hoc. | speculative |
| Contradictions must be APPENDED as a competing trace with an arbitrator, never written in place. In-place editing is why sequential updating collapses. | Fly extinction does not erase the original trace: a second, opposing-valence trace is written in a different MB compartment and the two compete at the output (Felsenberg et al., Cell 175:709-722, 2018); revaluation on unreinforced re-exposure is driven by MBON->DAN recurrent feedback (Felsenberg et al., Nature 544:240-244, 2017). ARM and LTM are separate, mutually exclusive consolidated pathways (Isabel, Pascual & Preat, Science 304:1024-1027, 2004). ML side: sequential ROME/MEMIT editing degrades and then catastrophically disables the model (Gupta, Rao & Anumanchipalli, ACL Findings 2024, arXiv 2401.07453), and localization does not even indicate where to edit (Hase et al., NeurIPS 2023, arXiv 2301.04213). | Represent each update as a new addressable unit — a memory slot (Larimar, arXiv 2403.11901), a memory-layer key block (arXiv 2412.09764), or a rank-r adapter — plus a learned arbitration/routing signal, rather than as a modification of existing parameters. Orthogonality between successive units is a cheap, principled arbitration prior (O-LoRA, Wang et al., EMNLP Findings 2023, arXiv 2310.14152). Pre-registered claim: append-and-arbitrate degrades sub-linearly in the number of sequential updates where MEMIT degrades super-linearly. This is measurable, currently unmeasured at 10^3-10^5 updates, and directly falsifiable. | promising |
| Forgetting is an ACTIVE, separately-clocked process that must be switched off during consolidation — not passive decay. | Rac1-dependent active forgetting in the fly runs on a timescale of hours and is dissociable from encoding (Shuai et al., Cell 140:579-589, 2010); ongoing dopaminergic activity erases MB traces, and SLEEP works by suppressing that erasure (Berry et al., Cell 161:1656-1667, 2015; Berry et al., Neuron 74:530-542, 2012). So the fly's consolidation controller has two levers with different clocks: write gain and erase gain. | Give the fast store an explicit, learned forget gate alpha_t (Gated DeltaNet's S_t = S_{t-1}(alpha_t(I - beta_t k_t k_t^T)) + beta_t v_t k_t^T, arXiv 2412.06464; Titans' (1-alpha_t) decay, arXiv 2501.00663) AND a global 'consolidation mode' flag that clamps alpha_t = 1 (no forgetting) during the offline phase.
