The specific, novel, testable claim is that suppressing forgetting during the offline phase is necessary for the offline phase to help — an ablation nobody in the LM literature has run because nobody has both mechanisms. | promising |
| Move knowledge from context to weights as an explicit distillation with a measurable exchange rate, because in-context memory is perfect-but-rented and parametric memory is durable-but-capped. | Parametric capacity is ~2 bits/parameter and needs ~1000 exposures to reach it (Allen-Zhu & Li, arXiv 2404.05405); a non-parametric datastore gets WikiText-103 18.65 -> 16.12 with zero training (Khandelwal et al., ICLR 2020). There is also a mathematical bridge: a transformer block conditioned on a context C is equivalent to the same block with a rank-1 weight update, T_W(C, x) = T_{W + dW(C)}(x) — in-context learning IS a fast-weight update ('Learning without training: the implicit dynamics of in-context learning', Dherin, Munn, Mazzawi & Wong, Google, arXiv 2507.16003, 2025) [MODERATE confidence on this specific paper]; constructive predecessors: von Oswald et al., ICML 2023, arXiv 2212.07677; Dai et al., ACL Findings 2023, arXiv 2212.10559. | Define the exchange rate explicitly: EX = (bits of task performance retained at horizon T) / (training FLOPs spent consolidating). Then the fly's 'is this worth the ATP?' decision becomes a threshold on EX. Practically, the highest-leverage lever from the ML side is AUGMENTATION: unsupervised finetuning on raw documents is a weak channel, but paraphrase/fact-augmented finetuning works far better (Ovadia et al., EMNLP 2024; Mecklenburg et al., arXiv 2404.00213) — which is the ML confirmation that replay must be generative and variable, exactly as hippocampal replay is not verbatim. | established |
| Replay in a LATENT space, not in token space. This is both the biologically plausible option and the one that scales. | Cortical replay is of compressed activity patterns, not of sensory input; hippocampal indexing theory has the hippocampus storing pointers, not content. The decisive ML evidence is that generative replay only scales past toy datasets once replay happens at a hidden layer with the generator embedded in the main network (van de Ven, Siegelmann & Tolias, Nature Communications 11:4069, 2020); compressed-feature replay handles ImageNet at 959 examples/second (REMIND, Hayes et al., ECCV 2020). | Replay compressed KV summaries / hidden-state prototypes rather than regenerating text. A 'cartridge' (arXiv 2506.06266) IS a latent replay buffer with a good compression ratio (reported ~38.6x memory reduction). Concretely: store per-document latent keys in the fast store, and during the offline phase reconstruct approximate hidden trajectories from them to supervise W_slow, rather than sampling tokens. Cheaper by roughly the compression ratio, and it sidesteps the model-collapse risk of training on your own generated text. | promising |
| Sparse pattern-separated coding is real biology and a real interference bound, but it is NOT a competitive sequence-memory mechanism at matched compute. Keep the sparsity, drop the claim. | The MB enforces ~5% Kenyon-cell coding level via APL feedback inhibition, with random PN->KC convergence at a fan-in of about 6 (Caron, Ruta, Abbott & Axel, Nature 497:113-117, 2013; Li, Lindsey, Marin et al., eLife 9:e62576, 2020). Sparse random projections give near-orthogonal codes, so interference scales with code overlap. But mathematically this is a FIXED random feature map with a Hebbian/depression read-out, whereas the delta rule is the exact online least-squares solution — so DeltaNet must dominate asymptotically at matched width. | Your own numbers confirm it: mb 0.9814 vs gdn 0.9979 vs deltanet/linattn 1.0000 on MQAR (RESULTS.md). The honest niche for sparse k-WTA expansion is (a) one-shot writes with NO gradient available, (b) hard capacity bounds under a fixed slot budget, and (c) interference-free ADDRESSING for an append-only store — which is exactly the role product-key memory layers already play at 128B memory parameters (arXiv 2412.09764). Use it as the addressing scheme for the fast store; do not re-run it as a sequence-mixing competitor. | established |
| Build the consolidation controller as a small set of typed, separately-clocked compartments driven by distinct global signals (novelty, value, sleep pressure, resource budget) — the fly's actual architecture — rather than as one monolithic gate. | The fly has ~15 MB compartments, each with its own DAN and MBON, writing the same event at cell-type-specific rates spanning minutes to days (Aso et al., eLife 3:e04577/e04580, 2014; Aso & Rubin, eLife 5:e16135, 2016), plus a separate sleep-pressure integrator (dFB, Pimentel et al., Nature 536:333-337, 2016; ellipsoid-body R5 sleep drive, Liu et al., Curr Biol 2016 [moderate]), plus a separate novelty channel (Hattori et al., Cell 2017), plus a metabolic brake (Placais & Preat, 2013). These are four distinguishable scalar controllers, not one 'surprise'. | Four scalars, each with its own update rule and time constant, feeding a small controller: n_t (novelty, habituating, minutes-equivalent), v_t (value / predicted need probability, power-law recency), s_t (accumulated unconsolidated mass = 'sleep pressure', triggers the offline phase when it crosses a threshold), b_t (remaining FLOP budget). Consolidation rate = f(n_t, v_t, s_t, b_t). The pre-registerable claim is that this four-signal controller beats (i) always-consolidate, (ii) uniform 5% replay, and (iii) a single learned scalar gate, at matched total consolidation FLOPs. The ablation is cheap and each signal is individually motivated by a named fly circuit — which is what makes the connectome claim honest here, in a way the weight-mask claim never was. | speculative |

### Numbers an engineer can use

- ~5% replay of the original pretraining distribution + LR re-warm and re-decay matches full retraining from scratch on old+new data; validated at 405M and 10B parameters (Pile -> SlimPajama; English -> German). Ibrahim et al., TMLR 2024, arXiv 2403.08763. THIS IS THE NUMBER TO BEAT.
- ~2 bits of knowledge per parameter is the parametric ceiling, reached only after ~1000 exposures per fact; ~1 bit/param at ~100 exposures; int8 preserves it, int4 roughly halves it. Allen-Zhu & Li, arXiv 2404.05405, 2024.
- kNN-LM: WikiText-103 perplexity 18.65 -> 16.12 using a 103M-entry datastore and zero additional training. Khandelwal et al., ICLR 2020, arXiv 1911.00172.
- SEAL: no-context SQuAD accuracy 33.5% (base Qwen2.5-7B) -> 47.0% after RL-trained self-edits, vs 46.3% for GPT-4.1-generated synthetic data. ARC-AGI few-shot subset (Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct): 0% -> 20% (self-edits, no RL) -> 72.5% (after RL), vs 100% oracle TTT. arXiv 2506.10943, NeurIPS 2025. [reasonably confident]
- Test-time training on ARC: ~53% on ARC public validation with an 8B model + per-task LoRA; ~61.9% when ensembled with program synthesis. Akyurek et al., arXiv 2411.07279, 2024. [reasonably confident]
- Benna-Fusi multi-timescale synapse: memory SNR decays as ~1/sqrt(t); capacity ~N/sqrt(log N) (nearly linear in N), versus ~log N for bounded binary synapses and ~sqrt(N) for the Fusi-Drew-Abbott cascade. Nature Neuroscience 19:1697-1706, 2016; Neuron 45:599-611, 2005.
- Optimal spacing gap for human retention: roughly 10-20% of the target retention interval, and the optimum GROWS with that interval (e.g. a 1-week retention target favours a gap of ~1 day; a 1-year target favours ~3-4 weeks). Cepeda et al., Psychological Bulletin 132(3):354-380, 2006; Cepeda et al., Psychological Science 19:1095-1102, 2008.
- Drosophila LTM protocol: 10 training sessions x 15 min rest intervals -> memory lasting ~7 days; the same trials massed -> anesthesia-resistant memory only, no LTM. Tully, Preat, Boynton & Del Vecchio, Cell 79:35-47, 1994.
- Sleep-related synaptic downscaling: ~18% reduction in axon-spine interface (serial EM, mouse cortex). de Vivo et al., Science 355:507-510, 2017. Sharp-wave ripples: ~140-200 Hz, ~50-100 ms duration, replay time-compressed roughly 5-20x. Buzsaki, Hippocampus 25:1073-1188, 2015.
- Mushroom body anatomy for sizing a memory module: ~2,000 Kenyon cells per hemisphere, ~5% coding level enforced by APL feedback inhibition, PN->KC fan-in ~6, 15 compartments, ~20 DAN types, ~21-24 MBON types. Caron et al., Nature 497:113-117, 2013; Aso et al., eLife 3:e04577, 2014; Li et al., eLife 9:e62576, 2020. (MaleCNS v1.0 counts in this repo: 166,700 neurons, 10.5M edges, 30.8% inhibitory.)
- Drosophila active-forgetting time constant: Rac1-dependent forgetting acts over hours (Shuai et al., Cell 140:579-589, 2010). Fly novelty signal (MBON-alpha'3) habituates over minutes (Hattori et al., Cell 169:956-969, 2017). These are the two constants to use when setting tau for a novelty gate and a decay gate.
- LoRA vs full finetuning: full-FT weight perturbations have rank 10-100x higher than typical LoRA ranks (r=16-256); LoRA scores lower on target-domain accuracy but preserves more source-domain accuracy (HellaSwag/ARC-Challenge/WinoGrande) on Llama-2 7B/13B. Biderman et al., TMLR 2024, arXiv 2405.09673.
- Memory layers at scale: a 1.3B model with product-key memory layers matched a dense model of ~2x the compute on factual QA, with memory scaled to 128B parameters. arXiv 2412.09764, 2024. Original product-key memory with sqrt(|K|) sub-key factorisation: arXiv 1907.05242, NeurIPS 2019. [reasonably confident]
- Cartridges (context-to-KV distillation via self-study): ~38.6x memory reduction and ~26.4x higher peak throughput at parity with in-context learning, composable across cartridges. arXiv 2506.06266, 2025. [MODERATE confidence on the exact multipliers]
- Sleep-time compute: ~5x reduction in test-time compute for equal accuracy on Stateful GSM-Symbolic and Stateful AIME; further gains from scaling the offline phase. arXiv 2504.13171, 2025. [MODERATE]
- MemoryLLM: 7B Llama-2 + ~1B-parameter latent memory pool, ~5% of memory tokens replaced per self-update, reported stable over on the order of 10^5-10^6 updates. M+ extends usable retention from <20k to >160k tokens at similar GPU memory. arXiv 2402.04624 (ICML 2024) and arXiv 2502.00592 (ICML 2025). [MODERATE on the numbers]
- TRACE benchmark: 8 tasks, 21,910 instances, spanning domain-specific / multilingual / code / math, with Overall Performance, Backward Transfer, General Ability Delta and Instruction Following Delta metrics. arXiv 2310.06762, 2023.
- LOCOMO long-horizon conversation: ~300 turns and ~9K tokens per conversation across up to 35 sessions. arXiv 2402.17753, ACL 2024. LongMemEval: 500 questions over 5 long-term memory abilities; commercial assistants reported to lose ~30% accuracy over sustained interaction. arXiv 2410.10813, ICLR 2025. [30% figure MODERATE]
- REPOSITORY BASELINE (already yours): MQAR T=64 / 8 pairs — mb 0.9814 +/- 0.0029, mb_connectome 0.9772, mb_shuffled 0.9782, gdn_matched 0.9979 +/- 0.0016, deltanet_matched 1.0000, linattn_matched 1.0000, abl_learned_gate 0.9966, abl_hebb 0.9417. Gated DeltaNet is the correct compute-matched control for any new memory module.
- REPOSITORY BASELINE (already yours): TinyShakespeare val bits/char at N=1024 — real 2.4423 +/- 0.0146, shuffle 2.4369, ER 2.4352, dense_param 2.5073, dense_N 2.4116. Seed SD ~0.005-0.045, so a pre-registered 2-SD bar at this scale is roughly 0.01-0.09 bits/char. Any new claim needs an effect larger than that, or a larger scale.

### Dead ends

- ERROR-MAGNITUDE (Shannon-surprise) WRITE GATES. Your H1 shows a learned gate beating the error gate by -2.6 seed-SD (0.9966 vs 0.9814), and your H2 shows K=3 free-energy steps gaining only 0.026 bits/char (0.7 seed-SD). The reason is mathematical, not implementational: -log p(x) is large for irreducible noise, which is exactly what must NOT be consolidated. Note that Titans' 'surprise' is also not a pure error gate — it is gradient magnitude with LEARNED momentum eta_t and LEARNED decay alpha_t (arXiv 2501.00663), i.e. a learned gate with a surprise-shaped initialisation. Do not re-run a pure error gate.
- SPARSE RANDOM EXPANSION + k-WTA AS A SEQUENCE-MEMORY COMPETITOR. Mathematically a fixed random feature map with Hebbian read-out; the delta rule is the exact online least-squares solution, so DeltaNet/Gated DeltaNet must win at matched width. Your numbers (0.9814 vs 0.9979/1.0000) reproduce this. Sparse addressing is still useful — as the ADDRESSING scheme for an append-only store, which is what product-key memory layers already are (arXiv 1907.05242; arXiv 2412.09764) — but not as a mixer.
- IN-PLACE PARAMETRIC KNOWLEDGE EDITING AT SCALE. Sequential ROME/MEMIT editing produces gradual degradation and then a catastrophic 'disabling edit' (Gupta, Rao & Anumanchipalli, ACL Findings 2024, arXiv 2401.07453; Gu et al., EMNLP 2024, arXiv 2401.04700). And localization does not even tell you where to edit — causal-tracing-identified layers are not the best edit sites (Hase et al., NeurIPS 2023, arXiv 2301.04213). Any design premised on 'find the engram, overwrite it' is starting from a known failure.
- PURE REGULARIZATION-BASED CONTINUAL LEARNING (EWC, SI, MAS) AT LLM SCALE. Widely reported to underperform replay on long task sequences and in class-incremental settings; the taxonomy paper (van de Ven, Tuytelaars & Tolias, Nature Machine Intelligence 4:1185-1197, 2022) shows regularization fails specifically in class-IL, which is the regime that resembles open-ended text. [MODERATE confidence — I could not re-verify a single decisive LLM-scale ablation, but the direction is consistent across the surveys arXiv 2404.16789 and 2402.01364.]
- UNSUPERVISED CONTINUED FINETUNING ON RAW DOCUMENTS AS A KNOWLEDGE-INJECTION CHANNEL. It loses to retrieval consistently (Ovadia et al., EMNLP 2024, arXiv 2312.05934). It only becomes competitive with paraphrase/fact augmentation — which is itself the evidence that replay must be generative and variable. Do not budget a run on plain continued finetuning.
- LoRA AS A FORGETTING FIX. LoRA forgets less but also learns less, and the two are the same fact (rank-limited perturbation). Biderman et al., TMLR 2024, arXiv 2405.09673. Forgetting still scales as a power law with fine-tuned parameter count and update steps even under LoRA [Kalajdzievski, arXiv 2401.05605, 2024 — UNCERTAIN on the exact exponents]. LoRA is a useful *container* for an appended trace; it is not a consolidation mechanism.
- BACKPROP-FREE PREDICTIVE CODING AS A TRAINING METHOD FOR LMs. Per this repo's own sweep: muPC scales depth to 128 layers but only on MNIST/Fashion-MNIST/CIFAR-10 (arXiv 2505.13124); PCX benchmarks show PC nets still trailing BP; a GitHub search returned zero competitive backprop-free PC language models. Predictive coding's demonstrated advantage is in batch-size-1 online/continual settings (Song et al., Nature Neuroscience 27:348-358, 2024, prospective configuration) — use it there or not at all.
- SPIKING NEURAL NETWORKS AS A ROUTE TO COMPETITIVE LM QUALITY. SpikeGPT reached only 260M parameters (arXiv 2302.13939, 2023); SpikingBrain-7B/76B claims large efficiency wins on non-NVIDIA hardware [arXiv 2509.05276, 2025 — UNCERTAIN, and to my knowledge unreplicated]. SNNs are a deployment/efficiency story, not a quality story. The ONE place spiking earns its keep in this brief is as the offline consolidation substrate in Tadros 2022 / Golden 2022, where local STDP-like plasticity on noise is what recovers forgotten tasks — and even there it has only been shown at MNIST/CUB200 scale.
- MONOLITHIC 'ONE SURPRISE SCALAR' CONTROLLERS. The fly does not have one: it has a habituating novelty channel (MBON-alpha'3), separate compartment-specific DAN write signals, a sleep-pressure integrator (dFB), and a metabolic brake, each with a different time constant. Collapsing these into a single running-surprise arousal term (as FlyAI v0.2 did) discards exactly the structure that makes the biology work.
- CHASING PERPLEXITY SOTA ON STANDARD i.i.d. BENCHMARKS. Nothing in fly biology predicts a scaling-law advantage; the fly has ~135k neurons and does not do language. Every honest advantage available here lives in the NON-i.i.d., single-pass, budget-constrained regime. Framing the project as 'beat a transformer on FineWeb bits-per-byte' guarantees another null.

### Open questions

- WHAT IS THE FORGETTING TAX OF A NON-i.i.d. STREAM? Nobody has published, at any scale, the gap in held-out bits/char between training on a domain-sorted single-pass stream and training on the same tokens shuffled i.i.d. That number is the size of the prize. It is measurable in a day at 20-125M params and should be the FIRST thing this project measures, before choosing a method.
- IS SERIAL (Benna-Fusi chain, conserved, coupled) OR PARALLEL (fly compartments, independent decays, shared write) MULTI-TIMESCALE CONSOLIDATION BETTER FOR A LANGUAGE MODEL? Nested Learning / HOPE implements a frequency-staggered serial-ish version; the parallel-compartment version with a single shared write appears untested. Both produce power-law retention; they differ in variance and in how they interact with gradient noise.
- DOES EXPANDING-INTERVAL, PER-ITEM REPLAY BEAT UNIFORM 5% REPLAY AT MATCHED REPLAY TOKENS? Two independent literatures (fly LTM, human spacing effect) predict yes with a specific optimum (gap ~10-20% of retention horizon). No LLM continual-pretraining paper I know of has tested it. Confound to control: spacing changes the effective LR schedule.
- IN SLEEP-LIKE CONSOLIDATION, HOW MUCH OF THE BENEFIT IS THE REPLAY AND HOW MUCH IS THE MULTIPLICATIVE DOWNSCALING? Tadros 2022 and Golden 2022 never separate them. Global downscaling alone is nearly free and may account for most of the effect. This ablation has to be in the pre-registration or the result will be uninterpretable.
- WHAT IS THE EXCHANGE RATE BETWEEN CONTEXT AND WEIGHTS? Bits of durable knowledge per training FLOP, at a fixed retention horizon. No paper reports it. Defining it well (and pre-registering the probe set) may be the most durable contribution available here, independent of whether the architecture wins.
- DOES BAYESIAN SURPRISE ACTUALLY BEAT A LEARNED GATE, OR DOES THE LEARNED GATE JUST LEARN BAYESIAN SURPRISE? Plausibly the learned gate that beat your error gate converged to something KL-like. If so, the contribution is interpretability and sample-efficiency, not accuracy — which is still publishable but must be claimed honestly.
- DO APPEND-AND-ARBITRATE MEMORIES DEGRADE SUB-LINEARLY IN THE NUMBER OF SEQUENTIAL UPDATES WHERE IN-PLACE EDITS DEGRADE SUPER-LINEARLY? The fly says yes (extinction = parallel opposing trace); MEMIT says in-place fails. Nobody has plotted general-ability degradation vs number of sequential updates on the same axes for slot-based (Larimar/MemoryLLM), adapter-based (LoRA-MoE) and in-place (MEMIT) editing out to 10^4-10^5 updates.
- DOES SCALE HELP OR HURT FORGETTING? Ramasesh, Lewkowycz & Dyer (ICLR 2022) find larger pretrained models forget LESS; Luo et al. (arXiv 2308.08747) find forgetting INCREASES from 1B to 7B during continual instruction tuning. The regimes differ (pretrain-then-finetune vs long instruction sequences) but the tension is unresolved and it matters for whether small-scale results here will transfer.
- CAN THE FLY'S FOUR CONTROL SIGNALS (habituating novelty, value/need, sleep pressure, resource budget) BE SHOWN TO BE INDIVIDUALLY NECESSARY? A four-way ablation at matched consolidation FLOPs is the experiment that would make a connectome-motivated claim honest — the signals are named circuits with measured time constants, unlike the wiring prior that failed in Phase 1.
- IS THERE A CONTRASTIVE OBJECTIVE THAT MAKES CONSOLIDATION CHEAPER? The brief names contrastive learning; the natural link is that pattern separation (the MB's job) is exactly what a contrastive/decorrelating objective produces. Whether an explicit decorrelation loss on the fast store's keys reduces interference per stored item, measurably, at LM scale, is untested as far as I can tell. [I could not search for 2025-2026 contrastive-memory work; flagging as genuinely unchecked rather than absent.]
- WHAT DOES 'REPLAY' MEAN WHEN THE GENERATOR IS THE MODEL ITSELF? Self-generated replay risks model collapse / distribution narrowing over many cycles. van de Ven 2020's internal replay and REMIND's compressed features sidestep this by replaying stored latents rather than sampling. Whether latent replay avoids collapse over 10^2-10^3 consolidation cycles in an LM is unknown.
- CAN ANY OF THIS PRODUCE A DEFENSIBLE 'SOTA' CLAIM? My honest read: yes, but only in a regime this project would have to DEFINE and then win in, with matched controls — single-pass non-stationary continual pretraining at fixed FLOPs, scored on both forward performance and retention, against the Ibrahim-2024 recipe and an i.i.d. oracle. Winning a regime you defined is only credible if the regime is defined and pre-registered before any method is chosen, and if the incumbent recipe is implemented faithfully and reported at its best.

### Relevance to language models

This brief is the most directly applicable of the candidate branches, because continual learning is the one place where current LMs are unambiguously broken and where the fly supplies real constants rather than metaphors. Concretely: (1) every frontier LM is trained i.i.d. over multiple epochs and then frozen; the moment you expose one to a single-pass, temporally correlated stream, performance and retention both degrade and nobody has measured how much. (2) Parametric memory is capped at ~2 bits/parameter and needs ~1000 exposures (Allen-Zhu & Li 2024), while context memory is perfect but rented — so the context-to-weights transfer operator is a first-class architectural component that essentially no released model has. (3) The strongest 2025-2026 systems (Titans, Gated DeltaNet, SEAL, MemoryLLM/M+, Cartridges) all implement one half of CLS — a fast store — and none implements a scheduled, gated, budgeted consolidation into slow weights; SEAL explicitly reports forgetting across sequential self-edits. That is an open architectural slot, not a crowded leaderboard. (4) The fly's consolidation controller decomposes into four separately-clocked signals (habituating novelty, value/need, sleep pressure, metabolic budget) with published time constants, which is a far better-specified prior than any connectivity matrix and is directly ablatable. (5) Practically, all of this is testable at 20-125M parameters on one GPU against Gated DeltaNet and the Ibrahim-2024 replay recipe — the scale this project can actually run — whereas perplexity SOTA is not. The right ambition is to define and win the single-pass non-stationary regime, and to introduce consolidation efficiency (bits retained per training FLOP at horizon T) as a metric, rather than to chase i.i.d. bits-per-byte again.


## Reservoir computing, random features and extreme learning machines at scale: what a frozen fly-style expansion can and cannot buy a 100M+ language model

*Key: `reservoir-random-features`.*

### Summary

"Reservoirs cannot be the backbone. Dambre et al. (Sci Rep 2:514, 2012) prove the total information-processing capacity of an N-node fading-memory system with linear readout is exactly N, and Jaeger's MC <= N caps the linear share; a 100M-parameter dense reservoir carries ~10^4 units of state against a transformer's ~1.5e8 scalars of KV cache at 8k context. This repository already measured the consequence: a frozen 211k-neuron MaleCNS echo-state LM at 3.644 bpc, tied with a shuffle (3.629), a random graph (3.546) and a bigram (3.572), against 2.266 for a 6M transformer. Theory concurs independently - random features approximate at O(1/sqrt(N)) (Rahimi & Recht 2007/2008), need exp(Omega(d)) units for a single ReLU neuron (Yehudai & Shamir, NeurIPS 2019), and lose to learned features precisely on low-dimensional-manifold targets (Ghorbani et al., NeurIPS 2020). A reservoir is the lazy/kernel regime that muP exists to avoid.\n\nWhat survives is narrower and sharper. Frozen randomness helps (a) as a minority of layers in a trained transformer - Reservoir Transformers (ACL 2021): 22-27% wall-clock saving, better enwik8 BPC at most depths, with the authors stating the mixing must be learned; (b) as an adaptation device - VeRA (ICLR 2024) tunes Llama2-7B with 1.4M trainable parameters over shared frozen random matrices; (c) as a continual-learning readout - RanPAC (NeurIPS 2023, M=10,000, 20-62% error reduction) and its mushroom-body descendant Fly-CL (ICLR 2026), both image-only. The fixed operator that actually reached SOTA is derived, not random: S4's own ablation says 'random NPLR matrices still do not perform well... effectiveness primarily comes from the HiPPO initialization'.\n\nThe fly-specific payoff is ADDRESSING, not features. As a sparse key generator for a DeltaNet-class state - learned PN compression, frozen sparse binary PN->KC matrix, k-WTA, learned MBON readout, error-correcting delta writes - it costs m*K MACs/token (~9x cheaper than one d=768 dense projection at m=4096, K=16) and decouples capacity (width m) from cost (k). Measured here at equal compute: 0.82 vs 0.17 recall at 256 pairs, 0.70 vs 0.07 at 512. That places the claim squarely on the Zoology/BASED recall-versus-state-size frontier, the one regime where a falsifiable SOTA claim is available. Set K by the sqrt rule (~15-30 for d_PN=512), tune coding level f in [0.01,0.20] by participation ratio on real tokens, whiten before hashing, and put the novelty on the dopamine-gated write and the compartment timescales - because FlyVec already showed learned expansion weights beat random ones on text at every code length, and Product Key Memory already won at scale with learned keys."

### Established (with sources)

- TOTAL CAPACITY IS EXACTLY N. For a fading-memory dynamical system driven by i.i.d. input and read out linearly, the sum of normalised capacities over a complete orthonormal basis of functionals (linear memory + all nonlinear terms) is bounded by the number of observed state variables N, and is saturated for a suitable system. Dambre, Verstraeten, Schrauwen & Massar, 'Information Processing Capacity of Dynamical Systems', Scientific Reports 2:514 (2012). Consequence: linear memory and nonlinear computation are in strict zero-sum trade-off inside a reservoir; you cannot buy deep memory and rich nonlinearity from the same N units.
- LINEAR MEMORY CAPACITY MC <= N (Jaeger, GMD Report 152, 2002), approached only by near-linear, near-orthogonal, NON-NORMAL (delay-line-like) reservoirs as spectral radius -> 1; tanh reservoirs realise only a small fraction of N. Fisher-memory-curve formalism: White, Lee & Sompolinsky, PRL 92:148102 (2004); Ganguli, Huh & Sompolinsky, PNAS 105:18970 (2008).
- RANDOM FEATURES APPROXIMATE AT RATE O(1/sqrt(N)). Rahimi & Recht, 'Random Features for Large-Scale Kernel Machines', NIPS 2007; 'Weighted Sums of Random Kitchen Sinks', NIPS 2008. Halving the error costs 4x the features. This is the mathematical ceiling on every ELM / reservoir / frozen-projection readout.
- RANDOM FEATURES ARE PROVABLY WEAKER THAN LEARNED FEATURES. Yehudai & Shamir, 'On the Power and Limitations of Random Features for Understanding Neural Networks', NeurIPS 2019 (32:6598-6608): approximating a SINGLE ReLU neuron with random features requires exp(Omega(d)) features under natural distributions. Ghorbani, Mei, Misiakiewicz & Montanari, 'When Do Neural Networks Outperform Kernel Methods?', NeurIPS 2020 (33:14820-14830) and 'Linearized two-layers neural networks in high dimension' (Annals of Statistics 2021): kernel/random-feature methods need ~d^k samples and features to fit a degree-k polynomial that a trained network learns with ~d; the gap opens exactly when the target depends on a low-dimensional latent manifold, which is the case for language.
- LAZY vs FEATURE-LEARNING REGIME IS A REAL DICHOTOMY AND LLMs ARE TRAINED IN THE FEATURE-LEARNING ONE. Jacot, Gabriel & Hongler, 'Neural Tangent Kernel', NeurIPS 2018; Chizat, Oyallon & Bach, 'On Lazy Training in Differentiable Programming', NeurIPS 2019 (lazy/kernel behaviour is an artefact of a scaling choice, not of width); Yang & Hu, 'Tensor Programs IV: Feature Learning in Infinite-Width Neural Networks', ICML 2021 (muP): the standard parameterisation degenerates to a kernel at large width, muP preserves feature learning, and muP is what modern LLM scaling uses. A reservoir is the lazy regime by construction.
- THE FIXED OPERATOR THAT ACTUALLY SCALED IS PRINCIPLED, NOT RANDOM. S4 (Gu, Goel & Re, ICLR 2022, arXiv:2111.00396v3) states in its own ablation: 'random NPLR matrices still do not perform well, which validates that S4's effectiveness primarily comes from the HiPPO initialization, not the NPLR parameterization.' S4D (Gu, Gupta, Goel & Re, NeurIPS 2022, arXiv:2206.11893): freezing the state matrix A is comparable to training it on sCIFAR and BIDMC but substantially worse on Speech Commands, worst for the finite-window S4-LegT/S4-FouT variants. HiPPO (Gu, Dao, Ermon, Rudra & Re, NeurIPS 2020) derives the matrix as optimal online polynomial projection of the input history.
- FROZEN RANDOM LAYERS INSIDE A TRAINED TRANSFORMER GIVE A WALL-CLOCK WIN, NOT A QUALITY WIN, AND THE MIXING MUST BE LEARNED. 'Reservoir Transformers', Shen, Baevski, Morcos, Keutzer, Auli & Kiela, ACL 2021 long paper 2021.acl-long.331 (arXiv:2012.15045; rejected at ICLR 2021 as 5FRJWsiLRmA, accepted at ACL). Frozen ('reservoir') layers interleaved in the encoder: up to 22% wall-clock saving to best validation BLEU on IWSLT'14 de-en, up to 27% on a 24-layer WMT'16 en-de model; better enwik8 BPC at all tested decoder depths EXCEPT the 64-layer baseline; 2 frozen layers for IWSLT, 8 for enwik8, 4 for WMT and RoBERTa. Authors' own conclusion, verbatim: 'self-attention, or the mechanism to summarize context information, should be learned if present. Once the context features have been gathered, a random projection via a fixed FFN module appears to be beneficial.' The FFN Reservoir (no self-attention at all) was the best variant.
- RANDOM/FIXED KERNEL FEATURE MAPS ARE THE REASON LINEAR ATTENTION LOST, AND LEARNING THE MAP FIXES IT. Hedgehog (Zhang, Bhatia, Kumbong & Re, ICLR 2024, arXiv:2402.04347): prior linear attentions (Performer-style random features, elu+1, etc.) fail because they lose (i) low-entropy 'spiky' attention weights and (ii) monotonicity of the attention weight in q^T k. Replacing the fixed map with a trainable single-layer MLP with element-wise exp, distilled against softmax attention, recovers >99% of Transformer quality and gives SOTA-for-subquadratic WikiText-103 perplexity.
- RECALL IS BOUNDED BY RECURRENT STATE SIZE, NOT BY CLEVERNESS. Zoology (Arora, Eyuboglu, Timalsina, Johnson, Poli, Zou, Rudra & Re, arXiv:2312.04927, ICLR 2024) and BASED (Arora et al., ICML 2024, arXiv:2402.18668): any recurrent model depending causally on the input needs Omega(N) bits of state to solve MQAR; empirically recall rises monotonically with recurrent state bytes within an architecture class. At 1.3B params on the Pile associative-recall slice: Transformer++ 1.74 ppl (~6.5 MB state), BASED 1.87 ppl (~512 KB), Mamba 1.96 ppl (~256 KB). BASED uses a FIXED 2nd-order Taylor feature map phi(q)^T phi(k) = 1 + q^T k + (q^T k)^2/2 with feature dimension D = 1 + d' + d'^2, d' = 16-32.
- FLY HASHING IS REAL AND ITS ADVANTAGE IS AT SHORT CODES AND LOW COST. Dasgupta, Stevens & Navlakha, 'A neural algorithm for a fundamental computing problem', Science 358:793-796 (2017): ~50 PNs -> ~2,000 KCs (40x expansion), ~6 random PN inputs per KC, APL-style WTA keeps top ~5%. MNIST at hash length k=4: mAP 16.0% (dense-Gaussian LSH) vs 44.8% (fly); sparse binary projection ~20x cheaper to compute than dense Gaussian. Authors state multi-table/multi-probe/quantisation tricks were not ported (proof of concept). Novelty-detection variant: Dasgupta, Sheehan, Stevens & Navlakha, PNAS 115:13093 (2018).
- OPTIMAL EXPANSION IN-DEGREE AND CODING LEVEL ARE DERIVED QUANTITIES, NOT THE FLY'S CONSTANTS. Litwin-Kumar, Harris, Axel, Sompolinsky & Abbott, 'Optimal degrees of synaptic connectivity', Neuron 93:1153 (2017): the participation-ratio dimension of a random sparse expansion is maximised at an intermediate in-degree K, predicted K~7 for the fly (measured ~6-7 claws per KC out of ~50 glomeruli) and K~4 for cerebellar granule cells; the optimum grows roughly as sqrt(N_in). Xie, Muscinelli, Harris & Litwin-Kumar, eLife 12:e82914 (2023): the optimal coding level is task-dependent, below 0.1 for random categorisation but substantially denser for smooth low-dimensional targets. Muscinelli, Wagner & Litwin-Kumar, Nature Neuroscience 26 (2023): the efficient architecture is 'structured compression followed by random expansion'.
- ON TEXT, LEARNED EXPANSION WEIGHTS BEAT RANDOM ONES AT EVERY CODE LENGTH. FlyVec (Liang, Ryali, Hoover, Grinberg, Navlakha, Zaki & Krotov, ICLR 2021, arXiv:2101.06887): the random-projection expansion baseline (RandExp) lost to the Krotov-Hopfield-learned layer at every hash length; and the deployed architecture is a CONTRACTION (2*20,000 -> 400 KCs), not an expansion. Static word similarity (Spearman x100, K=400, hash ~50): MEN 56.6 (FlyVec) vs 69.5 (GloVe) vs 75.5 (word2vec); SimLex 21.0 vs 31.5 vs 41.7. FlyVec only wins at very short codes (k=4: MEN 34.0 vs 16.9-35.5 for binarised baselines).
- FROZEN RANDOM PROJECTIONS DO WORK AT 7B SCALE - FOR ADAPTATION, NOT PRETRAINING. VeRA (Kopiczko, Blankevoort & Asano, ICLR 2024, arXiv:2310.11454): a single pair of frozen random matrices A, B shared across all layers, with only per-layer scaling vectors d, b trained; 10x fewer trainable parameters than LoRA at comparable GLUE/E2E performance; Llama2-7B instruction tuning with 1.4M trainable parameters (vs ~14M LoRA at r=8). The stated mechanism is that pretrained features have low intrinsic dimensionality (cf. Li, Farkhoor, Liu & Yosinski, 'Measuring the Intrinsic Dimension of Objective Landscapes', ICLR 2018; Aghajanyan, Zettlemoyer & Gupta, ACL 2021).
- FROZEN RANDOM EXPANSION + RIDGE READOUT IS STATE OF THE ART FOR ONE NARROW THING: CLASS-INCREMENTAL CONTINUAL LEARNING ON FROZEN VISION FEATURES. RanPAC (McDonnell, Gong, Parvaneh, Abbasnejad & van den Hengel, NeurIPS 2023, arXiv:2307.02251): frozen random projection to M=10,000 with a nonlinearity, then decorrelated class-prototype ridge regression; 20-62% final-error reduction across seven class-incremental benchmarks with ViT-B/16 and no rehearsal; Split-CIFAR100 64% -> 75% vs nearest-class-mean; accuracy still rising with M up to ~15,000. Fly-CL (ICLR 2026, arXiv:2510.16877) is the explicit mushroom-body version: sparse random projection + top-k + streaming GCV ridge; 10-task CIFAR-100 76.5 vs RanPAC 76.4 vs SimpleCIL 70.8; ImageNet-R 78.8 vs 78.6 vs 71.4; 38-93% training-time reduction. FlyPrompt (ICLR 2026, arXiv:2602.01976) uses a 2x random expansion as an MoE router. All three are IMAGE-ONLY.
- NEXT-GENERATION RESERVOIR COMPUTING IS REAL AND IS RESTRICTED TO LOW-DIMENSIONAL DYNAMICAL SYSTEMS. Gauthier, Bollt, Griffith & Barbosa, 'Next generation reservoir computing', Nature Communications 12:5564 (2021): replace the random reservoir with an explicit nonlinear vector autoregression (polynomial features of a short delay embedding), requiring no random matrices, far fewer metaparameters, much shorter training data and training time on Lorenz-63-class benchmarks. A 2025 follow-up extends it to dynamical inference (Chaos 36(1):013115, per a third-party bibliography - UNCERTAIN, not verified at source).
- IN-HOUSE, ALREADY MEASURED IN THIS REPOSITORY: a frozen MaleCNS 211k-neuron echo-state language model reached 3.644 bpc versus 2.266 for a 6M-parameter transformer, and was statistically tied with a degree-preserving shuffle (3.629), a random graph (3.546) and even a bigram model (3.572); usable memory ~1 token (decay 0.3^lag). A MaleCNS-512 frozen leaky-tanh reservoir for byte-level span tagging gave F1 0.2753 +/- 0.068 (real) vs 0.2782 +/- 0.061 (shuffled) vs 0.2727 +/- 0.056 (byte-only, no recurrence). Source: docs/aif/LITERATURE.md, sections 'gap:Does a fixed connectome-derive' and 'lit:criticality'.
- IN-HOUSE CAPACITY RESULT (docs/phase3/MEMORY_CAPACITY.md, results/capacity_numpy.json): at EQUAL per-token compute (d_key = k active rows), one-shot recall of P stored pairs: at P=256, dense delta d_key=100 gives 0.17 while sparse delta 100-of-2048 gives 0.82; at P=512, 0.07 vs 0.70. Sparse Hebb decays much faster than sparse delta (0.84 -> 0.33 between P=64 and P=256 at 64-of-1024). This is the single strongest quantitative case for a fly-style expansion, and it is about ADDRESSING, not about features.

### Principles and their translation

| principle | biology / mathematics | translation to a sequence model | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| A reservoir's total computational budget is its state width N, and it is spent once. Expanding cheaply does not create capacity; it only re-allocates it. | Dambre et al. 2012: sum of normalised capacities over a complete functional basis <= N for any fading-memory system with linear readout. Jaeger 2002: linear MC <= N. The mushroom body's ~2,000 KCs per hemisphere are therefore a ~2,000-dimensional budget, not a 2,000x multiplier. | Never size a reservoir by parameter count; size it by STATE WIDTH and report capacity in the same units as the baseline's state (bytes of KV cache / recurrent state). A 100M-parameter dense reservoir has N ~ 10^4 units and hence IPC <= 10^4 scalars; a 12-layer d=768 transformer at 8k context carries ~1.5e8 scalars of exact KV state. This ~4-order-of-magnitude gap is the whole story of why reservoir LMs fail, and it is not fixable by better wiring. | established |
| Random expansion buys FLOPs, not capacity. Sparsity buys cost; width buys capacity; the WRITE RULE buys the difference between the two. | For a Hebbian outer-product memory with random k-of-m binary keys, the expected code overlap is k^2/m and the read SNR after P stores is ~ sqrt(m/P), INDEPENDENT of k to first order. So sparsity reduces per-token work (k rows touched instead of m) without changing capacity. An error-correcting delta write M += x (v - x^T M)^T lifts capacity toward the rank of the key set, i.e. toward m. Biologically the fly HAS the error-correcting rule: KC->MBON synapses are depressed by coincident dopamine (Hige, Aso, Modi, Rubin & Turner, Neuron 88:985, 2015; Cohn, Morantte & Ruta, Cell 163:1742, 2015), and MBON->DAN feedback closes a prediction-error loop (Eschbach et al., Nature Neuroscience 23:544, 2020; Felsenberg et al., Cell 178:60, 2019). This repo's own MB extraction counts 339 MBON->DAN feedback edges / 2,861 contacts per hemisphere (docs/CIRCUITS.md). | Build the KC layer as a sparse KEY GENERATOR for a DeltaNet-class fast-weight state, never as a feature backbone. Per-token cost m*K MACs (m=4096, K=16 -> 65,536 MACs, ~9x cheaper than one d=768 dense projection at 589,824 MACs); state m*d_v; capacity ~m associations rather than ~d_key. Measured in-house: 0.82 vs 0.17 recall at P=256 for equal compute. | promising |
| Fixed operators scale when they are DERIVED, not when they are random; and they scale as initialisations that are then trained. | HiPPO derives the state matrix as the optimal online projection of the input history onto an orthogonal polynomial basis under a measure; the LMU derives the same structure from a Pade approximant of a pure delay. S4's own ablation shows random NPLR matrices fail where HiPPO succeeds, isolating the derivation, not the fixedness, as the source of the gain. | If the fly motif is worth keeping, keep it as a STRUCTURED INITIALISATION with a stable reparameterisation (LRU: /lambda/ = exp(-exp(nu)), input gain sqrt(1-/lambda/^2), allowing negative/complex eigenvalues), and let training move it. Do not freeze anything you cannot derive an optimality statement for. Applies directly to the four MB compartment decay constants (4/16/64/256) already in the v0.2 spec: derive them as a geometric basis of timescales, then learn them. | established |
| The mixing/routing must be learned; the post-mixing nonlinear expansion may be frozen. | Reservoir Transformers (ACL 2021) found the FFN Reservoir (frozen random FFN, no attention) best, and stated that context-summarisation must be learned. Hedgehog (ICLR 2024) shows exactly which property a random feature map destroys: low-entropy spikiness and monotonicity in q^T k. In the fly this maps onto the fact that PN->KC sampling is not uniformly random (Zheng et al. 2022 detect glomerulus co-occurrence structure; a 2025 preprint reports projection bias up to 15x by input type) and that the MB's output routing (MBON compartments) is highly structured. | Permissible: freeze the PN->KC sparse binary matrix S and the k-WTA. Not permissible: freeze the PN compression (learn it) or the MBON readout (learn it), and do not expect a random feature map to substitute for attention. This is also the direct explanation of the repo's phase-1 H1d null: the PN->KC wiring is the one part that SHOULD be random-equivalent, so finding real == random there was a confirmation, not a failure. | established |
| Random features are competitive exactly where the residual function to be learned is already low-dimensional: adaptation, readout, retrieval, routing, novelty. They are not competitive where representation learning is the task: pretraining. | Intrinsic-dimension results (Li et al. ICLR 2018; Aghajanyan et al. ACL 2021) plus Rahimi-Recht O(1/sqrt(N)) plus the Ghorbani/Yehudai-Shamir separations. Biologically the fly does not learn its sensory front end within a lifetime either - it learns at KC->MBON only. | Three concrete, defensible placements inside a modern hybrid: (a) frozen sparse expansion + streaming ridge readout as a TEST-TIME continual-adaptation head over a frozen LM (the untested text analogue of RanPAC/Fly-CL); (b) FlyHash codes as cheap approximate keys/dedup for retrieval and data curation (fly Bloom filter, ~4 us/novelty query at 384-d, m=1200); (c) random expanded routing for MoE (FlyPrompt). None of these is a backbone claim, and all three are honest. | promising |
| Edge of chaos is a diagnostic, not an objective, and it is task-dependent. | Bertschinger & Natschlaeger, Neural Computation 16:1413 (2004) established the critical line for random threshold networks; Legenstein & Maass, Neural Networks 20:323 (2007) showed it is NOT a universal optimum for liquid-state machines; Cramer et al., Nature Communications 11:2853 (2020) showed criticality is optimal only for memory-heavy tasks and subcritical is better for simple ones; Zierenberg, Wilting & Priesemann, PRX 8:031018 (2018) showed homeostasis lands at a subcritical, input-dependent point (cortex measured at branching ratio m ~ 0.98). | Pin spectral radius at ~0.95-0.98 (not 1.0) after E/I mean-cancellation (Rajan & Abbott, PRL 97:188104, 2006), and report three pre-registered diagnostics - branching ratio, largest Lyapunov exponent under input drive (QR), and Dambre IPC decomposition - as CONTROLS that rule out a tuning confound, never as the hypothesis. This is the cheapest way to make a future null interpretable. | established |
| Optimal in-degree and coding level are computable from the data, so treat the fly's 7 and 5% as scaling laws, not constants. | Litwin-Kumar et al. 2017: K_opt maximises the participation ratio (tr C)^2 / tr C^2 of expansion-layer activity and grows ~sqrt(N_in); K~7 for ~50 glomeruli. Xie et al. eLife 2023: optimal coding level f is task-dependent, <0.1 for random categorisation, denser for smooth targets, and the result survives an online climbing-fibre-like rule. This repo's MaleCNS extraction measures KC fan-in 5.16 PN types (median 5) over 2,019 KCs with APL covering 100% of KCs. | Do not hard-code K=7 and f=0.05. For a d_PN=512 compression stage, the sqrt rule gives K ~ 15-30; tune K by maximising the participation ratio of KC activity on REAL token data, and sweep f in [0.01, 0.20] as a first-class hyperparameter with a pre-registered grid. Report the measured PR so the choice is auditable. | established |
| A sparse-addressed memory layer inside a transformer is already a scaled, successful architecture - and its keys are LEARNED. | Product Key Memory (Lample, Sablayrolles, Ranzato, Denoyer & Jegou, 'Large Memory Layers with Product Keys', NeurIPS 2019, arXiv:1907.05242): ~1M memory slots addressed by product-quantised LEARNED keys with top-k=32; a 12-layer transformer plus one memory layer outperformed a 24-layer baseline at roughly 2x inference speed (numbers RECALLED, not verified at source). Meta's 'Memory Layers at Scale' (arXiv:2412.09764, 2024/2025 - UNCERTAIN id) scales the idea to very large memory-parameter counts. Structurally this IS the mushroom body: compress, address sparsely, read a few slots, write. | The honest framing of a fly-style expansion is: PKM with (i) fixed sparse binary addressing instead of learned product keys, (ii) k-WTA instead of top-k on learned keys, (iii) delta/three-factor MODULATED writes AT TEST TIME instead of SGD writes at train time, (iv) several compartments with distinct decay constants. Only (iii) and (iv) are genuinely novel and genuinely fly-derived; (i) is the part FlyVec already showed loses to learned keys. Put the claim on (iii)+(iv). | promising |

### Numbers an engineer can use

- Dambre bound: total normalised information-processing capacity of an N-node fading-memory reservoir with linear readout = N exactly (Sci Rep 2:514, 2012). Linear MC <= N (Jaeger 2002). Reference implementations that compute it: github.com/kubota0130/ipc, github.com/nanotechdave/RCbench.
- State-size arithmetic for the pivot: a dense 100M-parameter reservoir has N = sqrt(1e8) = 10,000 units -> IPC <= 10,000 scalars. A 12-layer, d=768 transformer at 8,192 context holds 8192*768*2*12 ~ 1.51e8 scalars of exact KV state. Ratio ~1.5e4. (Arithmetic; the bound and the KV size are both established.)
- In-house frozen MaleCNS reservoir LM: 3.644 bpc (real 211k-neuron connectome) vs 3.629 (degree-preserving shuffle) vs 3.546 (random graph) vs 3.572 (bigram) vs 2.266 (6M-parameter transformer). Usable memory ~1 token (decay 0.3^lag). docs/aif/LITERATURE.md.
- In-house MaleCNS-512 reservoir span tagging, 5 seeds: F1 0.2753 +/- 0.068 (real) vs 0.2782 +/- 0.061 (shuffled) vs 0.2727 +/- 0.056 (byte-only). Paired real-minus-shuffled -0.0029 +/- 0.028.
- In-house compute-matched capacity (docs/phase3/MEMORY_CAPACITY.md, value dim 16, 5 trials, one-shot recall): P=128: dense delta d=100 0.40, sparse delta 100-of-2048 0.92. P=256: 0.17 vs 0.82. P=512: 0.07 vs 0.70. Sparse Hebb 64-of-1024: 0.84 (P=64) -> 0.33 (P=256) vs sparse delta 0.94 -> 0.75.
- In-house MQAR H1 at 8 KV pairs, T=64, 3 seeds: mb 0.9814 +/- 0.0029, mb_connectome 0.9772, mb_shuffled 0.9782, abl_learned_gate 0.9966, gdn_matched 0.9979 +/- 0.0016, deltanet_matched 1.0000 +/- 0.0000. Effect of MB vs gdn -5.6 seed-SD. NOTE: P=8 << d_key=65, i.e. entirely outside the regime where sparse addressing can pay (MEMORY_CAPACITY.md, reading 1).
- Sparse-key memory algebra (standard, derivable): expected overlap of two random k-of-m binary codes = k^2/m. Hebbian outer-product read SNR after P stores ~ sqrt(m/P), independent of k -> P <= m/SNR^2 (m=4096, SNR=4 -> 256 pairs). Delta-rule writes lift the ceiling toward m. m=2048, k=100 -> expected overlap 4.9 units against k=100.
- KC-stage FLOPs: m*K multiply-accumulates per token. m=4096, K=16 -> 65,536 MACs, versus 768x768 = 589,824 MACs for one dense projection: ~9x cheaper. FlyHash's own figure: sparse binary projection ~20x cheaper than dense Gaussian (Dasgupta 2017).
- FlyHash retrieval, MNIST at hash length k=4: mAP 16.0% (dense-Gaussian LSH) vs 44.8% (fly). Advantage concentrated at SHORT codes; third-party reimplementations report ~3x LSH on MNIST (tian-kun/Fly-LSH) or '30-50% improvement' (pantsing/flylsh).
- BioHash (Ryali, Hopfield, Grinberg & Krotov, ICML 2020), MNIST mAP@all: k=32 55.5% vs ITQ 43.6 vs LSH 26.2; k=16 54.9 vs 41.2 vs 20.3. CIFAR-10 mAP@1000: k=32 24.0 vs ITQ 20.6 vs LSH 18.1. Costs: codes need k*log2(m) bits, not k bits.
- FlyVec (ICLR 2021) static word similarity, Spearman x100: MEN 56.6 vs GloVe 69.5 vs word2vec 75.5; WS353 63.7 / 64.0 / 66.5; SimLex 21.0 / 31.5 / 41.7. Contextual WiC 57.7 vs BERT 61.2. Corpus OpenWebText ~6B tokens, K=400 KCs, w=11, 8-24 min/epoch on 3xV100 vs ~10 h for GloVe. Random-projection baseline (RandExp) lost at EVERY hash length.
- Reservoir Transformers (ACL 2021): up to 22% wall-clock saving to best validation BLEU on IWSLT'14; up to 27% on a 24-layer WMT'16 model; better enwik8 BPC at all decoder depths except 64 layers; frozen-layer counts 2 (IWSLT), 8 (enwik8), 4 (WMT16, RoBERTa). AUCC budgets: 4 h IWSLT, 20 h WMT, 30 h enwik8, 60 h RoBERTa pretraining.
- BASED (ICML 2024): 360M and 1.3-1.4B params on 10B/30B/50B Pile tokens; ~20% linear attention, ~20% sliding window (64-128 tokens), ~60% gated convolution. Pile AR-slice ppl at 1.3B: Transformer++ 1.74 (~6.5 MB state), BASED 1.87 (~512 KB), Mamba 1.96 (~256 KB). At 355M on the recall slice: Transformer 1.87, Mamba 2.21, pure linear attention 2.29, RWKV-v5 +0.1 worse, H3 +2.6 worse. Real recall tasks at 1.4B: BASED SWDE 37.71 / FDA 19.06 / SQUAD-completion 29.49 vs Attention 56-69 (SWDE) and 57-69 (FDA). Taylor feature dim D = 1 + d' + d'^2 with d' = 16-32.
- RanPAC (NeurIPS 2023): frozen random projection M=10,000; 20-62% final-error reduction over seven class-incremental benchmarks with ViT-B/16, no rehearsal; Split-CIFAR100 64% -> 75% vs nearest-class-mean; accuracy still improving up to M~15,000. Fly-CL (ICLR 2026): CIFAR-100 10-task 76.5 vs RanPAC 76.4 vs SimpleCIL 70.8; ImageNet-R 78.8 / 78.6 / 71.4; 38-93% post-feature training-time reduction. All image-only.
- VeRA (ICLR 2024): frozen shared random A, B; trainable params cut from 2*L*d_model*r to L*(d_model + r); ~10x fewer than LoRA; Llama2-7B instruction tuning at 1.4M trainable parameters vs ~14M LoRA at r=8; one third-party write-up reports 1.6M/2.4M VeRA vs 159.9M/250.3M LoRA at r=64 with all-linear targets.
- NG-RC feature blow-up for language: monomials of degree <= p over a delay embedding of k steps x d dims is C(kd+p, p). For d=768, k=2, p=2 this is 1 + 1536 + 1536*1537/2 = 1,181,953 features PER TOKEN. NG-RC is therefore unusable as an LM core; its published domain is 3-D chaotic systems (Lorenz-63), Nat Commun 12:5564 (2021).
- Mushroom-body anatomy measured from MaleCNS v1.0 in this repository (docs/CIRCUITS.md, left hemisphere): 120 PNs / 60 PN types, 2,019 KCs, 1 APL (GABA, covering 100% of KCs), 48 MBONs over 32 compartments, 158 PAM + 8 PPL1 DANs; KC fan-in 5.16 PN types (median 5); PN->KC 9,917 edges / 183,709 contacts; KC->MBON 17,931 edges; MBON->DAN feedback 339 edges / 2,861 contacts; KC->KC 56,963 edges. All checks against Caron 2013 and the MB literature PASS.
- Litwin-Kumar 2017 optimal in-degree: K~7 measured and predicted for ~50 glomeruli; K~4 for cerebellar granule cells; optimum grows ~sqrt(N_in). Scaling to a d_PN = 512 compression stage gives K ~ 15-30 rather than 7.
- Reference criticality settings from the sweep: pin spectral radius 0.95-0.98 after E/I mean cancellation (Rajan & Abbott 2006); cortical in-vivo branching ratio m ~ 0.98 with intrinsic timescales ~100-300 ms (Wilting & Priesemann; Zierenberg et al. PRX 8:031018, 2018).

### Dead ends

- A frozen reservoir as the BACKBONE of a language model. Measured twice in this repository: 211k-neuron MaleCNS ESN 3.644 bpc vs 2.266 for a 6M transformer and tied with shuffle/random-graph/bigram; 512-neuron MaleCNS reservoir tagging F1 0.2753 vs 0.2727 byte-only. Root cause is the Dambre/Jaeger capacity bound, not the wiring. Do not re-run this in any form.
- Whole-connectome reservoirs generally. The only published-adjacent Drosophila examples are unpublished repos: 'wetware' (larval connectome, 2,952 neurons, ~94% MNIST, no null model, no spectral-radius sweep) and an optic-lobe FlyWire reservoir with no reported numbers. The rigorous null-model study (cqw-acq/connectome-null-models, spectral radius pinned 0.95, n=15) found CIFAR-10 43.04 (connectome) vs 42.95 (degree-preserving), +0.09 pp, 95% CI [-0.20, +0.38], p=0.51; and MNIST 97.30 vs 97.28 vs 97.53 (Erdos-Renyi best). It also documents the winner's curse: +0.35 pp at n=5 (p=0.078) evaporated at the pre-committed n=15.
- Next-generation reservoir computing (Gauthier 2021) for language. The NVAR feature count is C(kd+p, p) = ~1.18M for d=768, k=2, p=2. It is a genuinely better method than ESNs for 3-D chaotic attractors and has no path to high-dimensional token streams. Also reported (UNCERTAIN, 'Catch-22s of reservoir computing', Zhang & Cornelius, Phys. Rev. Research 5:033213, 2023): RC and NG-RC are highly sensitive to warm-up and fail to generalise across attractor basins.
- Random feature attention as a softmax replacement at scale (Performer/FAVOR+, Choromanski et al. ICLR 2021, arXiv:2009.14794; RFA, Peng et al. ICLR 2021, arXiv:2103.02143). Superseded and diagnosed: Hedgehog (ICLR 2024) identifies the failure as loss of spiky low-entropy weights and loss of monotonicity in q^T k, and fixes it by LEARNING the feature map. Every 2024-2026 competitive linear-attention model uses a learned or derived map (Hedgehog MLP, BASED Taylor + sliding window, GLA/DeltaNet/Gated DeltaNet learned gates), not random features.
- Random projection of raw token one-hots as an embedding. FlyVec's own RandExp baseline lost to the learned layer at every hash length, and FlyVec itself abandoned expansion for a contraction (40,000 -> 400). The fly-style expansion must sit on top of a LEARNED compression (Muscinelli 2023: 'structured compression followed by random expansion').
- Extreme hard-sparsity k-WTA over LM hidden states for retrieval at scale. The hd-instrument preregistered sweep (Pythia-2.8B states, sparse fan-in K=5, expansions 8x-4096x) got fly-LSH top-1 0.997 at M=10k adversarially similar keys (55x over raw cosine 0.018), but at M=100k 'whitening / random hash / fly-LSH all HARD_FAIL'. The same project argues biological expansions converge on f = 0.05-0.10 and that their f=0.001 was 50-100x too sparse. Evidence is weak (unreviewed repo) but the direction matches Babadi & Sompolinsky (Neuron 2014): expansion improves discriminability up to a point and then degrades it, with relative noise in sparse tags growing as sqrt(2 ln(1/f)) while decorrelation gains grow only logarithmically.
- Binary / threshold-unit reservoirs and spiking liquid-state machines as the memory substrate. Buesing, Schrauwen & Legenstein, Neural Computation 22:1272 (2010): binary reservoirs show ABRUPT memory loss at the edge of chaos and extreme sensitivity to connectivity degree, where analog tanh units degrade smoothly. Use rate/analog units.
- Treating the edge of chaos as the objective. Legenstein & Maass, Neural Networks 20:323 (2007): the critical line predicts LSM performance for some tasks and not others. Cramer et al., Nat Commun 11:2853 (2020): criticality is optimal only for memory-heavy computation; simpler tasks are solved better subcritically. Phase 2's Lyapunov-regulated gain toward -0.02 was therefore optimising a diagnostic, not a task.
- Expansion-then-sparsify inside a local-learning predictive network. One unreviewed 2026 project (brunofitas/tsl) measured 1.048 NRMSE with the DG/mushroom-body expansion motif versus 0.778 for the base model on multi-task consolidation - i.e. the motif HURT. Weak evidence, but it is the only direct test of the motif in a predictive-coding setting and it is negative.
- Deep reservoirs (DeepESN; Gallicchio, Micheli & Pedrelli, Neurocomputing 268:87-99, 2017) as an LM route. They do develop a hierarchy of timescales and beat shallow ESNs on time-series tasks, but there is no language-modelling result at any scale, and the Dambre bound applies layerwise.
- Expecting the fly-derived component to win on perplexity per FLOP. No biological architecture has ever beaten a well-tuned transformer or a modern gated linear RNN on that axis, and the theory (Ghorbani/Yehudai-Shamir/muP) says a frozen component should not. The repository's phase-3 reflection already states this; reservoir evidence confirms it.

### Open questions

- Is there ANY published language model above 100M parameters with a frozen random recurrent core? I could not find one, and the absence is itself informative: searches across GitHub mirrors of arXiv/ACL/ICLR text returned only the ACL 2021 Reservoir Transformers (partial freezing of a trained transformer) and unpublished connectome-reservoir repos. Treat 'no competitive reservoir LM exists at 100M+' as the working conclusion, with the caveat that my web access was restricted to GitHub this session, so a 2025-2026 result could have been missed.
- Does the in-house sparse-delta capacity advantage (0.82 vs 0.17 at P=256, equal compute) survive LEARNED keys, learned gates, and a trained transformer backbone at 20M-125M parameters? The repository's H1b is exactly this experiment and it is the single highest-value thing to run. Pre-register the regime: P must exceed d_key of the compute-matched Gated-DeltaNet twin by at least 4x, or the test is void (phase-1 H1 ran at P=8 vs d_key=65 and was therefore uninformative about the hypothesis).
- Does RanPAC/Fly-CL (frozen sparse expansion + streaming decorrelated ridge readout) transfer from frozen vision features to frozen LM hidden states for streaming/continual text adaptation? Completely untested. This is the cleanest available 'defined regime' for a genuine first: a text-domain RanPAC with a fly-derived sparse projection and top-k, evaluated on a continual-domain-adaptation stream with forgetting curves against LoRA and against full fine-tuning.
- What is the optimal coding level f for sparse KEYS over LM residual streams? Xie et al. (eLife 2023) prove it is task-dependent (<0.1 for random categorisation, denser for smooth targets); nobody has measured it for text. Measure it by sweeping f in [0.01, 0.20] and reporting the participation ratio of the KC code on real token data alongside recall.
- Is the 'rank-1 trap' real for kWTA expansions of anisotropic LM states? One unreviewed source cites a Babadi-Sompolinsky-derived bound S <= sqrt(d'/r_eff) implying that if the input has a dominant direction, the expansion cannot separate beyond it. If true, a whitening/decorrelation step BEFORE the expansion is mandatory, and its omission may explain several negative results. Needs derivation from Babadi & Sompolinsky, Neuron 83:1213 (2014) at source.
- Can the fly's compartment structure (this repo: 32 MBON compartments, 158 PAM + 8 PPL1 DANs per hemisphere) be shown to beat a single learned decay in a test-time-learning memory? Compartments with distinct decay constants are the least-explored fly-derived idea, and unlike sparse addressing there is no ML prior art that already does it better. This is the most defensible novelty in the whole reservoir/memory branch.
- Does a DERIVED fixed operator in the HiPPO/LMU family, combined with a fly-style sparse address, beat either alone? The LMU LM claim (Chilkuri, Hunsberger, Voelker, Malik & Eliasmith, arXiv:2110.02402, 'Language Modeling using LMUs: 10x Better Data Efficiency or Improved Scaling Compared to Transformers' - same accuracy as transformers with 10x fewer tokens over three orders of magnitude, and self-attention complements it) is an arXiv preprint I could not verify at source and I am not aware of an independent replication at 100M+ scale. If it replicates, the 'principled fixed operator' branch is much stronger than the random one.
- What is the compute penalty for a FIXED random expansion at matched training compute for an LM? Nobody has fit the sparse scaling law with an exogenous mask. Nearest proxies: learned 75% sparsity only matches a dense model with ~2-2.2x the non-zero parameters (Frantar 2024); static masks lag RigL by ~5-7 pp at equal FLOPs; one-shot 2:4 masks give 2-3x worse perplexity than learned masks. Budget >= 2-5x tokens for any frozen-structure arm or expect it to lose at matched compute.
- Is the correct baseline for a 'fly memory layer' actually Product Key Memory / Memory Layers rather than Gated DeltaNet? PKM (NeurIPS 2019) is the same architecture with learned product keys and train-time writes, and it was a clear win at scale. A fly-derived variant must beat PKM-style learned addressing to claim anything about sparse addressing per se; it can only realistically claim the test-time, dopamine-modulated, multi-compartment write. I verified PKM exists and its arXiv id (1907.05242); its exact perplexity numbers here are RECALLED and must be checked at source before use.
- Are FlyHash's retrieval gains reproducible under the correct ground-truth metric? One reimplementation notes results diverge from Science Fig. 3 depending on whether mAP ground truth uses Euclidean or Hamming distance, and the original authors state multi-table/multi-probe/quantisation tricks were not ported. Any FlyHash-based claim must fix the ground-truth metric in the pre-registration.

### Relevance to language models

"BLUNT VERDICT ON RESERVOIRS AT 100M+ PARAMETERS: there is no competitive reservoir language model, there is no documented one above ~10M parameters, and the reason is a theorem rather than an engineering gap. Dambre et al. (2012) fix the total information-processing capacity of an N-node fading-memory system at exactly N, and Jaeger's MC <= N caps the linear part; a 100M-parameter dense reservoir has N ~ 10,000 units against a 12-layer d=768 transformer's ~1.5e8 scalars of exact KV state at 8k context. That gap of ~4 orders of magnitude is why this repository's own 211k-neuron MaleCNS echo-state LM reached 3.644 bpc while a 6M-parameter transformer reached 2.266, and why it tied with a bigram model (3.572). The theory side agrees independently: Yehudai & Shamir (NeurIPS 2019) and Ghorbani/Mei/Misiakiewicz/Montanari (NeurIPS 2020) show random features need exponentially or polynomially-in-d more units than learned features on exactly the low-dimensional-manifold targets that language presents; the lazy/kernel regime (Jacot 2018; Chizat 2019) is what a reservoir is, and muP (Yang & Hu 2021) is the parameterisation modern LLMs use precisely to STAY OUT of it.\n\nBEST DOCUMENTED RESULTS, RANKED. (1) The only frozen-randomness that has ever helped a transformer at a real benchmark is PARTIAL: Reservoir Transformers (ACL 2021) freeze 2-8 layers inside an otherwise trained model and buy 22-27% wall-clock to best validation BLEU plus better enwik8 BPC at most depths; the authors' own conclusion is that the mixing must be learned and only the post-mixing FFN may be frozen. (2) Frozen random projections are genuinely state-of-the-art for ADAPTATION and READOUT: VeRA (ICLR 2024) fine-tunes Llama2-7B with 1.4M trainable parameters over shared frozen random matrices; RanPAC (NeurIPS 2023) and its explicit mushroom-body descendant Fly-CL (ICLR 2026) win class-incremental continual learning with a frozen M=10,000 random projection plus ridge readout - but both are image-only. (3) The fixed operator that actually scaled into SOTA is HiPPO, and S4's own ablation says the gain comes from the derivation, not the fixedness: 'random NPLR matrices still do not perform well'.\n\nWHAT A FLY-STYLE EXPANSION BUYS INSIDE A MODERN HYBRID: addressing, not computing. Used as a sparse KEY generator for a DeltaNet-class fast-weight state - PN compression learned, PN->KC sparse binary matrix frozen, k-WTA, MBON readout learned, DELTA (error-correcting) writes - it costs m*K MACs per token (~9x cheaper than one dense d=768 projection at m=4096, K=16) and decouples capacity (set by state width m) from cost (set by k). This repository has already measured the payoff at equal compute: 0.82 vs 0.17 one-shot recall at 256 stored pairs, 0.70 vs 0.07 at 512. That is the one honest, quantified, fly-derived advantage in this whole branch, and it sits exactly on the axis the field is currently competing on - the Zoology/BASED recall-versus-state-size Pareto frontier, where at 1.3B a Transformer gets 1.74 AR-slice perplexity with ~6.5 MB of state and BASED gets 1.87 with ~512 KB. A defensible, pre-registerable SOTA claim exists in that regime and nowhere else: 'at equal recurrent-state bytes and equal per-token FLOPs, a sparse-addressed, dopamine-gated, multi-compartment delta memory achieves higher recall than Gated DeltaNet / Titans / BASED.' Note carefully that phase-1 H1 tested this at 8 KV pairs against d_key=65 - far below the crossover - so it did not test the hypothesis at all.\n\nWHERE THE EVIDENCE IS THIN. No fly-style expansion has ever been evaluated inside an autoregressive LM above 100M parameters. Every continual-learning win for random expansion (RanPAC, Fly-CL, FlyPrompt) is on frozen vision features. The LMU language-modelling claim (10x data efficiency) is an unreplicated arXiv preprint. Product Key Memory - the scaled architecture that most resembles the mushroom body - uses LEARNED keys, and FlyVec showed learned expansion weights beat random ones at every code length on text, so the frozen-randomness part of the fly story is the part most likely to lose. Put the claim on the write rule and the compartment timescales, not on the randomness."


## Local / backprop-free learning rules for deep sequence models: the measured gap, the compute cost, and the one place (fast weights) where locality is already state of the art

*Key: `local-learning-rules`.*

### Summary

"The brief asked where local, backprop-free learning stands for deep sequence models, and the honest answer is sharply bimodal.\n\nFor slow weights the evidence is unambiguous and negative. Nobody has trained a backprop-free language model above roughly 100M parameters. The only predictive-coding transformer LM reaches perplexity 175.90 against backprop's 162.64, and only after replacing its Gaussian output energy with a categorical one (standard PC gives 590). Incremental PC beats backprop on a MASKED objective (106.19 vs 120.02) and loses on the CAUSAL one (142.54 vs 113.32) -- and causal is the objective that matters. Forward-Forward's only language experiment was retracted by Hinton as a bug; purely Hebbian deep stacks cap at 27.3% ImageNet top-1 (~49 pp below backprop); target propagation and feedback alignment were shown not to scale in 2018 and still have not; NoProp needs the label at every layer and has no sequence formulation. Compute makes it worse: even a 20x-optimised PC implementation costs 1.7-3x backprop per epoch on an A100. And a 2026 theory result says that in the stable width/depth scaling limit PC's parameterisations and gradients coincide with backprop's -- the regime where PC differs is the unstable one. Removing backprop from the backbone buys nothing.\n\nFor fast weights the picture inverts, and this is the finding the next design should be built on. The state-of-the-art in-context memory rule is ALREADY local: Gated DeltaNet writes S_t = alpha_t S_{t-1}(I - beta_t k_t k_t^T) + beta_t v_t k_t^T, which is a three-factor Widrow-Hoff rule with synaptic decay -- functionally the fly's dopamine-gated KC->MBON synapse. Linear attention provably performs implicit local gradient descent in context. So locality is not a handicap to be defended there; it is the medium. The open design space is which local rule, and the fly supplies four cheap, untested variants: sparse k-of-n addressing (this repository measured 0.82 vs 0.17 one-shot recall at 256 pairs at matched per-token compute), inferred precision as the write rate (matches a change-point Kalman oracle without knowing the regime), timing-dependent signed writes, and recall-gated consolidation into slow weights, whose theory predicts a learnable timescale growing linearly rather than logarithmically with repetitions and which no language model implements. Finally, eligibility-trace rules are O(1) in sequence length where BPTT is O(T) -- the one advantage that is structural, not merely biological."

### Established (with sources)

- No backpropagation-free language model exists above ~100M parameters as of September 2026. The largest direct local-rule LM results are: a BERT-style conditional LM trained with predictive coding (Pinchetti, Salvatori et al., 'Predictive Coding beyond Gaussian Distributions', NeurIPS 2022, arXiv:2211.03481) and an incremental-PC LM on One Billion Word (Salvatori et al., iPC, arXiv:2212.00720). Both are far below 100M parameters. Treat any claim otherwise as unverified.
- The measured PC-vs-BP gap on an autoregressive objective is large and in BP's favour. Pinchetti et al. (NeurIPS 2022, arXiv:2211.03481): BERT-style conditional LM test perplexity BP 162.64+/-0.76, standard Gaussian PC 590.08+/-12.60, PC with a forward-KL (categorical) output energy 175.90+/-1.74. Standard Gaussian PC cannot handle softmax/attention layers at all; a non-Gaussian output energy is mandatory.
- Local rules can WIN on a masked objective and LOSE on the causal one. Salvatori et al. (iPC, arXiv:2212.00720), One Billion Word, 10 seeds: masked LM perplexity iPC 106.19+/-10.54 (10/10 seeds converged) vs BP 120.02+/-13.19 (7/10) vs standard PC 523.08+/-12.3 (3/10); conditional (causal) LM iPC 142.54+/-4.23 (7/10) vs BP 113.32+/-0.36 (10/10) vs PC 206.34+/-6.46. The autoregressive objective is where local PC loses.
- Predictive coding is provably NOT a different inductive bias at scale. 'On the Infinite Width and Depth Limits of Predictive Coding Networks' (arXiv:2602.07697, from the repository's prior sweep; not independently verified; the primary source could not be reached when this note was written): for linear residual nets the width/depth-stable feature-learning parameterisations of PC are exactly BP's, and the equilibrated PC energy converges to the BP loss when width >> depth. 'To maintain stability at scale, PC must behave more like BP.'
- Forward-Forward's only sequence/language experiment was retracted by its own author. Hinton, 'The Forward-Forward Algorithm: Some Preliminary Investigations', arXiv:2212.13345 (verified from the paper text): the next-character-from-a-ten-character-window result reported in an early draft could not be replicated and is attributed to a bug; separating positive and negative phases 'only works if the learning rate is very low and the momentum is extremely high', which Hinton calls 'probably the most important outstanding question about FF as a biological model'.
- Forward-Forward matches BP only on MNIST-class data. Hinton 2022 (verified): permutation-invariant MNIST 1.4% test error (4x2000 ReLU, 100 epochs -> 1.37%); supervised FF 1.36% after 60 epochs; local receptive fields 1.16%; with 25-shift jitter and 500 epochs 0.64%; recurrent 'video' FF 1.31%. On CIFAR-10 with 2-3 hidden layers of 3072 ReLUs and 11x11 local receptive fields, FF is 'only slightly worse' than BP and the gap does not grow with depth, but 'backpropagation reduces the training error much more quickly'.
- Forward-Forward does not survive contact with real images at scale. Layer-local learning audit (arXiv:2606.06539, 2026; from the repository's prior sweep, not independently verified): under identical backbone and recipe, BP with deep supervision beats the best FF variant by 2.40 pp (CIFAR-10) and 5.93 pp (CIFAR-100); at 224x224 resolution FF reaches 49.4% vs typical BP >75%; and FF's O(1)-in-depth activation memory produced NO measured hardware advantage over BP plus gradient accumulation on commodity hardware.
- Pure Hebbian deep learning is roughly 50 percentage points below BP on ImageNet. SoftHebb (Journe, Rodriguez, Guo, Moraitis, ICLR 2023, arXiv:2209.11883; abstract verified from github.com/NeuromorphicComputing/SoftHebb): with up to five hidden layers plus a linear classifier, no feedback, targets or error signals, accuracies are MNIST 99.4%, CIFAR-10 80.3%, STL-10 76.2%, ImageNet 27.3% top-1. BP-trained ResNet-50 on ImageNet is ~76%.
- Target propagation and feedback alignment were shown not to scale a decade ago and the result still stands. Bartunov, Santoro, Richards, Marris, Hinton, Lillicrap, NeurIPS 2018, arXiv:1807.04587 (abstract verified): 'for CIFAR and ImageNet we find that TP and FA variants perform significantly worse than BP, especially for networks composed of locally connected units.'
- Removing weight TRANSPORT scales; removing the backward PASS does not. Sign-symmetry feedback (Xiao, Chen, Liao, Poggio, ICLR 2019, OpenReview SygvZ209F7 / arXiv:1811.03567) trains ResNets on ImageNet and MS COCO close to BP, whereas plain feedback alignment does not; weight mirrors (Akrout et al., NeurIPS 2019, arXiv:1904.05391) close most of the remaining ImageNet gap (exact residual gap not re-verified this session). These methods still perform a full backward pass through a second network, so they are 'weight-transport-free', not 'backprop-free'.
- Local CONTRASTIVE learning is the strongest layer-local family and it works on sequences. Greedy InfoMax (Loewe, O'Connor, Veeling, NeurIPS 2019, arXiv:1905.11786; paper text verified): three gradient-isolated ResNet-50 modules trained with module-local InfoNCE reach 81.9% on STL-10 (79.8% when modules are frozen and trained strictly one after another), BEATING end-to-end CPC and end-to-end supervised training on that dataset, and cutting GPU memory by 2.8x. On LibriSpeech-100h phone classification (41 classes) GIM gets 62.5% vs end-to-end CPC 64.9% and supervised 77.7% -- i.e. locality costs only 2.4 pp against its own end-to-end counterpart, while the contrastive objective itself costs ~13 pp against supervised.
- A fully space- and time-local contrastive rule exists and its cost is measurable. CLAPP (Illing, Ventura, Bellec, Gerstner, NeurIPS 2021, arXiv:2010.08262; paper text verified): on LibriSpeech phoneme classification layer-wise training costs only 0.4% (layer-wise GIM) and 1.1% (CLAPP-s); replacing BPTT in the GRU layer with e-prop costs a further 3.1%; omitting the recurrent layer entirely costs 9.3%. On video (UCF-101-scale, 101 classes) layer-wise GIM and CLAPP-s beat their end-to-end counterparts, but fully-local CLAPP needs many more negative samples and converges prohibitively slowly.
- e-prop is a principled truncation of RTRL, not an approximation of BP through space. Bellec, Scherr, Subramoney, Hajek, Salaj, Legenstein, Maass, Nature Communications 11:3625 (2020), doi:10.1038/s41467-020-17236-y: dE/dW_ji = sum_t L_j^t e_ji^t, with a purely local eligibility trace e and a top-down learning signal L. It keeps only the per-neuron (block-diagonal) part of the RTRL Jacobian, so memory is O(#synapses) and independent of sequence length T, versus BPTT's O(T x #neurons). Its rate-based twin is RFLO (Murray, eLife 2019;8:e43299).
- The state-of-the-art in-context memory mechanism is ALREADY a local three-factor rule. DeltaNet (Schlag, Irie, Schmidhuber, ICML 2021, arXiv:2102.11174) and Gated DeltaNet (Yang, Kautz, Hatamizadeh, ICLR 2025, arXiv:2412.06464) update S_t = alpha_t S_{t-1}(I - beta_t k_t k_t^T) + beta_t v_t k_t^T. Every term is local: presynaptic key, postsynaptic error (v_t - S_{t-1}k_t), a scalar write gate beta_t and a decay alpha_t. This is the Widrow-Hoff delta rule with synaptic decay -- exactly the functional form of the fly's dopamine-gated KC->MBON rule.
- Linear attention performs implicit local gradient descent on an in-context regression problem. von Oswald et al., 'Transformers learn in-context by gradient descent', ICML 2023, arXiv:2212.07677, and 'Uncovering mesa-optimization algorithms in Transformers', arXiv:2309.05858. So in-context learning is mathematically an error-correcting local rule; there is no separate 'local learning' to add there, only a choice of which local rule.
- Titans/ATLAS/MIRAS are meta-learned local plasticity rules. Titans (Behrouz, Zhong, Mirrokni, arXiv:2501.00663): M_t = (1-alpha_t)M_{t-1} + S_t with S_t = eta_t S_{t-1} - theta_t grad_l(M_{t-1}; k_t, v_t), i.e. surprise-gated write, momentum on surprise, weight-decay forgetting. MIRAS ('It's All Connected', arXiv:2504.13173) formalises the design space as four choices: memory architecture, attentional bias, retention gate, learning algorithm. Backprop learns the rule's parameters offline; the rule itself runs locally at test time.
- The fly's own plasticity rule has been written down as a local three-factor equation and validated against many experiments. Gkanias, McCurdy, Nitabach, Webb, eLife 2022;11:e75611: dW = delta(t)[k(t) + W - w_rest] with w_rest = 1, using only presynaptic/local information, produces depression, potentiation, recovery and saturation, and reproduces 92 intervention experiments with r = 0.76 (p = 2.2e-18), including extinction, reversal, second-order and unpaired conditioning, and correctly predicts the ABSENCE of blocking.
- The fly's dopamine sign depends on relative timing, not just coincidence. Handler et al., Cell 178:60-75 (2019): forward vs backward odour/dopamine pairing flips the sign of plasticity via Dop1R1 (cAMP) vs Dop1R2 (ER Ca2+). Aso & Rubin, eLife 2016;5:e16135: a single DAN type can write aversive memory, reduce aversive memory, or write appetitive memory depending on timing relative to odour; the 20 DAN types have cell-type-specific rules for training requirement, decay, capacity and flexibility.
- Recall-gated consolidation is a derived, measured, and ML-untested local mechanism. Lindsey & Litwin-Kumar, eLife 2024;13:e90793: LTM synapses update only when short-term-memory recall exceeds a threshold; in the fly the gamma1 MBON (STM) depression disinhibits DANs of LTM compartments. With N = 2000 synapses (1000 STM / 1000 LTM), learning rates 0.25 (STM) / 0.05 (LTM) and reliable-memory probability 0.25, the learnable timescale grows LINEARLY with repetitions instead of logarithmically for simple synapses, and it predicts inverted-U spacing effects.
- Soft winner-take-all Hebbian learning is provably Bayesian. Moraitis, Toichkin, Journe, Chua, Guo, Neuromorphic Computing and Engineering 2:044017 (2022), doi:10.1088/2634-4386/aca710: WTA networks built from standard deep-learning elements with a derived Hebbian rule maintain a Bayesian generative model of the data and, without supervision, minimise cross-entropy. The key is a SOFT winner, not a hard one. This gives the mushroom-body APL/k-WTA motif a principled, backprop-free objective.
- Predictive coding's measured advantage is specific to batch size 1 and online/continual settings. Song, Millidge, Salvatori, Lukasiewicz, Xu, Bogacz, Nature Neuroscience 27:348-358 (2024), 'prospective configuration': infer activities first, then change weights; outperforms BP in online learning with batch size 1 and reduces catastrophic interference, and matches neural/behavioural data. No large-scale (ImageNet/LM) advantage has been reported.
- NoProp is a supervised-classification algorithm with no sequence result. Li, Teh, Pascanu, 'NoProp: Training Neural Networks without Back-propagation or Forward-propagation', arXiv:2503.24322 (2025; ID verified): each layer independently learns to denoise a noised label embedding z_t = sqrt(alpha_t) z_{t-1} + sqrt(1-alpha_t) eps_t toward z_0 = u_y. It requires the LABEL at every layer, so it needs a fixed, low-cardinality target embedding. Evaluated only on MNIST/CIFAR-10/CIFAR-100. No language-model, sequence or >100M-parameter result exists.
- The compute cost of iterative local rules on GPUs is a genuine 1.7x-3x penalty, not a wash.
