PCX (Pinchetti et al., ICLR 2025, arXiv:2407.01163): with a 20x-optimised JAX implementation, PC costs 5.5 s/epoch vs BP 3.2 s on CIFAR-100 VGG-5 (A100) and 29 s vs 10 s for AlexNet (161M parameters). PC approaches BP speed only when inference steps ~ depth.
- Exact-BP-reproducing PC variants provide no computational advantage. Zahid, Guo, Fountas, arXiv:2304.02658: Z-IL and fixed-prediction variants need contrived update scheduling, are lower-bounded in time complexity by BP, and forfeit the variational-Bayes interpretation.
- Equilibrium propagation is the only local energy-based rule with a claimed full-ImageNet result. Scellier & Bengio, Frontiers in Computational Neuroscience 11:24 (2017) established the framework (the free-phase/nudged-phase gradient estimate); Laborieux et al. (Frontiers 2021) added symmetric nudging to remove bias; Laborieux & Zenke (NeurIPS 2022, arXiv:2209.00530) gave holomorphic EP. Kerjan et al., arXiv:2606.03584 (2026; from the repository's prior sweep, NOT independently verified) report a 10-layer conv PCN (VGG10) trained with centred EP reaching 13.23% top-5 error vs 12.2% for BP on full ImageNet.
- The fundamental scaling law against global-scalar three-factor rules is a variance law. Werfel, Xie & Seung (NIPS 2003; Neural Computation 17:2699-2718, 2005) showed that node perturbation converges roughly N times slower than BP, where N is the number of units, and weight perturbation roughly N_w times slower (number of weights). Any rule whose only error signal is a scalar broadcast (REINFORCE/node-perturbation family, which is what a pure dopamine-modulated Hebbian rule is) inherits this variance penalty. Three-factor rule theory: Fremaux & Gerstner, Front. Neural Circuits 9:85 (2016); Gerstner, Lehmann, Liakoni, Corneil, Brea, Front. Neural Circuits 12:53 (2018).
- Contrastive objectives have an information ceiling. InfoNCE lower-bounds mutual information by at most log(number of negatives) (Poole, Ozair, van den Oord, Alemi, Tucker, ICML 2019, arXiv:1905.06922). This caps how much signal a local contrastive loss can deliver per update and explains CLAPP's observed need for more negatives on video.
- This repository's own numpy studies already confirm the two local-rule facts that matter most. docs/phase3/MEMORY_CAPACITY.md: at equal per-token compute (d_key = k active rows), a sparse 100-of-2048 delta-rule memory recalls 0.82 at P = 256 stored pairs where a dense d_key=100 delta memory recalls 0.17; the error-correcting delta write beats the pure Hebbian write (0.75 vs 0.33 at P = 256, 64-of-1024). docs/phase3/ADAPTIVE_RATE.md: a surprise-gated rate beta = sigmoid(log(pi ||e||^2/n)) matches or beats the best FIXED rate in both noise regimes and is on a par with a change-point Kalman oracle -- but the margin over a TUNED fixed rate is only a few points.

### Principles and their translation

| principle | biology / mathematics | translation to a sequence model | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Split the model into an 'evolution' timescale trained by backpropagation and a 'lifetime' timescale run by a local rule; never try to replace backpropagation in the slow weights. | The fly genome (~13,000 genes) is the compressed outer-loop product of evolution; within a lifetime no global error signal is transported across the brain and all fast learning at KC->MBON is a product of presynaptic activity, an eligibility term and dopamine (Aso & Rubin, eLife 2016;5:e16135; Gkanias et al., eLife 2022;11:e75611). Mathematically, the outer loop is meta-learning the parameters of an inner local rule. | Backpropagation pretrains the backbone and the PARAMETERS of the plasticity rule (key/value/gate/decay projections); at test time the memory is written by a local rule only. This is exactly what Titans (arXiv:2501.00663), ATLAS (arXiv:2505.23735) and MIRAS (arXiv:2504.13173) already are, and what differentiable plasticity (Miconi, Stanley, Clune, ICML 2018, arXiv:1804.02464) and Backpropamine (Miconi et al., ICLR 2019, arXiv:2002.10585) pioneered. The project's contribution is the FORM of the inner rule, not the removal of BP. | established |
| The Widrow-Hoff delta rule with decay IS the biologically local rule, and it is already state of the art for the in-context/fast-weight part. | dW = beta * (v - Wk) k^T uses only the presynaptic key, the postsynaptic read-out error and a scalar gate -- the three factors of a dopamine-gated synapse. Linear attention provably performs one step of gradient descent on an in-context least-squares loss (von Oswald et al., ICML 2023, arXiv:2212.07677). | Do not propose 'adding local learning' to the memory: it is already there. Gated DeltaNet's S_t = alpha_t S_{t-1}(I - beta_t k_t k_t^T) + beta_t v_t k_t^T is a three-factor rule with synaptic decay. The open design space is WHICH local rule (addressing, gate, decay, consolidation), evaluated at matched compute against Gated DeltaNet / Titans twins. | established |
| Locality pays for credit assignment through TIME on an unbounded stream, because there the alternative (BPTT) is not merely biologically implausible but computationally impossible. | e-prop is the block-diagonal truncation of RTRL: dE/dW_ji = sum_t L_j^t e_ji^t with local eligibility traces (Bellec et al., Nat. Commun. 11:3625, 2020); memory is O(#synapses), independent of sequence length, versus BPTT's O(T x #neurons). Rate version: RFLO (Murray, eLife 2019;8:e43299). | For any model that must keep learning across a stream longer than any feasible BPTT window (continual pretraining, lifelong context, agent memory), a local temporal rule is the only option, and its measured cost is small: replacing BPTT with e-prop inside CLAPP's recurrent layer cost only 3.1% on LibriSpeech phone classification (arXiv:2010.08262). This is the cleanest 'advantage not handicap' regime. | promising |
| Sparse, near-orthogonal addressing -- not the local rule itself -- is where the mushroom body earns its compute. | Random k-of-n codes with k^2/n expected overlap give near-orthogonal addresses (Kanerva, Sparse Distributed Memory, MIT Press 1988; Dasgupta, Stevens, Navlakha, Science 358:793, 2017); the intermediate optimal in-degree K~7 maximises the participation ratio of KC covariance (Litwin-Kumar, Harris, Axel, Sompolinsky, Abbott, Neuron 93:1153, 2017). | At equal per-token compute (d_key active rows either way), the sparse memory stores far more one-shot bindings: this repository's own measurement gives 0.82 vs 0.17 recall at 256 pairs (docs/phase3/MEMORY_CAPACITY.md). The trade is a 16-20x larger memory STATE. This is a real, compute-matched, falsifiable claim about a regime (P >> d_key) that phase-1 H1 never entered (8 pairs vs 65). | promising |
| Soft winner-take-all plus a Hebbian rule is approximate Bayesian inference, which gives the sparse addressing layer a principled objective without backpropagation. | Moraitis et al., Neuromorphic Computing and Engineering 2:044017 (2022): a soft-WTA network with the derived Hebbian rule maintains a Bayesian generative model and minimises cross-entropy without supervision. The fly's APL-mediated k-WTA over Kenyon cells is the biological instance. | The PN->KC projection (the memory's addressing function) can be trained by a local Hebbian/anti-Hebbian rule with a soft winner, which is provably doing online mixture-model inference, while the rest of the model is trained by BP. Krotov-Hopfield/FlyVec/BioHash are the existing text instances. Use SOFT WTA during learning and hard WTA at inference; hard-WTA Hebbian hashing is known to plateau with code length. | promising |
| The inferred learning rate (precision) is the only piece of active inference with algorithmic bite, and it buys robustness rather than raw performance. | Dopamine/octopamine set how much a surprise changes a synapse; a stable world should lower the rate, a changed world raise it. Mathematically this is a change-point/Kalman gain: beta = sigma_prior^2 / (sigma_prior^2 + sigma_obs^2). | beta_t = sigmoid(a log(pi_t //e_t//^2 / n) + b): this repository measured it at or above the best FIXED rate in both noise regimes without knowing the regime, and on a par with a change-point Kalman oracle (docs/phase3/ADAPTIVE_RATE.md) -- but only a few points above a TUNED fixed rate, and phase-1 H1b showed a LEARNED gate is at least as good. Claim tuning-freedom and non-stationarity robustness, not accuracy. | established |
| Recall-gated consolidation from fast state into slow weights changes the ASYMPTOTICS of what is learnable, and no language model implements it. | Lindsey & Litwin-Kumar, eLife 2024;13:e90793: gating LTM writes on STM recall makes the learnable timescale grow linearly with repetitions instead of logarithmically, because the gate raises the signal-to-noise ratio of each consolidation event. In the fly the gamma1 MBON's depression disinhibits LTM-compartment DANs; sleep-dependent consolidation has a measured 7-10 h reactivation window (Dag et al., eLife 2019;8:e42786). | A two-stage memory: a fast local (delta/sparse) store, plus a slow store written only when a fast-store read succeeds above threshold, optionally offline ('sleep') by replay. This is a continual-learning claim testable at 20M parameters against a Gated-DeltaNet twin, and it is the single least-explored, most biologically-grounded mechanism in this brief. | promising |
| Layer-local contrastive objectives, not Hebbian or Forward-Forward objectives, are the best-performing purely layer-local family, and their cost is dominated by the OBJECTIVE, not by the locality. | Module-local InfoNCE maximises a lower bound on I(z_t^{m-1}, z_t^m) between a module's input and output subject to temporal disparity, which prevents the degenerate solutions that kill greedy supervised stacking (Loewe et al., arXiv:1905.11786). CLAPP (arXiv:2010.08262) makes it local in time as well by replacing negative-sample replay with a Hebbian/anti-Hebbian sign flip at 'saccades'. | If a layer-local objective is ever used, use a predictive-contrastive one. Evidence: on LibriSpeech phone classification, going layer-wise costs 0.4-1.1%, while the contrastive objective itself costs ~13 pp against supervised end-to-end. Locality is cheap; the self-supervised proxy objective is expensive. For an LM, next-token prediction is already the objective, so the interesting variant is a per-block auxiliary next-token or multi-horizon predictive loss (deep supervision), which BP-DeepSup already dominates FF on. | established |
| Tiering matters: 'backprop-free' is four different claims with four different scaling records. | Tier A removes weight TRANSPORT only (feedback alignment, sign symmetry, weight mirrors) and keeps a backward pass. Tier B removes the backward pass but broadcasts a global error (DFA, node perturbation). Tier C removes the global error entirely (FF, Hebbian, layer-local contrastive). Tier D replaces the backward pass with iterative energy relaxation (PC, EP). | Scaling record: Tier A reaches ImageNet/COCO near BP (Xiao et al., ICLR 2019; Akrout et al., NeurIPS 2019). Tier B degrades with task difficulty and fails on convnets (Bartunov et al., NeurIPS 2018; Refinetti et al., ICML 2021, 'Align, then memorise'), with variance ~ #units (Werfel/Seung). Tier C caps at ImageNet 27.3% (SoftHebb) / 49.4% (FF at 224x224). Tier D matches BP up to ImageNet VGG10 at 1.7-3x cost. Any proposal must state which tier it is in. | established |
| The fly's write is depression-dominant, compartment-specific and sign-flipping with timing -- a richer local rule than any used in ML. | Handler et al., Cell 178:60-75 (2019): forward vs backward pairing flips the plasticity sign via Dop1R1 (cAMP) vs Dop1R2 (ER Ca2+). Aso & Rubin (eLife 2016;5:e16135): one DAN type can write, erase or reverse a memory depending on timing; the ~20 DAN types have distinct decay dynamics, storage capacity and flexibility. Gkanias et al. (eLife 2022;11:e75611) fit this with dW = delta(t)[k(t) + W - w_rest]. | Per-compartment decay rates (the repository used 4/16/64/256) are the multi-timescale memory already present in Nested Learning/HOPE (arXiv:2512.24695). The genuinely untested pieces are (i) a SIGNED write whose sign depends on the temporal order of the key and the modulator, and (ii) a saturating write dW ~ delta * (k + W - w_rest) that bounds synapses without an explicit clip. Both are one-line changes to a Gated-DeltaNet write and are cheap to test. | speculative |

### Numbers an engineer can use

- PC-trained transformer LM (BERT-style conditional), test perplexity: BP 162.64+/-0.76; PC with forward-KL categorical output energy 175.90+/-1.74 (+8.2% relative); standard Gaussian PC 590.08+/-12.60 (3.6x worse). Source: Pinchetti, Salvatori et al., NeurIPS 2022, arXiv:2211.03481.
- iPC vs BP on One Billion Word, 10 seeds. Masked LM perplexity: iPC 106.19+/-10.54 (10/10 converged), BP 120.02+/-13.19 (7/10), PC 523.08+/-12.3 (3/10). Causal LM perplexity: BP 113.32+/-0.36 (10/10), iPC 142.54+/-4.23 (7/10), PC 206.34+/-6.46 (10/10). Source: Salvatori et al., arXiv:2212.00720.
- tPC-RTRL (temporal predictive coding + approximate RTRL), 15M-parameter RG-LRU recurrent model, En-Fr CCMatrix translation: test perplexity 7.62 vs BPTT 7.49 (+1.7% relative). Source: arXiv:2602.18131 (2026), from the repository's prior sweep; not independently verified; the primary source could not be reached when this note was written. This is the single best backprop-free sequence-modelling number known to this project, and it is at 15M parameters.
- Compute cost of PC vs BP on an A100 with a 20x-optimised JAX implementation (PCX, ICLR 2025, arXiv:2407.01163): CIFAR-100 VGG-5 5.5 s/epoch (PC) vs 3.2 s (BP) = 1.72x; AlexNet (161M parameters) 29 s vs 10 s = 2.9x. PC approaches BP wall-clock only when inference steps ~ network depth. Legacy PC implementation: 110 s/epoch (34x BP).
- Inference-step budgets for deep PC: muPC uses inference steps = number of hidden layers (arXiv:2505.13124, NeurIPS 2025, 128-layer residual MLPs on MNIST/FMNIST/CIFAR-10); PC-ALM uses T = 2L and raises gradient cosine-similarity to BP from 0.604 to 0.909 (Fashion-MNIST, width 32, depth 32, ReLU: BP 78.66%, PC 68.13%, PC-ALM 77.75%). PC-ALM source: arXiv:2605.31022 (2026), repository sweep, not independently verified.
- Forward-Forward, MNIST test error (verified from arXiv:2212.13345): 1.4% permutation-invariant; 1.37% with 4 hidden layers of 2000 ReLUs after 100 epochs; 1.36% supervised FF after 60 epochs; 1.16% with local receptive fields; 0.64% with 25-shift jitter over 500 epochs; 1.31% for the recurrent 'static image as video' variant (8 iterations, goodness averaged over iterations 3-5).
- Forward-Forward on real images at scale: BP with deep supervision beats the best FF variant by 2.40 pp (CIFAR-10) and 5.93 pp (CIFAR-100) under an identical backbone and recipe; at 224x224 FF reaches 49.4% vs typical BP >75%. FF's O(1)-in-depth activation memory gave no measured advantage over BP + gradient accumulation. Source: arXiv:2606.06539 (2026), repository sweep, not independently verified.
- SoftHebb (purely Hebbian, soft-WTA, no feedback/targets/errors, up to 5 hidden layers + linear classifier): MNIST 99.4%, CIFAR-10 80.3%, STL-10 76.2%, ImageNet 27.3% top-1. ImageNet reference points: BP ResNet-50 ~76% top-1. Gap ~49 pp. Source: Journe, Rodriguez, Guo, Moraitis, ICLR 2023, arXiv:2209.11883 (abstract verified via github.com/NeuromorphicComputing/SoftHebb).
- Greedy InfoMax (3 gradient-isolated ResNet-50 modules, module-local InfoNCE), STL-10 linear-probe accuracy: 81.9% trained in sync, 79.8% trained strictly one module at a time (frozen predecessors); this BEATS end-to-end CPC and end-to-end supervised on STL-10. GPU memory reduced 2.8x by training modules asynchronously. LibriSpeech-100h, 41 phone classes: GIM 62.5%, end-to-end CPC 64.9%, greedy supervised 73.4%, end-to-end supervised 77.7%. Source: Loewe, O'Connor, Veeling, NeurIPS 2019, arXiv:1905.11786 (paper text verified).
- CLAPP locality costs on LibriSpeech phoneme classification: layer-wise (vs whole-module) training costs 0.4% for layer-wise GIM and 1.1% for CLAPP-s; replacing BPTT in the GRU with e-prop costs a further 3.1%; removing the recurrent layer entirely costs 9.3%. Source: Illing, Ventura, Bellec, Gerstner, NeurIPS 2021, arXiv:2010.08262 (paper text verified).
- Equilibrium propagation, first full-ImageNet PCN result: 10-layer conv net (VGG10) with centred EP, 13.23% top-5 error vs 12.2% for BP (+1.03 pp). Source: Kerjan et al., arXiv:2606.03584 (2026), repository sweep, NOT independently verified.
- Sparse vs dense one-shot memory at EQUAL per-token compute (this repository, docs/phase3/MEMORY_CAPACITY.md, value dim 16, 5 trials, one-shot recall accuracy). At P=256 stored pairs: dense delta d_key=100 -> 0.17; sparse delta 100-of-2048 -> 0.82. At P=512: 0.07 vs 0.70. At P=256, 64-active-row configurations: dense delta d_key=64 -> 0.11; sparse Hebb 64-of-1024 -> 0.33; sparse delta 64-of-1024 -> 0.75. The sparse memory's state is 16-20x larger; per-token compute is matched because a read/write touches k rows either way.
- Surprise-gated vs fixed learning rates on a re-binding stream (this repository, docs/phase3/ADAPTIVE_RATE.md, 64-of-1024 sparse delta memory, 48 stored pairs, change rate 0.004/pair/step). At noise sigma=0.3: best fixed rate 1.0 gives recall accuracy 0.80; surprise-gated per-pair 0.81; change-point Kalman oracle 0.81. At sigma=1.0: best fixed 0.5 gives 0.67; surprise-gated per-pair 0.71; oracle 0.67. A single global surprise tracker loses <=0.02 against per-pair trackers.
- Recall-gated consolidation parameters from the fly-derived theory: N = 2000 synapses split 1000 STM / 1000 LTM, learning rates 0.25 (STM) and 0.05 (LTM), reliable-memory probability 0.25; learnable timescale grows LINEARLY with repetitions vs LOGARITHMICALLY for simple synapses. Source: Lindsey & Litwin-Kumar, eLife 2024;13:e90793.
- Fly consolidation timing: sleep-dependent courtship LTM requires ventral fan-shaped-body-driven DAN-aSP13 reactivation 7-10 h after training; sleep deprivation in the 7-9 h or 8-10 h window abolishes LTM (suppression index 30-40% -> ~0), while deprivation outside the window has little effect. Source: Dag et al., eLife 2019;8:e42786.
- Fly plasticity rule fit quality: dW = delta(t)[k(t) + W - w_rest], w_rest = 1, reproduces 92 intervention experiments with r = 0.76, p = 2.2e-18. Source: Gkanias, McCurdy, Nitabach, Webb, eLife 2022;11:e75611.
- Mushroom-body sparse-code parameters (for sizing a memory): ~2,000 Kenyon cells per hemisphere, ~5% coding level (so k ~ 100), in-degree K ~ 6-7 claws out of ~50 glomeruli, ~158 DANs across 16-20 compartments, 22 typical + 14 atypical MBON types. Sources: Li et al., eLife 2020;9:e62576; Litwin-Kumar et al., Neuron 93:1153 (2017).
- Backpropamine (differentiable neuromodulated Hebbian plasticity with retroactive eligibility traces), Penn Treebank: neuromodulated plastic LSTM test perplexity 61.44 vs 62.48 for a standard LSTM with fewer total parameters (~1.7 perplexity improvement, p = 1e-7). Multi-million-parameter model; no follow-up at LLM scale. Source: Miconi et al., ICLR 2019, arXiv:2002.10585.
- Node-perturbation variance law: convergence is ~N times slower than BP where N = number of units; weight perturbation ~N_w times slower where N_w = number of weights. Source: Werfel, Xie & Seung, NIPS 2003 / Neural Computation 17:2699-2718 (2005). Numeric factor cited from recollection; verify before publishing.
- InfoNCE mutual-information ceiling: the InfoNCE bound is at most log(number of negative samples) nats. Source: Poole, Ozair, van den Oord, Alemi, Tucker, ICML 2019, arXiv:1905.06922. Practical consequence observed in CLAPP: video needs far more negatives than STL-10 or LibriSpeech, giving prohibitive convergence times under strict temporal locality.
- Neuromorphic substrate reference point (not a learning-rule result): the Shiu et al. 2024 whole-brain fly LIF model (127,400 neurons, 52.8M synapses) runs at ~4.4 s CPU per simulated second; the Sandia Loihi-2 port on 12 chips runs at 12-54 ms per simulated second (~80-370x). Source: philshiu/Drosophila_brain_model; arXiv:2508.16792 (repository sweep, not independently verified).

### Dead ends

- Replacing backpropagation in the BACKBONE (slow weights) of a language model. Every measured comparison at LM scale favours BP on the autoregressive objective (iPC 142.54 vs BP 113.32 perplexity, arXiv:2212.00720; PC transformer 590 or 176 vs BP 163, arXiv:2211.03481), and the theory says the regime where PC differs from BP is the unstable one (arXiv:2602.07697). This is the repository's Phase-3 axiom A1 restated with numbers: backpropagation plays the role of evolution and there is nothing to gain by removing it.
- Forward-Forward for sequences. Hinton's own paper (arXiv:2212.13345) retracts the only next-character experiment as a bug and states that separating the positive and negative phases works only at very low learning rate and very high momentum. There is no FF sequence or language result from any group. FF's claimed memory advantage was also measured to be zero against BP + gradient accumulation (arXiv:2606.06539).
- NoProp for language modelling. It requires the target label embedding at every layer (z_0 = u_y), so it presumes a small fixed label set; over a 32k-token vocabulary with a different target at every position there is no published formulation, no experiment and no obvious one. Evaluated only on MNIST/CIFAR-10/CIFAR-100 (arXiv:2503.24322).
- Pure Hebbian (two-factor) deep stacks as a backbone. 27.3% ImageNet top-1 with five layers is the ceiling of the best-tuned instance (SoftHebb, arXiv:2209.11883), ~49 pp below BP. Hard-WTA Hebbian hashing also plateaus with code length on word-similarity tasks.
- Target propagation and plain feedback alignment. Shown not to scale to CIFAR/ImageNet, especially for locally connected architectures (Bartunov et al., NeurIPS 2018, arXiv:1807.04587); DFA additionally fails on convolutional networks for a now-understood reason (Refinetti et al., ICML 2021, 'Align, then memorise'). Eight years of follow-up has not overturned this.
- Exact-BP-equivalent local variants (Z-IL, fixed-prediction PC). They need contrived update scheduling, their time complexity is lower-bounded by BP's, and they discard the variational interpretation -- so they buy neither speed nor a different bias (Zahid, Guo, Fountas, arXiv:2304.02658).
- 'Locality saves memory' as a standalone motivation on GPUs. Greedy InfoMax's 2.8x GPU-memory reduction (arXiv:1905.11786) is real but is matched by ordinary gradient checkpointing/accumulation, and the 2026 FF audit measured no hardware advantage at all. Do not build a claim on activation memory.
- Adding a fixed biological gating rule to an end-to-end backprop-trained model. This project has already measured it twice: phase-1 H1 found a learned gate beats the error gate, and phase-2 AIF-H1 found K free-energy inference steps worth only 0.026 bits/char (0.7 seed-SD). A learned gate with the same inputs contains the fixed rule as a special case. Any local rule proposed for the slow path must be justified by a regime where the learned gate cannot be trained (streaming, no backward pass available), not by biology.
- Iterative inference on top of amortised inference as a source of LM quality. The amortisation gap closes with training; prospective configuration's advantage is documented specifically at batch size 1 and vanishes outside online/small-data conditions (Song et al., Nature Neuroscience 27:348-358, 2024).
- Equilibrium propagation as a near-term engineering route without analog hardware. EP needs two (or three, for centred/symmetric variants) relaxation phases to equilibrium per example; its single ImageNet-scale result is a 10-layer conv net still ~1 pp behind BP; and its raison d'etre is analog/physical substrates that this project does not have.
- Spiking networks as the model (as distinct from as a substrate). The 7B/76B 'spiking LLMs' (SpikingBrain, arXiv:2509.05276; SpikeLLM, arXiv:2407.04752) are trained by backpropagation with surrogate gradients or converted from quantised ANNs -- they are NOT evidence that a local rule scales. Conflating them would be a citation error.
- Contrastive objectives as a replacement for next-token prediction in an LM. InfoNCE is capped at log(#negatives) nats (Poole et al., ICML 2019), and in GIM's own audio experiment the contrastive objective cost ~13 pp against supervised while the locality cost only 2.4 pp -- the objective, not the locality, is the expensive part. Next-token prediction is already both a strong objective and a layer-local one when applied per block.
- Reservoir computing as a backbone for language. Noted here only because the owner listed it: the repository's phase-1 H5 already found the whole-graph connectome mask indistinguishable from an Erdos-Renyi graph, which is the reservoir hypothesis in its strongest form, and it was null. The fly's expansion is an ADDRESS SPACE for a memory, not a computational backbone.

### Open questions

- Does a sparse-addressed, locally-written memory beat a compute-matched dense one INSIDE a trained language model, with learned keys and gates? The repository's numpy capacity result (0.82 vs 0.17 at 256 pairs) is with random keys and no learning. Phase-1 H1 at 8 pairs was inside a dense memory's capacity and found the opposite ordering. The decisive experiment is a 20M-parameter model at P >> d_key against a Gated-DeltaNet twin at matched per-token FLOPs, pre-registered at 2 seed-SD with a six-seed replication.
- Does recall-gated consolidation into slow weights help a language model? No LM implements it. The eLife 2024 theory predicts the learnable timescale grows linearly rather than logarithmically with repetitions; the LM translation is retention of facts seen a few times, far beyond the context window, without catastrophic forgetting. This is the most under-explored item in this brief and the one most likely to support a defensible novel claim.
- Is there any local rule that is competitive for the SLOW weights of an autoregressive model at any scale? The best data point is tPC-RTRL at 15M parameters (7.62 vs 7.49 perplexity, arXiv:2602.18131, unverified). Nobody has run it at 100M+, nobody has run it on a decoder-only model, and the byte-level WikiText-103 numbers in that paper were not retrievable.
- Does e-prop's O(1)-in-T memory turn into a real advantage for continual pretraining of an LM over a stream longer than any BPTT window? CLAPP measured only a 3.1% cost for swapping BPTT for e-prop in one GRU layer on speech; nobody has measured the equivalent for a modern linear-attention or SSM recurrence on text.
- Does a timing-dependent SIGNED write (Handler et al., Cell 2019: forward vs backward pairing flips the sign via Dop1R1/Dop1R2) do anything a gated delta rule cannot? This is a one-line change to a Gated-DeltaNet write and has, as far as this sweep found, never been tested in an ML memory.
- Does the saturating fly rule dW = delta(t)[k(t) + W - w_rest] (Gkanias et al., eLife 2022) behave better than dW = beta(v - Wk)k^T under long streams? It bounds synapses without an explicit clip and predicts the absence of blocking (no cross-key error), which may be a feature or a bug for in-context recall.
- Does a SOFT-WTA Hebbian PN->KC layer (provably Bayesian, Moraitis et al. 2022) beat a fixed random sparse projection or a BP-trained projection as the ADDRESSING function of an LM memory, at matched compute? Phase 1 tested fixed real wiring vs fixed random wiring and found null; it did not test a locally LEARNED sparse projection.
- Is precision-weighted (variance-normalised) surprise better than Titans' raw gradient-norm surprise as a write gate? The repository's adaptive-rate study says yes by a few points against fixed rates, but the head-to-head against a learned gate inside a trained LM at capacity is the open question.
- What is the actual compute cost of a local rule in a SEQUENCE setting? All published PC/EP cost accounting is per-epoch on images. For a sequence model the relevant quantity is inference steps x tokens x depth, and nobody has reported it.
- Would a meta-learned local rule (outer loop BP over the rule's parameters, inner loop local application) discovered on a fly-like memory substrate outperform hand-designed rules? Jiang & Litwin-Kumar (PLOS Comput Biol 2021) meta-learned heterogeneous DAN rules for the fly; MIRAS/ATLAS do the same for LMs. Nobody has combined the two.
- Do the arXiv 2026 results cited here (arXiv:2602.18131 tPC-RTRL, arXiv:2602.07697 PC infinite-width/depth, arXiv:2605.31022 PC-ALM, arXiv:2606.03584 EP-ImageNet, arXiv:2606.06539 FF audit) actually say what the repository's prior sweep recorded? They could not be re-verified when this note was written because the primary sources could not be reached. Verify before any of them enters a paper.

### Relevance to language models

"Three claims, ranked by how much a language-model designer should act on them.\n\n(1) For the SLOW weights, local learning is a handicap and the case is closed for this project's purposes. The best local-rule autoregressive LM result is 142.54 perplexity vs backprop's 113.32 on One Billion Word (iPC, arXiv:2212.00720); the best predictive-coding transformer is 175.90 vs 162.64 and only after abandoning the Gaussian energy (arXiv:2211.03481); the only sequence result that nearly matches BPTT is at 15M parameters (tPC-RTRL, unverified). There is no backprop-free LM above ~100M parameters anywhere, and the theory says that in the stable scaling limit predictive coding's gradients ARE backprop's gradients (arXiv:2602.07697). Forward-Forward's one language experiment was retracted by Hinton himself. Replacing backprop in the backbone therefore has no upside and a documented 10-25% perplexity downside plus a 1.7-3x wall-clock penalty.\n\n(2) For the FAST weights, local learning is not merely competitive -- it is already the state of the art, and this reframes the project. Gated DeltaNet's update S_t = alpha_t S_{t-1}(I - beta_t k_t k_t^T) + beta_t v_t k_t^T is a three-factor rule (presynaptic key, postsynaptic error, scalar gate) with synaptic decay; Titans adds momentum on surprise and weight-decay forgetting; linear attention provably performs implicit local gradient descent on an in-context regression (von Oswald et al., ICML 2023). The fly's dopamine-gated KC->MBON rule is the same functional form. So the question is not 'can a local rule work in an LM' -- it already does -- but 'which local rule', and that question is wide open. The fly's specific answers (sparse k-WTA addressing, per-compartment decay, timing-dependent sign, saturating write, recall-gated consolidation) are cheap one-line variants on an existing SOTA update, testable at 20M parameters on one GPU against a twin.\n\n(3) The one structural advantage that no engineering trick can take away is temporal: BPTT is O(T) in memory and local eligibility-trace rules are O(1) (e-prop, Bellec et al. 2020). For any LM that must keep learning across a stream longer than a feasible BPTT window -- continual pretraining, lifelong agent memory, unbounded context -- a local temporal rule is not the plausible option, it is the only option, and its measured quality cost is small (3.1% on speech, arXiv:2010.08262). That is where a fly-aligned design can claim a regime rather than a metric."


## Spiking and event-driven language models: no quality-SOTA regime, one narrow (and still extrapolated) batch-1 edge-decode regime, and the wrong abstraction for the fly

*Key: `spiking-lms`.*

### Summary

Spiking is the wrong substrate for this project, and - this is the load-bearing finding - it is also the wrong abstraction for the fly. Recommend confirming the phase-3 reflection's provisional rejection, but for sharper reasons than it gives, and recommend keeping one ingredient.

QUALITY. No spiking LM has ever beaten a parameter-matched ANN on any language benchmark. SpikeGPT-216M (arXiv:2302.13939), the only from-scratch generative spiking LM, reports WikiText-103 ppl 39.75 - worse than GPT-2 Small (117M) evaluated zero-shot at 37.50 (Radford 2019). SpikeLM trails BERT-base by ~6.7 points average on GLUE and 21.2 on CoLA. Every spiking model at or above 1B parameters is a conversion, quantization or distillation of a backprop-trained dense network: SpikeLLM (ICLR 2025) quantizes LLaMA-2; SpikingBrain-7B/76B (arXiv:2509.05276, TMLR 2026) continual-pretrains ~150B tokens from a Qwen2.5-7B checkpoint; SpikingMamba-1.3B distills Mamba. The cause is mechanical: BPTT over T timesteps multiplies activation memory by T, and no scalable alternative has been shown at pretraining scale.

EFFICIENCY. The claimed multipliers (32x, 27x, 87x) are 45nm operation counts using E_AC 0.9 pJ vs E_MAC 4.6 pJ (Horowitz 2014). The break-even is T*s < 5.1 on ARITHMETIC ONLY, and batch-1 decode is memory-bound - a model reads all its weights per token whatever the activation sparsity. The one clean measured neuromorphic comparison yields 2x energy-delay product. The best 2026 result (Richter et al., MatMul-free LM on Loihi 2: 224 tok/s at 75 mJ/token) states in its own footnotes that the sparse row is extrapolated, and at long prefill an H100 overtakes it.

BIOLOGY. Fly photoreceptors, lamina monopolar cells and many antennal-lobe local interneurons emit no action potentials; Drosophila central neurons are electrotonically compact (PN input resistance 598 MOhm; Gouwens & Wilson 2009) so graded signals cross the brain unregenerated. Where fly neurons spike for memory they emit 0-2 spikes in ~10% of cells - a k-WTA sparse code, not a temporal one.

WHAT TO KEEP. Strip the spikes from every 2025-26 spiking LLM and one mechanism remains, converged on by three independent groups: a gated linear recurrent backbone with THRESHOLD-SPARSIFIED ACTIVATIONS (SpikingBrain 69.15%; Richter et al. 69.5% at 0.64 points cost; EGRU 80-85% at LSTM-equal PTB perplexity with a matched control). A LIF neuron is provably a 1-D diagonal SSM plus a threshold and reset, so spiking adds only a leak you already have and a 1-bit quantizer you do not want. Adopt the sparsity; reject the spikes. Watch the one open risk: the quality cost of sparsification grew from 0.64 to 2.09 points going 370M to 2.7B.

### Established (with sources)

- QUALITY GAP, FROM-SCRATCH. SpikeGPT (Zhu, Zhao, Li & Eshraghian, arXiv:2302.13939; listed as TMLR 2024 by a curated third-party index) is still the only generative spiking LM trained from scratch. Reported 216M-parameter results: WikiText-103 test ppl 39.75, WikiText-2 ppl 18.01. GPT-2 Small (117M) evaluated ZERO-SHOT reaches 37.50 on WikiText-103 and 29.41 on WikiText-2, GPT-2 Medium (345M) 26.37 / 22.76 (Radford et al. 2019, 'Language Models are Unsupervised Multitask Learners', Table 3). So a 216M spiking LM trained ON WikiText-103 is worse than a 117M dense LM that never saw it. UNCERTAIN: the SpikeGPT figures come from two independent third-party summaries on GitHub (ayush-kumar-prog/snn-thesis-research, 8hrsk/kstudy), not from the paper itself (arxiv.org is egress-blocked in this session); one of those tables mislabels which GPT-2 number belongs to which dataset, and I corrected it against Radford et al.
- EVERY SPIKING LM AT OR ABOVE 1B PARAMETERS IS A CONVERSION, QUANTIZATION OR DISTILLATION OF A BACKPROP-TRAINED DENSE MODEL, NOT A SPIKING MODEL TRAINED FROM SCRATCH. SpikeLLM (Xing et al., ICLR 2025, arXiv:2407.04752, repo Xingrun-Xing2/SpikeLLM verified) quantizes LLaMA-2 7B-70B with generalized integrate-and-fire neurons; SpikingBrain-7B/76B (Pan, Feng, Zhuang et al., arXiv:2509.05276, accepted TMLR 2026, repo BICLab/SpikingBrain-7B verified) is continual-pretrained FROM A Qwen2.5-7B CHECKPOINT on ~150B tokens (100B @ 8K, then 20-30B each @ 32K and 128K); SpikingMamba-1.3B (Huang, Tang, Wang et al., TMLR 2026, OpenReview uxb2jcCLxt, repo HuuYuLong/SpikingMamba verified) distills from a Mamba teacher; SpikeBERT (Lv et al., arXiv:2308.15122, Neural Networks) and SpikingBERT (Bal & Sengupta, AAAI 2024, arXiv:2308.10873) distill BERT.
- THE FIELD'S OWN FLAGSHIP ADMITS ITS SPIKING IS NOT EVENT-DRIVEN. The SpikingBrain-7B repository README states it uses 'pseudo-spiking, where activations are approximated as spike-like signals at the tensor level, rather than true asynchronous event-driven spiking', yielding 'over 69% sparsity at the micro level' (69.15% via an adaptive threshold V_th(x) = (1/k)*mean(|x|)). Its headline '>100x speedup in TTFT for 4M-token sequences' comes from the hybrid efficient-attention backbone (gated linear attention + sliding-window attention, 1:1 inter-layer hybrid; the 76B adds 16 routed + 1 shared expert and 128 sink tokens), not from spikes. No published ablation separates the two contributions.
- THE BEST 2026 NEUROMORPHIC-LM RESULT IS AN ACTIVATION-SPARSITY RESULT, AND ITS HARDWARE ROW IS EXTRAPOLATED, NOT MEASURED. Richter, Lin, Yik, Kergan, Zhu, Moradi & Eshraghian (2026), 'Event-Driven Language Models with Sparse Neural Activity for Neuromorphic Hardware' (Aarhus / UC Santa Cruz / Harvard / Univ. Southern Denmark; full text mirrored at ZhangCurosr/zhangcursor-papers-arxiv-lg-001, dated 2026-09-01; arXiv id not in the mirror) applies learnable per-projection thresholds to a MatMul-free LM (ternary weights, 8-bit activations, MMFree-Linear-GRU). Its Table I footnotes state explicitly that the Loihi 2 SPARSE row is 'extrapolated from dense model using equations 11 and 12', the Loihi 2 DENSE row is taken from a prior multi-chip deployment, and the H100 / Jetson rows are quoted from another paper. Nothing in that table is a fresh end-to-end measurement of the sparse model.
- WHERE SPIKING HAS NEARLY CLOSED THE GAP IS VISION, NOT LANGUAGE. Spike-driven Transformer V3 / E-SpikeFormer (Yao, Qiu, Hu et al., IEEE TPAMI 47(4):2973-2990, 2025; arXiv:2411.16061; repo BICLab/Spike-Driven-Transformer-V3 verified) reports ImageNet-1K top-1 of 78.5% @ 10M params, 79.8% @ 19M, 84.0% @ 83M, 86.2% @ 173M, with 4.5x faster training and 3.9x inference energy efficiency via Spike Firing Approximation (integer training, spike-driven inference). Earlier steps: Spikformer (Zhou et al., ICLR 2023, arXiv:2209.15425), Spike-driven Transformer (Yao et al., NeurIPS 2023, arXiv:2307.01694; 77.07% @ 8 layers x 768 channels, T=4, 6.09 mJ, 'up to 87.2x lower computation energy than vanilla self-attention'), Meta-SpikeFormer / V2 (ICLR 2024, arXiv:2404.03663, ~80%). The vision gap to comparable ANNs is now ~1-2 points; the language gap is 5-21 points on GLUE.
- SPIKING-TRANSFORMER LEADERBOARD NUMBERS NEED INDEPENDENT VERIFICATION. The QKFormer repository (Zhou et al., NeurIPS 2024, arXiv:2403.16552, advertised as the first directly-trained SNN past 85% ImageNet top-1) carries an author notice of 'an implementation issue in the SSA module' affecting its ImageNet results and now recommends a different model (MaxFormer) as the research subject. This is a self-reported correction on the field's headline vision result.
- THE GLUE GAP IS LARGE AND TASK-DEPENDENT. SpikeLM (ICML 2024, arXiv:2406.03287) vs BERT-base, as tabulated by a third-party research digest (UNCERTAIN, not verified against the paper): MNLI 77.1/77.2 vs 83.8/83.4 (-6.7/-6.2), QNLI 85.3 vs 90.7 (-5.4), QQP F1 83.9 vs 90.5 (-6.6), MRPC F1 85.7 vs 89.8 (-4.1), STS-B 84.9 vs 89.4 (-4.5), RTE 69.0 vs 69.3 (-0.3), CoLA 38.8 vs 60.0 (-21.2). The pattern - near-parity on easy/short tasks, collapse on CoLA (grammatical acceptability, the most syntax-sensitive task) - is the signature of losing activation precision, not of losing a temporal code.
- THE ENERGY NUMBERS IN SNN PAPERS ARE ARITHMETIC OPERATION COUNTS AT 45 nm, NOT MEASUREMENTS. The near-universal convention is E_AC = 0.9 pJ (32-bit FP add) vs E_MAC = 4.6 pJ (32-bit FP mult 3.7 + add 0.9), from Horowitz, 'Computing's Energy Problem (and what we can do about it)', ISSCC 2014. Independent digests of two 2025-26 SNN papers state the failure mode plainly: TaWQ (arXiv:2511.17567) 'relies entirely on theoretical energy models (based on 45nm hardware metrics) and does not account for real-world overheads like data movement, memory access latency, or control logic'; SpikeQuant (arXiv:2510.19498) derives energy 'from synthesized 22nm process measurements and analytical models... not from deployment on actual SNN accelerators'.
- THE ONE CLEAN APPLES-TO-APPLES MEASUREMENT ON REAL NEUROMORPHIC SILICON GIVES ~2x, NOT ~50x. Human-activity recognition on Intel Loihi (arXiv:2308.00787): SNN 87.5% accuracy vs ANN on a GAP8 MCU 88.1%, with a 2x better energy-delay product (0.66 uJ*s vs 1.32 uJ*s). UNCERTAIN: from a third-party digest, not the paper.
- THE NEUROMORPHIC SOFTWARE STACK IS IN TRANSITION AND PARTLY CLOSED. The Lava repositories (lava-nc/lava) were ARCHIVED on 13 May 2026; Intel states it is 'developing the next-generation Loihi architecture and SDK' and that 'All Lava repositories are archived'. Access to Loihi hardware and to the proprietary Lava extension that compiles to it requires membership of the Intel Neuromorphic Research Community (INRC). Verified directly from the repository page.
- MANY FLY NEURONS DO NOT SPIKE AT ALL, AND THE ONES THAT DO ARE ELECTROTONICALLY ODD. Gouwens & Wilson 2009, 'Signal propagation in Drosophila central neurons', J Neurosci 29(19):6239-6249, DOI 10.1523/JNEUROSCI.0764-09.2009, PMID 19439602, measured antennal-lobe PN input resistance 598.0 +/- 69.3 MOhm (n = 14) and fitted specific membrane resistance 8.3-20.8 kOhm*cm^2 across 3 reconstructed cells (numbers cross-checked in two independent GitHub parameter corpora). Drosophila central neurons are electrotonically compact for subthreshold signals while somatic spikes are heavily attenuated, so the soma-recorded 'spike' is a small artefact of a distant axonal event. Photoreceptors R1-R6 and lamina large monopolar cells signal by graded potentials with no action potentials, and a substantial fraction of antennal-lobe local interneurons are non-spiking or weakly spiking. UNCERTAIN: I could not re-verify the photoreceptor/LMC/local-interneuron statements from a primary source in this session (elifesciences.org, PMC, arXiv all egress-blocked); they are standard textbook insect neurophysiology (Laughlin's blowfly work; Wilson 2013 Annu Rev Neurosci 36:217-241).
- WHERE THE FLY DOES SPIKE FOR MEMORY, IT EMITS 0-2 SPIKES IN ~10% OF CELLS. Ray, Aldworth & Stopfer, eLife 2020, 9:e53281 (full-scale locust model, 50,000 KCs, 830 PNs, GGN with 5,283 compartments): GGN feedback inhibition raises the KC threshold and confines spiking to brief windows so that ~10% of KCs respond per odour with 0-2 spikes ACROSS INTENSITIES. In Drosophila the equivalent is ~5% of ~2,000 KCs per hemisphere, enforced by the single GABAergic APL neuron, which synapses with 1,871/1,919 KCs (97.5%) and 126/136 PNs (92.6%) (Prisco, Deimel, Yeliseyeva, Fiala & Tavosanis, eLife 2024, 13:e74172, DOI 10.7554/eLife.74172). This is a k-winner-take-all binary code per event, not a rate code and not a precise temporal code.
- A WHOLE FLY BRAIN AS LIF NEURONS FITS ON 12 LOIHI 2 CHIPS AND RUNS FASTER THAN REAL TIME. Shiu et al. 2024, Nature 634:210-219, built a leaky integrate-and-fire model of 127,400 FlyWire neurons / 52.8M synapses (Brian2, dt 0.1 ms; v_rst -52 mV, v_th -45 mV, tau_m 20 ms, tau_syn 5 ms, refractory 2.2 ms, delay 1.8 ms, w_syn 0.275 mV per synapse, so ~25 coincident unitary inputs reach threshold) with ONE free parameter, validating 91% of 164 testable predictions. It costs ~4.4 s of CPU per simulated second; a Sandia port to Loihi 2 (arXiv:2508.16792) on 12 chips runs 12-54 ms per simulated second, i.e. roughly 100-350x faster than CPU and 20-80x faster than real time. (Verified in this repository's own docs/aif/LITERATURE.md against philshiu/Drosophila_brain_model.)
- ACTIVITY SPARSITY WITHOUT SPIKES IS ALREADY DEMONSTRATED WITH A MATCHED CONTROL, AND IS FREE. EGRU (Subramoney, Nazeer, Schoene, Mayr & Kappel, 'Efficient recurrent architectures through activity sparsity and sparse back-propagation through time', ICLR 2023, notable-top-25%, OpenReview lJdOlWg8td, code Efficient-Scalable-Machine-Learning/EvNN and KhaleelKhan/EvNN - citation block verified verbatim): event-based GRU units transmit only when they cross a threshold, reaching ~80-85% activity sparsity on Penn Treebank at perplexity ~56-57 versus a dense LSTM at ~57-58. Follow-ups show the two sparsities multiply: Nazeer et al., arXiv:2311.07625 'Activity Sparsity Complements Weight Sparsity for Efficient RNN Inference'; Mukherji, Schoene, Nazeer, Mayr, Kappel & Subramoney, arXiv:2405.00433 'Weight Sparsity Complements Activity Sparsity in Neuromorphic Language Models'. UNCERTAIN: the PTB perplexity figures come from a third-party digest of the follow-up paper, not from the paper itself.
- THERE IS A LIVE, PRINCIPLED SPIKING SEQUENCE-MODEL LINE. SpikingSSMs (Shen, Wang, Huang, Zhong, Guo, Lu, Zhang & Leng, AAAI 2025, 39(19), DOI 10.1609/aaai.v39i19.34245, arXiv:2408.14909, code shenshuaijie/SDN - citation verified from the repo page) combines a surrogate dynamic network with state-space models for long sequences. This is the branch that makes the LIF-as-SSM equivalence explicit.

### Principles and their translation

| principle | biology / mathematics | translation to a sequence model | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| A LIF neuron IS a one-dimensional diagonal linear state-space model with a threshold and a reset. 'Spiking' therefore adds exactly two things to a modern gated linear RNN: a leak you already have, and 1-bit activation quantization you probably do not want. | Discrete-time LIF with soft reset: u_t = lambda*u_{t-1} + (W x)_t - theta*s_{t-1}, s_t = H(u_t - theta), lambda = exp(-dt/tau_m). Drop the Heaviside and this is literally a diagonal SSM / gated linear attention recurrence h_t = a*h_{t-1} + b_t (Mamba, GLA, DeltaNet, RWKV). The fly's measured tau_m ~20 ms with dt 0.1 ms gives lambda ~0.995 (Shiu et al. 2024 constants). The only non-SSM content is the quantizer and its state-dependent reset. | Do NOT build an SNN. Build a gated linear recurrent backbone with (i) an explicit per-channel decay lambda spanning multiple timescales and (ii) a learnable threshold sparsifier on the activations. This is provably a superset of the LIF model and is exactly what SpikingBrain (adaptive threshold V_th = mean(/x/)/k) and Richter et al. 2026 (learnable per-projection Delta) independently converged on after calling themselves spiking. | established |
| Spike count over T timesteps is an integer activation of log2(T+1) bits bought at T times the latency and T times the training memory. Time is the currency you spend to buy activation precision; on synchronous hardware that trade is always bad. | A neuron emitting n in {0..T} spikes in a window encodes an integer in [0, T]. T = 1 is 1-bit; T = 4 is ~2.3 bits. BPTT over T timesteps multiplies stored activations by T. The fly does not pay this: KCs fire 0-2 spikes per odour event (Ray et al. 2020), i.e. it uses T ~ 2, a ~1.5-bit code, and buys back the precision with 2,000-dimensional sparse expansion instead of with time. | If you want low-precision activations, quantize to k-bit integers at T = 1 with a straight-through estimator. Never unroll T timesteps for an LM. Recover the lost precision the way the fly does - by dimensional expansion with a sparse code - not by adding timesteps. SpikeLLM's generalized-IF neurons and 'All-in-one timestep' work (arXiv:2510.24637) are the field independently rediscovering this. | established |
| The energy inequality: an SNN beats an ANN on ARITHMETIC only if T * s < E_MAC / E_AC ~ 5. But autoregressive decoding is memory-bound, and activation sparsity does not reduce weight traffic. This is why every honest spiking-LM efficiency win is at tiny model size on a chip that holds weights in SRAM. | Arithmetic energy ratio = (T * s * E_AC) / E_MAC with s = spikes per neuron per timestep. Horowitz (ISSCC 2014, 45 nm): E_AC = 0.9 pJ, E_MAC = 4.6 pJ, DRAM access ~640 pJ per 32-bit word (as commonly tabulated, e.g. Han et al., EIE, ISCA 2016) - a ~700:1 ratio between memory and arithmetic. A B-parameter model at b bits reads B*b/8 bytes per decoded token REGARDLESS of how sparse the activations are. At batch 1 arithmetic is a single-digit percentage of the energy. | Any per-token efficiency claim must be reported as (i) measured joules per token on named hardware, (ii) with the batch size stated, and (iii) split into weight traffic, activation traffic and arithmetic. Activation sparsity is worth ~0 at batch-1 GPU decode. It is worth a lot only when weights sit in on-chip SRAM beside the compute - which is why the entire measurable advantage lives at <=1B parameters on Loihi 2 / SpiNNaker 2 / Darwin3, and evaporates at frontier scale where the weights cannot fit. | established |
| SPARSITY, NOT SPIKES, IS THE TRANSFERABLE FLY PRINCIPLE - and it is transferable because it costs almost nothing in quality when it is thresholded rather than binarized. | The mushroom body's computational content is a ~5% k-WTA code over ~2,000 KCs enforced by one global GABAergic feedback neuron (APL contacting 97.5% of KCs; Prisco et al. eLife 2024) plus divisive normalization upstream in the antennal lobe (Olsen, Bhandawat & Wilson, Neuron 66:287, 2010). No part of that mechanism requires a temporal spike code; it requires a threshold and a normalizer. | Threshold-sparsify activations on a gated linear backbone with a learnable per-projection Delta and a divisive normalizer. Measured costs: Richter et al. 2026 raise MAC sparsity from 10.0% (SiLU baseline) to 69.5% at a 0.64-point average zero-shot drop (41.88 -> 41.24) on a 370M MatMul-free LM. SpikingBrain-7B reports 69.15% with performance 'comparable to mainstream open-source models'. EGRU reaches 80-85% activity sparsity at LSTM-equal perplexity on PTB. Three independent groups, same conclusion. | promising |
| GRADED, NOT BINARY, IS WHAT MOST OF THE FLY DOES - because in a 0.5 mm brain there is nothing for a spike to defeat. Spikes are an axonal transmission technology, not a computational primitive. | Cable theory: the action potential exists to regenerate a signal that would otherwise attenuate over a long axon. Drosophila central neurons have very high input resistance (598 MOhm for a PN; Gouwens & Wilson 2009) and neurite lengths comparable to their electrotonic length constant, so subthreshold voltage spreads across the cell almost losslessly. Accordingly, photoreceptors, lamina monopolar cells and many local interneurons dispense with spikes entirely; PNs, KCs, MBONs, CX and descending neurons - the long-range and output pathways - keep them. | The fly-aligned unit is a LEAKY ANALOG INTEGRATOR WITH A SPARSIFYING NONLINEARITY, i.e. a sparse gated linear RNN. This is the same object the project's phase-3 memory branch already points at, which is a convergence, not a coincidence. A design that adopted binary spikes in the name of fly-alignment would be LESS fly-like than one that did not. | established |
| Event-driven computation pays when the INPUT is sparse in time. Text is not. Every token is an event and every token needs the whole model, so there is no temporal sparsity to harvest - you have to manufacture it, and the manufactured versions are already called MoE and early exit. | The fly's event-driven advantage is real because its sensory world is sparse: photoreceptor transients, odour plume encounters, looming events. An odour arrives, ~100 of 2,000 KCs fire 0-2 spikes, and nothing else in the mushroom body does any work. Text has no analogue: the arrival rate of tokens is constant and the required computation per token is roughly constant. | Do not expect an event-driven win on text. If you want conditional computation, take it at the level the field already exploits - routed experts (SpikingBrain-76B uses 16 routed + 1 shared expert precisely because macro sparsity is where the weight traffic is), per-token early exit, or the block-diffusion route that amortizes 'multiple tokens per parameter access' (Neuromorphic Diffusion Language Models, IEEE SiPS 2026, arXiv:2607.24841, per a curated third-party index - UNVERIFIED). | established |
| Surrogate-gradient training is the reason nobody has pretrained a spiking LM at scale, and the alternatives that would fix it have never been shown at LM scale. | Surrogate gradients replace the Heaviside derivative with a smooth pseudo-derivative (Neftci, Mostafa & Zenke, IEEE Signal Processing Magazine 36(6):51-63, 2019; Zenke & Ganguli, 'SuperSpike', Neural Computation 30:1514, 2018). BPTT over T timesteps multiplies activation memory by T on top of the usual sequence-length cost. Exact-gradient and online alternatives exist - EventProp (Wunderlich & Pehle, Scientific Reports 11:12829, 2021), e-prop (Bellec, Scherr, Subramoney, Hajek, Salaj, Legenstein & Maass, Nature Communications 11:3625, 2020), OTTT (NeurIPS 2022), SLAYER (Shrestha & Orchard, NeurIPS 2018) - and the tooling is mature (snnTorch: Eshraghian, Ward, Neftci, Wang, Lenz, Dwivedi, Bennamoun, Jeong & Lu, Proceedings of the IEEE 111(9), 2023, DOI 10.1109/JPROC.2023.3308511 - citation verified verbatim from the repo; SpikingJelly; GeNN/mlGeNN). None has been demonstrated on a language-model pretraining run. | If the design wants event-like behaviour, obtain it by THRESHOLDING A NORMALLY TRAINED MODEL - post-hoc, or with a straight-through estimator at T = 1 during a short fine-tune - which is exactly what SpikingBrain, SpikeLLM and Richter et al. all do. Do not put BPTT-over-timesteps anywhere near the training loop. This also removes the single largest engineering risk in the whole branch. | established |
| IF a spiking claim is ever made, it must be made under a standardized neuromorphic benchmark with measured joules, not under an operation-counting energy model. | Not biology - measurement hygiene. The field's own correction mechanism (QKFormer's self-reported SSA implementation issue) and the systematic 'energy estimated from 45nm analytical models' caveat mean that an unaudited energy multiplier carries approximately zero evidential weight. | Use NeuroBench (Yik et al., 'NeuroBench: A Framework for Benchmarking Neuromorphic Computing Algorithms and Systems', Nature Communications, 2025; arXiv:2304.04640 - venue/year from a third-party summary, UNCERTAIN) for the efficiency axis and a standard zero-shot LM suite (ARC-E, ARC-C, HellaSwag, OpenBookQA, PIQA, WinoGrande - the exact set Richter et al. use) for the quality axis. Report joules per token on named silicon at a stated batch size, or report nothing. | promising |

### Numbers an engineer can use

- SpikeGPT 216M: WikiText-103 test ppl 39.75; WikiText-2 ppl 18.01; trained on 5B OpenWebText tokens for the released checkpoint; max sequence length 128 (third-party tables; UNCERTAIN). Reference points from Radford et al. 2019 Table 3 (zero-shot): GPT-2 117M WikiText-103 37.50 / WikiText-2 29.41; GPT-2 345M 26.37 / 22.76; GPT-2 762M 22.05 / 19.93; GPT-2 1542M 17.48 / 18.34.
- SpikeGPT text classification (third-party table, UNCERTAIN): SST-2 88.76% vs BERT 91.73% (-2.97); SST-5 51.27% vs 53.21% (-1.94); MR 85.63% vs 86.72% (-1.09); Subj 95.30%.
- SpikeLM vs BERT-base on GLUE (third-party table, UNCERTAIN): MNLI 77.1/77.2 vs 83.8/83.4; QNLI 85.3 vs 90.7; QQP-F1 83.9 vs 90.5; MRPC-F1 85.7 vs 89.8; STS-B 84.9 vs 89.4; RTE 69.0 vs 69.3; CoLA 38.8 vs 60.0; SST-2 87.0 vs 92.3. Average gap ~6.7 points; worst case CoLA -21.2 points.
- SpikeLLM (ICLR 2025) on LLaMA-2, WikiText2 ppl (third-party table, UNCERTAIN): 7B 2W16A 14.16 vs baseline 38.05; 13B 2W8A 13.56 vs 53.87; 70B 2W16A 6.35 vs 10.04. Note these are quantization-baseline comparisons (vs GPTQ/OmniQuant-class methods), not ANN-vs-SNN comparisons.
- SpikingBrain: 7B linear (1:1 GLA + sliding-window hybrid) and 76B MoE (16 routed + 1 shared expert, 128 sink tokens, 7 dense FFN layers); continual pretraining ~150B tokens total (100B @ 8K ctx, 20-30B @ 32K, 20-30B @ 128K) from a Qwen2.5-7B init on a MetaX GPU cluster; 69.15% micro-level activation sparsity from adaptive threshold V_th(x) = (1/k)*mean(|x|); >100x TTFT speedup at 4M tokens; '<2% of the data' for continual pretraining. arXiv:2509.05276, TMLR 2026.
- SpikingMamba-1.3B (TMLR 2026): 4.76x energy benefit vs Mamba with a 4.78-point zero-shot accuracy gap before RL, narrowing to 2.23 points after DPO-based refinement. Distilled single-stage from a Mamba teacher of equal scale; SI-LIF neurons with signed multi-level spikes.
- Richter et al. 2026, MatMul-free LM with learnable per-projection thresholds - MAC SPARSITY vs QUALITY: 370M baseline (SiLU) 10.0% sparsity / 41.88% avg zero-shot; 370M lambda=1.0 -> 69.5% / 41.24%; 370M lambda=2.0 -> 80.1% / 39.74%; 2.7B lambda=1 -> 63.2% / 48.50% vs baseline 50.59%. Benchmarks: ARC-Easy, ARC-Challenge, HellaSwag, OpenBookQA, PIQA, WinoGrande. NOTE THE SCALING DIRECTION: the quality cost of sparsification GREW from 0.64 points (370M @ 69.5%) to 2.09 points (2.7B @ 63.2%).
- Richter et al. 2026 Table I - DECODE/GENERATION (tokens/s, then mJ/token; identical across sequence lengths 500/1000/4000/8000 for the Loihi rows): MMFree sparse on Loihi 2 (EXTRAPOLATED) 224.1 tok/s @ 75.0 mJ/token; MMFree dense on Loihi 2 (prior multi-chip deployment) 41.5 @ 405; MMFree dense on H100 13.2-13.5 @ 9.9-10.1 J/token; Transformer++ on H100 21.3-22.9 @ 5.5-6.8 J/token; Alireo-400M on Jetson 14.3-15.2 @ 723-853 mJ/token; Qwen2-500M on Jetson 13.4-15.4 @ 785-912 mJ/token. Implied best case: ~15x throughput and ~10x energy versus an edge GPU at batch-1 decode; the paper's own headline is 'project 37x higher throughput and 16x lower power versus edge GPU transformer inference'.
- Richter et al. 2026 Table I - PREFILL: MMFree sparse on Loihi 2 23.2k tok/s @ 1.1 mJ/token (flat in sequence length); MMFree dense on Loihi 2 6,632 @ 3.7; MMFree dense on H100 11.4k/13.1k/30.6k/51.6k @ 6.1/5.3/2.5/1.4; Transformer++ on H100 21.6k/32.7k/44.3k/55.4k @ 11.3/7.3/5.4/4.3. AT LONG PREFILL THE H100 OVERTAKES LOIHI 2 ON THROUGHPUT (55.4k vs 23.2k tok/s) and closes to within ~4x on energy. The neuromorphic advantage is specifically a batch-1, short-context, decode-side advantage.
- Spike-driven Transformer V3 / E-SpikeFormer (TPAMI 2025, arXiv:2411.16061): ImageNet-1K top-1 78.5% @ 10M, 79.8% @ 19M, 84.0% @ 83M, 86.2% @ 173M; 4.5x training acceleration; 3.9x inference energy efficiency.
- Spike-driven Transformer (NeurIPS 2023): 77.07% ImageNet top-1 at 8 layers x 768 channels, T = 4, 6.09 mJ; SDSA claimed 'up to 87.2x lower computation energy than vanilla self-attention' (analytical).
- Energy model constants (Horowitz, ISSCC 2014, 45 nm 0.9 V): 32-bit INT add 0.1 pJ; 32-bit FP add 0.9 pJ; 32-bit INT mult 3.1 pJ; 32-bit FP mult 3.7 pJ. SNN convention: E_AC = 0.9 pJ, E_MAC = 4.6 pJ, ratio 5.1. DRAM access ~640 pJ per 32-bit word (commonly tabulated figure; ~700x the cost of one add).
- Break-even for an SNN on arithmetic alone: T * s < 5.1, where T = timesteps and s = spikes per neuron per timestep. At T = 4 and s = 0.2 you get T*s = 0.8, a 6.4x arithmetic win - which is ~6.4x on the <10% of batch-1 decode energy that is arithmetic, i.e. under 1.06x end-to-end on a von Neumann machine.
- Measured (not modelled) neuromorphic comparison: Loihi SNN for human activity recognition 87.5% accuracy vs ANN on GAP8 88.1%, 2x better energy-delay product (0.66 uJ*s vs 1.32 uJ*s), arXiv:2308.00787 (third-party digest, UNCERTAIN).
- Neuromorphic hardware scale: Loihi 2 ~1M neurons and ~120M synapses per chip (derived from Hala Point totals). Intel Hala Point (2024): 1,152 Loihi 2 chips, 1.15B neurons, 128B synapses, ~140k neuromorphic cores, ~2.6 kW. SpiNNaker2 chip: 152 ARM Cortex-M4F cores, ~152k neurons, ~152M synapses per chip, 22 nm FDSOI, 16-bit weights (Gonzalez et al., arXiv:2401.04491; chip paper Scholze et al., arXiv:2607.24396). Zhejiang University 'Darwin Monkey' (2025): 960 Darwin3 chips in 15 blade servers, >2B spiking neurons, >100B synapses, ~2 kW. DarwinWafer (arXiv:2509.16213): 64 Darwin3 chiplets on a 300 mm interposer, 0.15B neurons / 6.4B synapses, ~100 W, 4.9 pJ per synaptic operation.
- MEMORY, THE REAL WALL: a 7B model at 4 bits is 3.5 GB of weights. At ~120M synapses (~tens of MB of on-chip SRAM) per Loihi 2 chip, holding those weights on-chip needs O(100-1000) chips - so the only spiking LMs that fit a single neuromorphic board are in the 100M-1B range, which is exactly the size range where every reported win lives.
- Fly electrophysiology: antennal-lobe PN input resistance 598.0 +/- 69.3 MOhm (n = 14); specific membrane resistance 8.3-20.8 kOhm*cm^2 (Gouwens & Wilson 2009). Whole-brain LIF constants (Shiu et al. 2024): v_rst = -52 mV, v_th = -45 mV, tau_m 20 ms, tau_syn 5 ms, refractory 2.2 ms, delay 1.8 ms, w_syn 0.275 mV per synapse (~25 coincident inputs to threshold); 127,400 neurons, 52.8M synapses; 91% of 164 testable predictions validated with ONE free parameter.
- Fly sparse coding: ~2,019 KCs per hemisphere (this repo's MaleCNS extraction), KC fan-in 5.16 PN types (Caron 2013 range 5-7, PASS), APL covers 100% of KCs; ~10% of KCs respond per odour with 0-2 spikes (locust, Ray et al. eLife 2020); APL synapses with 97.5% of KCs and 92.6% of PNs (Prisco et al. eLife 2024). LHON baseline firing ~10x quieter than PNs, evoked 14 Hz vs 21 Hz (Frechter et al., eLife 2019, 8:e44590).
- Fly brain on neuromorphic silicon: 12 Loihi 2 chips run the 127,400-neuron LIF model at 12-54 ms per simulated second, vs ~4,400 ms per simulated second on CPU (arXiv:2508.16792).
- EGRU (ICLR 2023): ~80-85% activity sparsity on Penn Treebank at perplexity ~56-57 vs a dense LSTM at ~57-58 (third-party digest, UNCERTAIN).

### Dead ends

- TRAINING A SPIKING LANGUAGE MODEL FROM SCRATCH TO COMPETITIVE QUALITY. Evidence: in four years the only from-scratch generative spiking LM is SpikeGPT at 216M, whose WikiText-103 perplexity (39.75) is worse than a 117M GPT-2 evaluated zero-shot (37.50). Every model at or above 1B parameters - SpikeLLM 7-70B, SpikingBrain 7B/76B, SpikingMamba 1.3B, SpikeBERT, SpikingBERT, SpikeZIP-TF, LAS (AAAI 2026) - is a conversion, quantization or distillation of a backprop-trained dense network. The reason is mechanical: BPTT over T timesteps multiplies activation memory by T, and no scalable alternative (e-prop, EventProp, OTTT) has ever been run at LM pretraining scale. For this project, a from-scratch spiking pretrain is a guaranteed loss against a Gated-DeltaNet twin on any compute budget you have.
- EXPECTING A QUALITY WIN FROM SPIKING ANYWHERE. There is no benchmark at any scale where a spiking LM beats a parameter-matched ANN on quality. The gaps are systematic and largest exactly where language is hardest: SpikeLM trails BERT-base by ~6.7 points average on GLUE and by 21.2 points on CoLA. Spiking is an efficiency technique with a quality cost; it is never an accuracy technique. Any phase-3 hypothesis of the form 'spikes improve X' is pre-falsified.
- BELIEVING ANY ENERGY MULTIPLIER THAT WAS NOT MEASURED IN JOULES ON NAMED SILICON. The published multipliers (SpikeGPT 32.2x, Sorbet 27x vs BERT, SDSA 87.2x, SpikeLM 12.9x, 'ICANN 2023 sentiment' 64.93x) are all 45 nm operation counts using E_AC = 0.9 pJ vs E_MAC = 4.6 pJ. Two 2025-26 papers' own limitation sections say so. The single clean measured comparison on real neuromorphic silicon (Loihi, human activity recognition) yields 2x energy-delay product. Even the strongest 2026 LM result (Richter et al.) states in its table footnotes that the sparse Loihi 2 row is extrapolated from a dense deployment and that the GPU rows are quoted from a third paper. Nothing here supports a 'state of the art energy' claim that would survive review.
- BETTING ON A PARTICULAR NEUROMORPHIC CHIP AS A DEPLOYMENT TARGET. Intel archived ALL Lava repositories on 13 May 2026 pending a next-generation Loihi architecture and SDK; the compiler back-end that actually targets Loihi silicon is proprietary and gated behind INRC membership. A phase-3 claim whose validity depends on a specific chip is a claim you cannot re-run, and a reviewer cannot reproduce.
- BINARY SPIKES AS THE 'FLY-ALIGNED' ABSTRACTION. This is the central finding for the pivot, and it inverts the intuition. Fly photoreceptors, lamina monopolar cells and many antennal-lobe local interneurons produce no action potentials at all; Drosophila central neurons are electrotonically compact (PN input resistance 598 MOhm), so graded signalling spans the ~0.5 mm brain without regeneration, and the soma-recorded spike is a strongly attenuated artefact of a distant axonal event (Gouwens & Wilson 2009). Where fly neurons do spike for memory, they emit 0-2 spikes in ~10% of cells (Ray et al. 2020) - a k-WTA binary sparse code, not a rate code and not a temporal code. A design that adopted binary spikes to be fly-like would be LESS fly-like than one that used leaky analog units with a sparsifying nonlinearity.
- TEMPORAL CODING (time-to-first-spike, rank-order, phase) FOR TEXT. Temporal codes exist to exploit the relative timing of asynchronous sensory events. Tokens arrive synchronously at a constant rate; there is no timing to code with. The field's own temporal-coding results are confined to small vision and audio tasks. I found no evidence of a temporal-coding advantage on any language benchmark.
- EVENT-DRIVEN COMPUTATION AS A SOURCE OF SPEEDUP ON TEXT. Every token is an event and every token touches the whole model, so activation sparsity does not reduce the weight traffic that dominates batch-1 decode. This is why SpikingBrain-76B reaches for MoE (macro sparsity, which DOES skip weights) and why its >100x TTFT number comes from gated-linear + sliding-window attention rather than from spikes. Manufacturing conditional computation at the weight level is a well-explored non-spiking research area.
- SPIKING RESERVOIRS / LIQUID STATE MACHINES AS AN LM BACKBONE. Already assessed and rejected in this repository's own literature sweep: Legenstein & Maass (Neural Networks 20(3):323-334, 2007) showed the critical line predicts LSM performance for some tasks and not others, and binary/spiking reservoirs at the edge of chaos show abrupt memory loss and high sensitivity to connectivity (Buesing 2010). Phase 1's ring-attractor result (0.24 vs 0.67 for an unstructured layer) is the same lesson in this project's own data.
- RUNNING THE CONNECTOME AS SPIKING NEURONS AND EXPECTING LANGUAGE. Shiu et al. 2024's LIF model is a genuine scientific success (91% of 164 predictions with one free parameter) and it ports to 12 Loihi 2 chips at 12-54 ms per simulated second - but it models fly behaviour, has zero language content, and is not gradient-trained. Public 'connectome-in-a-body' spiking projects (Fly.exe, FruitFly-repo) self-rate as unvalidated, zero their photoreceptor edges, and one of them (Fly.exe) had to SUSPEND its 'topology-specific' claim when it emerged that the shuffle control compared a 9 Hz network against a 56 Hz one: exact-connectome AUROC 0.8077, shuffled 0.500, but cell-type-identity-only 0.7972 - the topology margin was 0.0105, below its own 0.05 gate.

### Open questions

- Does the Loihi 2 advantage survive an actual end-to-end measurement of the SPARSE model? This is the single most decision-relevant unknown in the whole brief: Richter et al. 2026's sparse row (224.1 tok/s, 75 mJ/token) is extrapolated from the dense deployment via their equations 11-12, and their dense row is itself quoted from prior work. A measured sparse deployment could confirm ~15x, or collapse toward the dense chip's 41.5 tok/s / 405 mJ/token.
- Does the quality cost of activation sparsity GROW with scale? Richter et al. lose 0.64 points at 69.5% sparsity on 370M but 2.09 points at a LOWER 63.2% sparsity on 2.7B. Two points is not a scaling law, but the direction is the opposite of what the efficiency story needs, and nobody has run this past 3B. This is a cheap, decisive experiment: sweep threshold lambda at two model scales and fit the quality-vs-sparsity curve.
- How much of SpikingBrain's >100x TTFT speedup is the hybrid linear attention (GLA + sliding window + MoE) and how much is the spike encoding? No published ablation separates them. If the answer is 'almost all attention', then the entire 2025-26 spiking-LLM literature reduces to 'linear attention plus thresholded activations', and the spiking framing is marketing.
- Can event-driven computation be made to reduce WEIGHT traffic rather than activation MACs? This is the only attack on the actual bottleneck. Neuromorphic Diffusion Language Models (IEEE SiPS 2026, arXiv:2607.24841) claim 'multiple tokens per parameter access' by fusing block diffusion with spike-based computation. UNVERIFIED - from a curated third-party index. If it holds, it is the one genuinely novel structural idea in the branch.
- Is there ANY sequence task where a spiking model beats a parameter-matched ANN? The honest place to look is not text: it is event-camera and event-audio streams (DVS128 Gesture, Spiking Heidelberg Digits, Spiking Speech Commands), where the INPUT is temporally sparse. Current SHD/SSC leaderboard numbers could not be verified when this note was written (the curated hub that could be reached does not list SHD/SSC). If the project wants a defensible 'SOTA in a defined regime' claim, this regime - not language - is where to look, and it is off-mission.
- Do fly graded neurons compute anything a leaky analog unit cannot? At least one counterexample exists: Ishida, Sethi, Mohren, Haraguchi, Abbott & Maimon (2025, Cell) show central-complex calcium spikes implement VECTOR INVERSION - a nonlinearity, not a linear sum. The algebra of which fly circuit operations are linear and which require a regenerative event is not catalogued, and this is the one place where 'spike' might carry irreducible computational content for this project.
- What is the measured power of a whole fly brain? The energy argument for graded-over-spiking ultimately rests on Laughlin-lineage metabolic measurements in the blowfly retina, which I could not re-verify (all primary sources egress-blocked). Without a number, 'the fly runs on microwatts' should be stated as an order-of-magnitude expectation, not as a fact, in any phase-3 document.
- Is the SpikeGPT WikiText-103 number (39.75) correct, and what was its matched dense control? My comparison to GPT-2 rests on third-party GitHub tables plus Radford et al. 2019; one of those tables had the two GPT-2 columns transposed. Before this number appears in any project document it should be read off the paper itself.

### Relevance to language models

The honest answer to the brief's question is: there is no regime in which a spiking language model is state of the art on QUALITY, and there is exactly one narrow regime where it is plausibly state of the art on EFFICIENCY - batch-1, short-context, autoregressive DECODE of a 0.1-3B model whose weights fit in on-chip SRAM on neuromorphic silicon. In that corner, Richter et al. (2026) project ~15x throughput and ~10x energy per token against an edge GPU (224 tok/s at 75 mJ/token on Loihi 2 versus Qwen2-500M on Jetson at ~14 tok/s and ~790 mJ/token) - but their own Table I footnotes say the sparse row is extrapolated, not measured, and at long prefill the H100 overtakes Loihi 2 on throughput (55.4k vs 23.2k tok/s) and closes to ~4x on energy. That corner is real, it is defensible, and it is not reachable by this project: it requires neuromorphic hardware whose vendor SDK (Intel Lava) was archived in May 2026 and whose compiler back-end is gated behind INRC membership. Recommending it would be recommending a claim the project cannot run.

The transferable content survives the rejection of spiking, and it points at the same place the phase-3 reflection already points. Strip the spikes from every 2025-26 'spiking LLM' and what remains is identical across three independent groups: a GATED LINEAR RECURRENT BACKBONE WITH THRESHOLD-SPARSIFIED ACTIVATIONS. SpikingBrain-7B calls its threshold V_th(x) = mean(|x|)/k and gets 69.15% sparsity; Richter et al. call theirs a learnable per-projection Delta and get 69.5% at a 0.64-point zero-shot cost; EGRU (ICLR 2023) calls it an event threshold and gets 80-85% activity sparsity at LSTM-equal perplexity on Penn Treebank with a proper matched control. That is a mechanism, it is cheap, it is testable on one GPU, and it is what the fly's mushroom body actually implements (a ~5% k-WTA code enforced by one global inhibitory neuron), as opposed to what an SNN implements (binary events in discrete timesteps). Mathematically the case is closed: a LIF neuron is a one-dimensional diagonal linear SSM plus a threshold and a reset, so 'spiking' contributes a leak the phase-3 memory branch already has, plus a 1-bit quantizer that costs accuracy. The recommendation is therefore to adopt SPARSITY AS A FIRST-CLASS DESIGN VARIABLE and to reject SPIKES AS A SUBSTRATE - and to note, as a genuinely useful inversion, that a sparse leaky-analog design is MORE fly-aligned than a spiking one, because most fly neurons do not spike.


## Sparse distributed, hyperdimensional and Hopfield memory for a fly-aligned language model: what the capacity mathematics actually licenses

*Key: `sparse-hd-hopfield`.*

### Summary

"First, a caveat the designer must carry forward: primary research sources could not be reached when this note was written; only code repositories could be. Citations are therefore tagged VERIFIED-repo (from the project's own verified sweep), VERIFIED-github (metadata confirmed from the repository), or FROM-MEMORY (knowledge to May 2026, to be re-checked). Nothing is fabricated; the FROM-MEMORY numbers are not yet publication-grade.\n\nThe brief's question has a clean answer. Sparse distributed, hyperdimensional and Hopfield memory offer a language model almost nothing that attention lacks — with one exception that is real, quantitative and worth building on.\n\nWhat they do not offer: exponential capacity (attention has it; Ramsauer's modern Hopfield update IS attention), content-addressable retrieval (softmax attention IS a Kanerva SDM read, Bricken and Pehlevan 2021), VSA binding algebra (linear attention IS the outer-product VSA memory, and the delta rule that repairs its capacity is already in the project's Gated DeltaNet control), exact long-range recall (provably impossible for fixed-state memory, Jelassi 2024), and any win at matched state bytes (bounded by state size, Arora 2024).\n\nThe exception: a sparse address decouples capacity from per-token compute. A dense delta-rule store is rank-limited to d_key, which equals its per-token cost. A k-of-n store touches k rows but has rank ceiling n, so at matched compute its ceiling is n/k times higher, paid in state. The project already measured this: 0.82 vs 0.17 recall at 256 pairs, 0.70 vs 0.07 at 512, with k = 100 in both arms — and the crosstalk arithmetic (expected overlap k^2/n) predicts the knee exactly where it appears.\n\nTwo boundaries on that claim. Sparsity buys item count, not bits — the Gardner bound caps information per synapse regardless of coding level, so the sparse store must carry many small bindings, not semantic content. And sparse hashed memory at scale is not new: Product Key Memory, PEER, UltraMem and Meta's Memory Layers already work. Novelty must come from what those lack and the mushroom body has: gradient-free one-shot writes with a precision-inferred rate, multiple compartments sharing one address code at different decay rates, APL-style global inhibition that keeps a fixed hash valid under shift, and selective replay. The middle two are cheap, anatomical and unmeasured for language.\n\nDesign discipline: pre-register matched-FLOPs and matched-state-bytes arms separately; expect the second to be null; test at P spanning the dense control's ceiling (phase 1 tested 8 pairs against a 65-capacity store and could not have seen the effect); treat perplexity as a no-regression check only."

### Established (with sources)

- RESEARCH CONSTRAINT FOR THIS BRIEF (read first): primary research sources could not be reached when this note was written; only code repositories and their metadata could be. Therefore: citations below come from (a) the repository's own already-verified sweep in docs/aif/LITERATURE.md, (b) repository and venue metadata, and (c) background knowledge to May 2026. Every item is tagged [VERIFIED-repo], [VERIFIED-github], or [FROM-MEMORY, re-verify]. Nothing is invented, but the [FROM-MEMORY] numbers must be re-checked before they enter a paper.
- Modern Hopfield networks with the softmax/log-sum-exp energy ARE transformer attention: the retrieval update xi_new = X softmax(beta X^T xi) is one attention step; retrieval converges in essentially one update with error exponentially small in the pattern separation, and storage capacity is exponential in the pattern dimension d (Ramsauer, Schaefl, Lehner, Seidl, Widrich, Adler, Gruber, Holzleitner, Pavlovic, Sandve, Greiff, Kreil, Kopp, Klambauer, Brandstetter, Hochreiter, 'Hopfield Networks is All You Need', arXiv:2008.02217, ICLR 2021; code ml-jku/hopfield-layers, 1,967 stars) [VERIFIED-github for repo/paper identity; theorem constants FROM-MEMORY]. CONSEQUENCE FOR THIS PROJECT: 'exponential capacity' is not a reason to add a Hopfield module to a transformer — the transformer already has it. Any capacity claim must be stated per unit of STATE or per unit of per-token COMPUTE, never in the abstract.
- Softmax attention approximates a Kanerva Sparse Distributed Memory read: with L2-normalised keys/queries, attention reproduces SDM's Hamming-ball circle-intersection weighting, and the fitted softmax beta corresponds to SDM's read radius; different GPT-2 heads learn different effective betas (Bricken & Pehlevan, 'Attention Approximates Sparse Distributed Memory', NeurIPS 2021) [VERIFIED-repo, docs/aif/LITERATURE.md line 1087]. CONSEQUENCE: SDM's *read* is not a differentiator either. The differentiators are SDM's fixed-size state, its O(k) write, and its one-shot writeability without gradients.
- Linear attention IS a vector-symbolic (VSA) outer-product memory: keys bind to values by outer product, bundling is summation, retrieval is unbinding by dot product; the failure mode is exactly the VSA capacity limit (when the number of stored keys exceeds d_key the keys cannot stay near-orthogonal and retrieval collapses), and the published fix is the delta rule (Schlag, Irie, Schmidhuber, 'Linear Transformers Are Secretly Fast Weight Programmers', ICML 2021, arXiv:2102.11174; Yang, Wang, Zhang, Panda, Kim, 'Parallelizing Linear Transformers with the Delta Rule over Sequence Length', NeurIPS 2024, arXiv:2406.06484; Yang, Kautz, Hatamizadeh, 'Gated Delta Networks', ICLR 2025, arXiv:2412.06464) [FROM-MEMORY for ids, but DeltaNet/Gated DeltaNet are already the project's controls]. CONSEQUENCE: VSA *algebra* is already the state of the art in the linear-attention line. The fly's possible contribution is the sparse ADDRESS SPACE, not the algebra.
- Recall capability of any fixed-state sequence model is governed by recurrent state size, and this is provable, not empirical: Arora et al. establish a recall-throughput tradeoff and a state-size lower bound for associative recall, and construct the MQAR benchmark that isolates it (Arora, Eyuboglu, Timalsina, Johnson, Poli, Zou, Rudra, Re, 'Zoology: Measuring and Improving Recall in Efficient Language Models', arXiv:2312.04927, ICLR 2024; and 'Simple linear attention language models balance the recall-throughput tradeoff' (BASED), arXiv:2402.18668, ICML 2024) [FROM-MEMORY for ids; MQAR is already used in phase 1].
