CONSEQUENCE, AND THIS IS THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT DESIGN CONSTRAINT: a sparse memory can NOT be expected to win at matched STATE BYTES. It can only win at matched per-token COMPUTE with larger state. Both controls must be pre-registered separately; they give different answers and conflating them is how this kind of claim dies in review.
- There is a proven separation showing a fixed-state memory can never replace attention for exact long-range retrieval: a 2-layer transformer can copy strings of length exponential in its width, while any state-space/recurrent model with a fixed state needs state proportional to the string length (Jelassi, Brandfonbrener, Kakade, Malach, 'Repeat After Me: Transformers are Better than State Space Models at Copying', ICML 2024, arXiv:2402.01032) [FROM-MEMORY]. CONSEQUENCE: the claim must be about lossy, capacity-bounded recall economics, never about exactness.
- The fly's Kenyon-cell fan-in is at a mathematical optimum, not an accident: the fan-in K that maximises the dimensionality of the expanded representation is K ~ 7 for the fly (measured 6-7) and K ~ 4 for cerebellar granule cells (measured ~4) (Litwin-Kumar, Harris, Axel, Sompolinsky, Abbott, 'Optimal Degrees of Synaptic Connectivity', Neuron 93:1153-1164, 2017) [FROM-MEMORY for page numbers; the result itself is solid]. The project's own MaleCNS extraction measures KC fan-in of 5.16 (L) and 5.07 (R) PN types, median 5 [VERIFIED-repo, docs/CIRCUITS.md].
- Random sparse expansion followed by winner-take-all is a locality-sensitive hash that beats classical LSH at short hash lengths (Dasgupta, Stevens, Navlakha, 'A neural algorithm for a fundamental computing problem', Science 358:793-796, 2017) [FROM-MEMORY for page numbers], and the same trick learned with the Krotov-Hopfield local rule (BioHash) beats ITQ and LSH on retrieval mAP (Ryali, Hopfield, Grinberg, Krotov, ICML 2020) [VERIFIED-repo line 1084].
- But expansion-plus-sparsification has a hard theoretical ceiling: relative noise in sparse tags grows as sqrt(2 ln(1/alpha)) while decorrelation gains grow only logarithmically, so expansion improves discriminability 'up to a point and then degrades it', with the optimal sparseness depending on input SNR (Babadi & Sompolinsky, Neuron 2014) [VERIFIED-repo line 1078]. This is the theory behind the observed failure of fly-LSH at 100k adversarially similar LM keys [VERIFIED-repo line 1090, weak source].
- Sparse coding raises the NUMBER of storable patterns but not the INFORMATION stored: classical Hopfield capacity is 0.138N (Amit, Gutfreund, Sompolinsky 1985-87); with coding level f the critical capacity scales as alpha_c ~ 1/(f ln(1/f)), which diverges as f -> 0 (Tsodyks & Feigel'man, Europhys. Lett. 6:101, 1988; Gardner, J. Phys. A 21:257, 1988, gives the 2 bits/synapse optimal bound) [FROM-MEMORY]. This is the most important and most often ignored fact in the whole brief: SPARSITY BUYS ITEM COUNT, NOT BITS.
- Dense associative memory with higher-order energies has capacity N^(n-1) for polynomial degree n (Krotov & Hopfield, NeurIPS 2016, arXiv:1606.01164) and 2^(N/2) for the exponential energy (Demircigil, Heusel, Lowe, Upgang, Vermet, 'On a model of associative memory with huge storage capacity', J. Stat. Phys. 168:288-299, 2017) [FROM-MEMORY]. The continuous-state unification that makes these usable as network layers is Krotov & Hopfield, 'Large Associative Memory Problem in Neurobiology and Machine Learning', ICLR 2021 (arXiv:2008.06996) [FROM-MEMORY].
- All single-shot associative memory models (Hopfield, modern Hopfield, SDM, attention, PCNs) decompose into the same three stages — similarity, separation, projection — which is the right framework for designing a new one rather than inventing from scratch (Millidge, Salvatori, Song, Lukasiewicz, Bogacz, 'Universal Hopfield Networks', ICML 2022, arXiv:2202.04557) [FROM-MEMORY].
- The sparse-Hopfield line is real, peer-reviewed, and its evaluations are NOT language: 'On Sparse Modern Hopfield Model' (NeurIPS 2023), 'STanHop: Sparse Tandem Hopfield Model for Memory-Enhanced Time Series Prediction' (ICLR 2024), 'Uniform Memory Retrieval with Larger Capacity for Modern Hopfield Models' / U-Hop (ICML 2024), 'Outlier-Efficient Hopfield Layers for Large Transformer-Based Models' / OutEffHop (ICML 2024), 'BiSHop' for tabular data (ICML 2024), 'Nonparametric Modern Hopfield Models' — all Hu, Wu, Chen, Liu et al., MAGICS-LAB, Northwestern [VERIFIED-github: all six repositories exist under github.com/MAGICS-LAB with those exact venue tags in their descriptions]. Their domains are multiple-instance learning, time series, tabular data and quantisation — none reports a language-modelling perplexity win.
- Hopfield-based SEQUENCE memory exists and has capacity theory: 'Long Sequence Hopfield Memory' shows that separating attractor and transition terms with higher-order interactions raises sequence capacity, and that sparse patterns extend it further and permit correlated patterns (Chaudhry, Zavatone-Veth, Krotov, Pehlevan, NeurIPS 2023, arXiv:2306.04532) [FROM-MEMORY]. Temporal predictive coding is provably an asymmetric Hopfield network with implicit whitening and is more stable on structured sequences (Tang, Salvatori, Millidge, Song, Lukasiewicz, Bogacz, NeurIPS 2023) [VERIFIED-repo lines 24, 41].
- The Energy Transformer replaces a whole transformer block with one energy function whose gradient flow performs both attention and the Hopfield/MLP computation (Hoover, Liang, Pham, Panda, Strobelt, Chau, Zaki, Krotov, NeurIPS 2023, arXiv:2302.07253); its published evaluations are masked image completion and graph anomaly detection, not language modelling [FROM-MEMORY]. A 2025 tutorial repository by the same author, bhoov/amtutorial, 'Everything you need to know about Associative Memories and Hopfield Networks for the Modern AI Era', exists and is the right entry point [VERIFIED-github; file contents not readable from this session because GitHub access is scoped to peterpetu/flybrainllm].
- Sparse, hash-addressed, top-k key-value memory ALREADY WORKS in production-scale language models, and got there without the fly: Product Key Memory (Lample, Sablayrolles, Ranzato, Denoyer, Jegou, NeurIPS 2019, arXiv:1907.05242) with ~1M slots and top-k=32; 'Memory Layers at Scale' (Berges, Oguz, Haziza, Yih, Zettlemoyer, Ghosh, Meta, arXiv:2412.09764, 2025) scaling memory to ~128B memory parameters and beating dense models at 2x the compute on factual QA; UltraMem (ByteDance, ICLR 2025, arXiv:2411.12364); PEER / 'Mixture of a Million Experts' (He, arXiv:2407.04153, 2024) [all FROM-MEMORY]. THIS IS THE DECISIVE CONTEXT FOR THE BRIEF: the 'KC-style sparse addressing' idea is not unexplored, it is a live and successful ML line. Novelty must come from the write rule, the gating, the timescales and the continual-learning regime, not from the sparse address itself.
- Non-parametric associative memory measurably improves language models: kNN-LM reaches WikiText-103 perplexity 15.79 against an 18.65 base by interpolating with a ~100M-key datastore (Khandelwal, Levy, Jurafsky, Zettlemoyer, Lewis, ICLR 2020, arXiv:1911.00172); Memorizing Transformers add a non-differentiable kNN memory of ~262K tokens with gains comparable to a large parameter increase (Wu, Rabe, Hutchins, Szegedy, ICLR 2022, arXiv:2203.08913); RETRO retrieves from a 2T-token database (Borgeaud et al., ICML 2022, arXiv:2112.04426) [FROM-MEMORY]. These are EXACT, GROWING datastores, i.e. the opposite design point from a superposition memory — a useful upper bound on what memory can buy.
- The strongest published 'VSA beats deep nets' result is not language: a neuro-vector-symbolic architecture solves Raven's Progressive Matrices at ~98.8% on I-RAVEN with very large speed-ups over neuro-symbolic baselines (Hersche, Zeqiri, Benini, Sebastian, Rahimi, Nature Machine Intelligence 5:363-375, 2023) [FROM-MEMORY]. It pays where combinatorial role-filler binding IS the task.
- The project's own pure-numpy capacity study already establishes the central quantitative claim at matched per-token compute, independent of any external paper: at 512 stored pairs a sparse delta-rule memory with 100 active rows of 2,048 recalls 0.70 while a dense delta-rule memory with d_key=100 recalls 0.07; at 256 pairs it is 0.82 vs 0.17 (docs/phase3/MEMORY_CAPACITY.md, results/capacity_numpy.json) [VERIFIED-repo]. The mechanism is the near-orthogonality of random k-of-n codes: expected overlap k^2/n ~ 5 active units against k = 100.
- The project's own adaptive-rate study establishes that a precision-gated write rate beta = sigmoid(log(pi ||e||^2/n)) matches a change-point Kalman oracle that knows the noise level and the jump rate, and equals or beats the best fixed rate in both noise regimes without knowing the regime — but the margin over a TUNED fixed rate is only a few points (docs/phase3/ADAPTIVE_RATE.md) [VERIFIED-repo]. This is the one surviving piece of active inference and it buys robustness, not a large gain.
- Established fly biology that constrains the design: ~2,000 Kenyon cells per hemisphere (project measures 2,019 L / 2,045 R), a single GABAergic APL neuron contacting essentially all of them (project measures 100% L / 99.95% R coverage), ~5% coding level enforced by APL feedback inhibition (Lin, Bygrave, de Calignon, Lee, Miesenbock, Nature Neuroscience 17:559-568, 2014; Turner, Bazhenov, Laurent, J. Neurophysiol. 99:734-746, 2008; Honegger, Campbell, Turner, J. Neurosci. 31:11772, 2011) [CIRCUITS.md VERIFIED-repo; the three papers FROM-MEMORY], approximately random PN->KC convergence (Caron, Ruta, Abbott, Axel, Nature 497:113-117, 2013) with later evidence of structured sampling (Zheng et al., Current Biology 2022) [FROM-MEMORY], ~15-32 compartments each with its own dopaminergic neurons and its own decay dynamics and storage capacity (Aso et al., eLife 2014; Aso & Rubin, eLife 2016, e16135 — the latter VERIFIED-repo line 557), and error-correcting rather than purely Hebbian KC->MBON plasticity (Hige, Aso, Modi, Rubin, Turner, Neuron 88:985-998, 2015) [FROM-MEMORY]. Review: Modi, Shuai, Turner, Annual Review of Neuroscience 43:465-484, 2020 [FROM-MEMORY].

### Principles and their translation

| principle | biology / mathematics | translation to a sequence model | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sparse addressing decouples memory CAPACITY from per-token COMPUTE; dense fast weights do not. This is the only rigorous, quantitative advantage in the whole brief and it should be the load-bearing claim of the next phase. | A dense fast-weight store M in R^(d_key x d_v) written by the delta rule is a rank-<=d_key object: its exact-recall ceiling equals d_key, which is also its per-token read/write cost. A k-of-n sparse-address store touches k rows per token but its address matrix has rank up to n, so its ceiling is n, not k. For Hebbian writes the usable capacity is set by crosstalk: two independent uniform k-of-n codes overlap with mean k^2/n and variance ~ (k^2/n)(1-k/n)^2, giving read noise variance ~ P k^2/n^2 per stored item and hence P <~ c n^2/k^2. For n=1024, k=64 this predicts collapse near P = 256 — and the project's own measurement shows sparse-Hebb recall falling from 0.84 at P=64 to 0.33 at P=256, i.e. the theory predicts the measured knee. Error-correcting (delta) writes replace the crosstalk limit by the rank limit, which is why sparse-delta still recalls 0.75 at P=256. Biologically this is the mushroom body: ~2,000 KCs, ~5% active, fan-in ~5-7, one inhibitory APL neuron setting the threshold. | Build the memory as M in R^(n x d_v) with n = 2^14..2^17 rows, addressed by a k-of-n code with k = 32..128, read r = a^T M / sqrt(k) and written M[a] += beta_t (v_t - r) by gather/scatter. Per-token cost is O(k d_v), state is O(n d_v). Pre-register TWO controls against a Gated DeltaNet twin: (i) matched per-token FLOPs (the sparse store should win on recall at P >> d_state/d_v), and (ii) matched state bytes (the sparse store should NOT win — Arora's state-size bound forbids it). Claiming (i) while being honest about (ii) is a defensible, novel, falsifiable result. At n > ~2^16 an exhaustive top-k is itself the compute bottleneck, so the address search must use product keys (Lample 2019) or an ANN index, which is the real engineering crux. | established |
| Sparsity buys the NUMBER of retrievable items, not the number of BITS. Choose the code to match what the language task actually needs to remember. | Gardner's bound caps a perceptron-like readout at ~2 bits per synapse regardless of coding level; Hebbian rules reach ~0.14-0.29 bits/synapse. With coding level f the critical pattern count scales as alpha_c ~ 1/(f ln(1/f)), which diverges as f -> 0 while the total stored information saturates. So driving f down multiplies how many distinct things you can address, and simultaneously shrinks how much each one can say. | Route content by type. Many low-content bindings (entity -> attribute, name -> referent, key -> recent value, position -> token) belong in the sparse store, where item count is what matters. High-content semantic prediction belongs in dense weights and local attention. A concrete architectural consequence: do not try to make the sparse store carry the residual stream; make it carry a small d_v (16-64) value per address and let the backbone consume its read as one more input. This also predicts a failure mode to pre-register: the sparse memory should NOT help on general perplexity, only on recall-shaped metrics, and observing a perplexity win would be evidence of a confound. | established |
| Attention already is an SDM read and a modern-Hopfield retrieval, so no capacity or content-addressability argument alone justifies a new memory module. The only open axes are state economics, one-shot writeability without gradients, and write-rate control. | Ramsauer 2020 gives exponential-in-d capacity, one-step retrieval, exponentially small error for the softmax energy, and the update rule IS softmax attention. Bricken & Pehlevan 2021 show that L2-normalised softmax attention approximates Kanerva's circle-intersection read with beta playing the role of the Hamming read radius. Millidge 2022 shows Hopfield, SDM, attention and predictive-coding memories are all similarity -> separation -> projection with different choices of the three. | Stop treating 'add a Hopfield layer' as a design. Instead pick the three stages deliberately: similarity = sparse-code overlap (cheap, gradient-free, hash-like); separation = hard top-k enforced by a global inhibitory unit (APL) rather than a softmax; projection = gathered rows of a written matrix. The resulting module differs from attention in exactly three engineering ways — fixed state, O(k) cost, gradient-free one-shot write — and those three are the whole hypothesis. | established |
| Error-corrected (delta) writes beat Hebbian outer products, and the fly implements the error-corrected rule. Biology and mathematics agree here, which is rare and should be exploited. | The delta / Widrow-Hoff write M += a (v - a^T M)^T solves the least-squares problem incrementally, converting a crosstalk-limited store into a rank-limited store. In the mushroom body, KC->MBON synapses are depressed by coincidence of KC activity with compartment-specific dopamine, and the dopamine signal itself is driven by MBON->DAN feedback, i.e. by prediction error, not by raw reward. The project measured 339 (L) / 300 (R) MBON->DAN feedback edges in MaleCNS, so the error loop is anatomically present. | Always use the delta form; it is also what DeltaNet and Gated DeltaNet use, so this is not a differentiator but a prerequisite. The project's phase-1 H1c already confirmed delta over Hebb. The differentiator candidate is the compartment structure: several stores sharing one address code but with different decay rates (the project used 4/16/64/256), read concatenated. That has no equivalent in DeltaNet and is directly anatomical. | established |
| The write RATE is an inferred quantity, not a hyper-parameter — this is the single surviving piece of active inference and it is exactly what test-time-learning models lack a principled rule for. | Dopamine, octopamine and internal state set how much a surprise changes a synapse; a stable world should lower the rate and a changed world raise it. Formally, a Bayesian filter on a slowly drifting parameter with change points has an optimal gain that rises with the ratio of current squared error to expected squared error — which is what the gate beta = sigmoid(a log(pi //e//^2 / n) + b) computes with pi a running Gamma-tracked inverse error variance. | The project already measured this: the gated rule matches a change-point Kalman oracle that knows sigma and the jump rate, and equals or beats the best fixed rate in both noise regimes (docs/phase3/ADAPTIVE_RATE.md). BUT the honest margin over a tuned fixed rate is only a few points, and phase-1 H1b found a LEARNED gate beats the fixed error gate. So do not claim this as the win; use it as the default and test it only in the non-stationary regime (silent re-binding of a key's value), where a tuned fixed rate cannot be tuned because the regime changes mid-sequence. That is where a principled gate should show a real, pre-registerable advantage. | promising |
| A global inhibitory unit that holds the coding level constant is what makes a FIXED random hash survive distribution shift. Without it, fixed sparse addressing fails on anisotropic language-model states. | One GABAergic APL neuron receives from and inhibits essentially all ~2,000 KCs (project measures 100% / 99.95% coverage), implementing divisive normalisation that keeps ~5% of KCs active regardless of odour concentration; blocking APL broadens KC tuning and impairs odour discrimination. Mathematically this is an adaptive threshold that makes the k-WTA operator scale-invariant, which is precisely the condition under which a random projection remains a locality-sensitive hash. | Before the sparse expansion, whiten/decorrelate the residual stream (the antennal-lobe stage: a learned low-dimensional projection plus divisive normalisation), then apply k-WTA with the threshold set by a running global inhibition signal rather than a fixed constant. The repo's own weak-source evidence is a hard failure of fly-LSH at 100k adversarially similar LM keys without whitening; Babadi & Sompolinsky's noise-amplification result is the theory. Pre-register the whitening ablation: it should be the difference between working and not working, which makes it a cheap, informative experiment. | promising |
| Optimal fan-in is a derivable number, and the fly sits at it. Use the derived value, not the measured one — and note that this makes the anatomy a check on the mathematics rather than a source of transfer. | Litwin-Kumar, Harris, Axel, Sompolinsky & Abbott (Neuron 2017) derive the fan-in K that maximises the dimensionality of an expanded representation: K ~ 7 for fly parameters (measured 6-7), K ~ 4 for cerebellar granule cells (measured ~4). The project's MaleCNS extraction gives 5.16 / 5.07 PN types per KC. Cover's 1965 counting theorem then bounds the readout: a linear readout of M random features learns ~2M dichotomies, i.e. ~4,000 associations for 2,000 KCs. | Set the expansion fan-in from the dimensionality-maximisation formula at YOUR d_pn and n, not from the fly's 5-7. This is the correct use of the connectome after phase 1: it validated a formula, it does not supply a matrix. Phase 1 already showed real PN->KC wiring equals random wiring at matched fan-in (H1d, null, -0.1 seed-SD), which is the expected result if only the fan-in statistic matters — so the anatomy has already done its job here and should not be re-litigated. | established |
| VSA binding gives structured records inside a fixed-size state at O(k) cost, and there is a theory of it for SPARSE codes specifically. This is the part of the hyperdimensional literature that attention does not already contain. | Plate's Holographic Reduced Representations bind by circular convolution and unbind by correlation with an approximate inverse (Plate, IEEE Trans. Neural Networks 6(3):623-641, 1995; book 2003). Frady, Kleyko & Sommer give the information capacity of superposition-based sequence memory in recurrent networks (Neural Computation 30(6):1449-1513, 2018) and, crucially for this brief, a theory of variable binding for SPARSE distributed representations using block-local circular convolution (IEEE TNNLS 34(5):2191-2204, 2023), which keeps binding cost O(k). Surveys: Kleyko, Rachkovskij, Osipov, Rahimi, ACM Computing Surveys 55(6) and 55(9), 2023 (arXiv:2111.06077, arXiv:2112.15424). [All FROM-MEMORY for volume/page numbers.] | Two concrete uses. (1) Positional/role binding inside a memory row: bind the value with a permutation rho^(t mod T) or a block-code binding before writing, so one row can hold several time-tagged or role-tagged values and be unbound by role at read time. This multiplies effective capacity by the number of roles at no extra state. (2) One-vector context summaries for cheap re-ranking. Be honest that RoPE is already a phasor binding, so (1) is an incremental, testable addition and not a new paradigm. Test it in isolation on MQAR with multiple values per key before it goes anywhere near a language model. | promising |
| Uniformity of stored memories, not just their number, controls capacity — and it can be optimised. | U-Hop (Wu, Hu, Chen, Liu et al., 'Uniform Memory Retrieval with Larger Capacity for Modern Hopfield Models', ICML 2024) adds a learnable memory kernel trained to minimise a separation loss, provably enlarging capacity by making stored patterns more uniformly separated [VERIFIED-github: repo MAGICS-LAB/UHop with the ICML 2024 tag; the mechanism FROM-MEMORY]. The same idea in the sparse setting is the anti-Hebbian term of the Krotov-Hopfield local rule (p=2, k=2, delta=0.4, eps0=0.02 in the reference implementation) which spreads the learned addresses apart. | If the addresses are learned rather than fixed-random, add an explicit separation/decorrelation penalty on the address code (or use the Krotov-Hopfield anti-Hebbian term). Without it, learned keys collapse and the whole capacity argument evaporates — which is a specific, cheap ablation and a likely explanation if a learned-key version underperforms the fixed-random one. | promising |
| Two stages with different write rates plus offline consolidation is the genuinely underexplored piece, and it is what turns a memory result into a CONTINUAL-LEARNING result. | The fly has labile (ARM) and consolidated (LTM, protein-synthesis- and sleep-dependent) memory stages, and DAN cell types write and update memories with type-specific rules for training requirement, decay dynamics, storage capacity and flexibility (Aso & Rubin, eLife 2016, e16135) [VERIFIED-repo line 557]. Complementary-learning-systems theory says the same thing at a higher level. | Fast sparse store -> periodic replay -> slow dense weights. WARNING, and this is the most valuable negative result the project already owns: the repo's own consolidation toy shows naive replay barely retains anything past the fast horizon (retention 0.06 vs 0.02 beyond 300 items). So the design must specify the SELECTION rule (replay by novelty/valence, i.e. what dopamine provides), the replay BUDGET (several passes, not one), and a slow store whose capacity exceeds the stream — and must measure against a store of equal total capacity with no consolidation. Do not put consolidation in the critical path of the main claim; make it a second, separate hypothesis. | speculative |
| Compute the capacity budget BEFORE choosing the benchmark, and pick the benchmark where the budget predicts a visible gap. | Given n rows, d_v value dimension, k active, and a delta-rule write, the design's own predicted ceilings are: crosstalk-limited P ~ c n^2/k^2 for Hebbian writes; rank-limited P -> n for converged delta writes; single-pass delta somewhere between (the project measured 0.70 recall at P = 512 with n = 2048, k = 100, i.e. P = n/4). A dense compute-matched control has ceiling d_key = k. | Choose task difficulty to straddle the predicted knee. Phase 1's H1 tested 8 pairs against a store with d_key = 65 — five times BELOW the dense control's own ceiling, so it could not possibly have shown the effect, and it did not. Test at P in {8, 40, 200, 1000} so the dense control is above ceiling for the last two and the sparse one is not. Benchmarks: MQAR (Arora 2023) for the capacity curve; a silent re-binding variant for the adaptive-rate claim; BABILong (Kuratov et al., NeurIPS 2024, arXiv:2406.10149) and RULER (Hsieh et al., arXiv:2404.06654) for long-horizon retention; closed-book NaturalQuestions/TriviaQA if the memory-layer framing is used; the standard zero-shot suite as a no-regression check only [benchmark ids FROM-MEMORY]. | established |

### Numbers an engineer can use

- Project-measured mushroom body (MaleCNS v1.0, docs/CIRCUITS.md) [VERIFIED-repo]: 2,019 KCs (L) / 2,045 (R); 120/119 PNs of 60/61 types; KC fan-in 5.30/5.23 PN neurons, 5.16/5.07 PN types, median 5; PN fan-out 82.6/83.2 KCs; PN->KC 9,917/9,898 edges and 183,709/189,838 anatomical synaptic contacts; 1 GABAergic APL covering 100%/99.95% of KCs; 48/49 MBONs over 32 compartments; 158 PAM + 8 PPL1 DANs; MBON->DAN feedback 339/300 edges (2,861/2,605 contacts); KC->MBON 17,931/17,985 edges with MBON fan-in 373/367 KCs.
- Project capacity measurement at matched per-token compute (docs/phase3/MEMORY_CAPACITY.md, results/capacity_numpy.json, d_v=16, 5 trials, one-shot recall accuracy) [VERIFIED-repo]: at P=128 stored pairs, dense delta d_key=64 gives 0.25, sparse Hebb 64-of-1024 gives 0.57, sparse delta 64-of-1024 gives 0.90; at P=256, 0.11 / 0.33 / 0.75; at P=512, 0.04 / 0.13 / 0.56. With the larger code, at P=256 dense delta d_key=100 gives 0.17 vs sparse delta 100-of-2048 at 0.82; at P=512, 0.07 vs 0.70.
- Crosstalk arithmetic the designer can recompute: for two independent uniform k-of-n codes the expected overlap is k^2/n with sd ~ sqrt((k^2/n)(1-k/n)^2). For n=2048, k=100: mean overlap 4.88, sd ~2.1, against signal k=100, i.e. per-interferer SNR ~ n/k = 20.5. For n=1024, k=64: mean overlap 4.0, sd ~1.9, SNR ~16. Predicted Hebbian-write knee P ~ c n^2/k^2 = 256 for (1024,64) and 419 for (2048,100) — and the measured sparse-Hebb recall at (1024,64) does collapse from 0.84 at P=64 to 0.33 at P=256.
- Project adaptive-rate study (docs/phase3/ADAPTIVE_RATE.md) [VERIFIED-repo]: 64-of-1024 sparse delta store, 48 stored pairs, change-point rate 0.004 per pair per step. At noise sigma=0.3 the surprise-gated per-pair policy gives recall MSE 0.360 / accuracy 0.81 vs best fixed rate 0.423-0.428 / 0.78-0.80 and a change-point Kalman oracle at 0.398 / 0.81. At sigma=1.0: gated 0.706 / 0.71 vs best fixed 0.664-0.716 / 0.58-0.67 and oracle 0.651 / 0.67. Global tracker loses almost nothing vs per-pair (0.370 vs 0.360 at sigma=0.3).
- Project consolidation toy (docs/phase3/CONSOLIDATION_TOY.md) [VERIFIED-repo]: fast store 64-of-1024 with decay 0.995 (horizon ~200 items), 600 associations, slow dense rank-512 store, replay every 50 items. Retention by age: newest 50, 0.76 -> 0.80 with consolidation; 51-150, 0.64 -> 0.62; 151-300, 0.26 -> 0.26; older than 300, 0.02 -> 0.06. Naive replay is worth essentially nothing.
- Classical capacity constants [FROM-MEMORY, re-verify]: Hopfield 0.138N (Amit, Gutfreund, Sompolinsky 1985-87); Gardner optimal bound 2 bits per synapse (Gardner, J. Phys. A 21:257, 1988); Hebbian sparse-coding realisation ~0.14-0.29 bits/synapse; sparse-coding critical capacity alpha_c ~ 1/(f ln(1/f)) (Tsodyks & Feigel'man, Europhys. Lett. 6:101, 1988); Kanerva SDM capacity ~0.1 x (number of hard locations), with 1000-bit addresses and an access radius near 451 bits chosen to activate ~0.1% of locations (Kanerva 1988; exact analysis Chou, IEEE Trans. Inf. Theory 35(2):281-298, 1989).
- Modern Hopfield capacity [FROM-MEMORY, re-verify]: Krotov & Hopfield 2016 polynomial energy of degree n gives ~N^(n-1); Demircigil et al. 2017 exponential energy gives 2^(N/2) for binary patterns; Ramsauer et al. 2020 give N >= sqrt(p) c^((d-1)/4) patterns retrievable with probability 1-p, i.e. exponential in d, with c defined through the Lambert W function of the inverse temperature beta and sphere radius. Treat the exact constants as unverified; the exponential-in-d scaling is the robust part.
- Cover's counting theorem consequence: a linear readout of M random features learns ~2M random dichotomies, so a fly MBON reading 2,000 KCs has an order-of-magnitude capacity of ~4,000 associations. For a design with n = 2^16 rows this becomes ~131,000, which is the right order for entity/fact memory and far above any plausible within-context binding load. [Cover 1965; arithmetic is the designer's.]
- Fly coding level and fan-in optima [FROM-MEMORY for sources]: ~5-10% of KCs respond to a given odour (Turner, Bazhenov, Laurent, J. Neurophysiol. 99:734-746, 2008; Honegger, Campbell, Turner, J. Neurosci. 31:11772, 2011); APL feedback is required to keep it sparse and to keep odour discrimination (Lin et al., Nat. Neurosci. 17:559-568, 2014); dimensionality-optimal fan-in K ~ 7 for fly, ~4 for cerebellum (Litwin-Kumar et al., Neuron 93:1153-1164, 2017).
- FlyVec [VERIFIED-repo line 1069]: 400 Kenyon cells (200-600 tested), vocabulary 20,000, window 11, trained on OpenWebText ~6B tokens with the Krotov-Hopfield rule and a 1/p_i frequency weighting; 8-24 min per epoch on 3xV100 using 122-150 MB of GPU memory, vs ~10 h for GloVe on the same corpus and ~4 days on 16 TPUs for BERT-base; Spearman plateaus after 2-3 of 15 scheduled epochs. Krotov-Hopfield rule defaults p=2, k=2, delta=0.4, eps0=0.02; FlyVec uses delta=0, lr 2e-4, batch 10,000.
- BioHash retrieval [VERIFIED-repo line 1084]: MNIST mAP@all at hash length k=32 is 55.5% vs ITQ 43.6% vs LSH 26.2%; at k=16, 54.9 / 41.2 / 20.3; CIFAR-10 mAP@1000 at k=32, 24.0 vs ITQ 20.6 vs LSH 18.1. Cost caveat: codes need k log2(m) bits, not k bits.
- SDM as a continual learner [VERIFIED-repo line 1080]: Split MNIST task-free single pass with 1K neurons, SDMLP 0.69 vs MLP 0.665 vs MLP+streaming EWC 0.708; with 10K neurons a third-party table reports SDMLP 0.53 vs MLP 0.621 vs EMLP 0.802 vs FlyModel 0.91. ICLR 2023 reviewer scores 5/8/6/8 with 'heavy hyperparameter tuning' and 'too many modifications' noted.
- Fly-CL [VERIFIED-repo line 1082, ICLR 2026, arXiv 2510.16877]: frozen features -> fixed sparse random projection -> top-k -> streaming ridge with GCV. 10-task CIFAR-100 76.5 vs RanPAC 76.4 vs SimpleCIL 70.8; ImageNet-R 78.8 vs 78.6 vs 71.4; post-feature training time cut 38-93%. Gains of 1.17-2.38 percentage points, images only.
- Sparse memory at LM scale [FROM-MEMORY, re-verify all of these before citing]: Product Key Memory ~1M slots with top-k=32 via product keys, a 12-layer transformer plus PKM reported to beat a 24-layer transformer at roughly 2x faster inference (Lample et al., NeurIPS 2019, arXiv:1907.05242); Memory Layers at Scale reaches ~128B memory parameters and reports beating dense models trained with ~2x the compute on factual QA (Meta, arXiv:2412.09764); UltraMem reports ~6x faster inference than MoE at equal parameters (ICLR 2025, arXiv:2411.12364); PEER retrieves from >1M tiny experts (arXiv:2407.04153).
- Non-parametric memory gains [FROM-MEMORY]: kNN-LM WikiText-103 perplexity 15.79 vs 18.65 base with a ~100M-key datastore (arXiv:1911.00172); Memorizing Transformers use a ~262K-token kNN memory (arXiv:2203.08913); RETRO uses a 2T-token database (arXiv:2112.04426).
- Neuro-VSA on Raven's Progressive Matrices ~98.8% on I-RAVEN with large speed-ups (Hersche et al., Nature Machine Intelligence 5:363-375, 2023) [FROM-MEMORY]. HDC language identification of 21 languages at roughly 96-97% with n-gram hypervectors (Rahimi, Kanerva, Rabaey, ISLPED 2016) [FROM-MEMORY]. Both are the ceiling of published HDC symbolic results and neither is a language model.
- Compute-matched control baselines already in the project: phase-1 H1 sparse MB memory 0.981 vs compute-matched Gated DeltaNet 0.998; ring attractor 0.24 vs unstructured layer 0.67; phase-2 AIF-H1 K=3 inference steps beat K=0 by 0.026 bits/char = 0.7 seed-SD against a 2 seed-SD pre-registered bar [VERIFIED-repo, REFLECTION.md].

### Dead ends

- Dropping a Hopfield layer into a transformer and expecting a capacity win. Ramsauer's HopfieldLayer IS attention (the update rule is literally softmax(beta X^T xi) X), so the module is a reparameterisation, not an addition. The ml-jku/hopfield-layers repository has 1,967 stars and no reported language-modelling perplexity improvement [VERIFIED-github for the repo; the absence of an LM result is FROM-MEMORY and should be re-checked]. Evidence: the equivalence theorem itself.
- Expecting the sparse-modern-Hopfield line (Hu, Wu, Chen, Liu et al.) to deliver a language win. All six MAGICS-LAB repositories are real and peer-reviewed at NeurIPS 2023 / ICLR 2024 / ICML 2024 [VERIFIED-github], and every one targets multiple-instance learning, time series, tabular data or quantisation. Their contributions are tighter retrieval-error bounds, sparser attention patterns and better outlier behaviour for quantisation — not perplexity. OutEffHop is genuinely useful if the project ever needs to quantise, and nothing else in that line is on the critical path.
- Energy Transformers as a backbone. The Energy Transformer runs K gradient-descent steps on an energy per block, which is structurally the same bet phase 2 already lost: iterative inference on top of an amortised network, where the amortisation gap closes with training (AIF-H1 confirmed it at +0.7 seed-SD against a 2 SD bar). Published Energy Transformer evaluations are masked image completion and graph anomaly detection; there is no language-modelling result to build on. Re-running that bet with a different energy would repeat a known negative.
- Pure HDC / binary hyperdimensional language models. The blocker is information-theoretic, not engineering: sparsity multiplies item count while total stored information saturates at ~0.14-2 bits per synapse depending on the rule, so a binary hypervector cannot carry the dense semantic content a language model's residual stream carries. The strongest published HDC symbolic results are 21-language identification at ~96-97% and ~98.8% on I-RAVEN — both combinatorial-binding tasks, neither a generative model. No HDC-native LM with a perplexity competitive with a transformer at any scale is known to me, and the Kleyko surveys' own challenges sections list scaling to deep models as open.
- Hyperseed and the VSA unsupervised-learning line as an architecture. Hyperseed (Osipov, Kahawala, Haputhanthri, Kempitiya, De Silva, Alahakoon, Kleyko; IEEE TNNLS, arXiv:2110.08343) [FROM-MEMORY, venue/year uncertain] is a VSA analogue of a self-organising map, demonstrated on UCI-scale datasets and language identification. It is elegant and has no path to a language model. Park it.
- Random expansion of raw token one-hots. FlyVec's own ablation found RandExp lost to learned weights at every hash length [VERIFIED-repo line 1099]. The expansion must sit on top of a LEARNED low-dimensional projection of the residual stream (the antennal-lobe stage), never on raw tokens.
- Fixed random sparse hashing of un-whitened language-model states. Reported hard failure at 100k adversarially similar keys — fly-LSH, random hash and whitening all HARD_FAIL in the one preregistered attempt I can find [VERIFIED-repo line 1090, but a weak unreviewed GitHub source], with Babadi & Sompolinsky's noise-amplification result as the theory (relative noise grows as sqrt(2 ln(1/alpha)) while decorrelation gains grow only logarithmically). Treat whitening/decorrelation before hashing as mandatory, and treat the 100k-key regime as the known breaking point to measure rather than assume.
- Trying to beat attention on EXACT long-range retrieval or copying with any fixed-state superposition memory. There is a proof against it: a 2-layer transformer copies strings of length exponential in its width while any fixed-state recurrent model needs state proportional to the string length (Jelassi, Brandfonbrener, Kakade, Malach, ICML 2024, arXiv:2402.01032) [FROM-MEMORY]. Any claim of the form 'our memory replaces attention' is dead on arrival; the architecture must be a hybrid with a local exact attention window.
- Claiming a sparse-memory win at matched STATE BYTES. Recall capability is bounded by recurrent state size (Arora et al., Zoology / BASED) [FROM-MEMORY], so a sparse store with the same bytes as a dense one has no theoretical advantage and the experiment should be expected to come out null. This is worth running precisely because it is expected to be null — it is the control that makes the matched-compute result credible.
- Re-litigating the connectome as a source of structure. Phase 1 settled it: whole-graph mask indistinguishable from degree-preserving shuffle and Erdos-Renyi; real PN->KC wiring equal to random wiring at matched fan-in (H1d, -0.1 seed-SD); the 148-neuron central-complex ring tracks state far worse than an unstructured layer of equal size (0.24 vs 0.67). The connectome's remaining role is to supply and validate SCALARS (fan-in ~5-7, coding level ~5%, compartment count ~15-32, E/I ratio 30.8%), and to check them against derived optima such as Litwin-Kumar's K~7. That is a legitimate and modest role; anything more has been measured and found absent.
- Straight-through top-k as an unexamined default. SDMLP needed a 'GABA switch' (annealed k to avoid dead units), non-negative weights, L2-normalised inputs and weights, and SGD without momentum to train at all, and still lost to a plain MLP at 10K neurons (0.53 vs 0.621) [VERIFIED-repo line 1080]. Budget for this stability kit explicitly, or use product keys (Lample 2019) where the top-k is a search rather than a differentiable nonlinearity.
- Naive replay-based consolidation. The project's own toy already shows it: retention beyond the fast horizon goes from 0.02 to 0.06 with one replay per item into a rank-512 store — essentially nothing. Consolidation needs a selection rule, a real replay budget and a slow store larger than the stream, and it should not sit in the critical path of the main hypothesis.

### Open questions

- Does a k-of-n sparse address beat a dense d_key = k key at matched per-token compute when the address code is LEARNED end-to-end rather than fixed-random? All the capacity theory assumes i.i.d. random codes. Learned keys correlate, and correlation is exactly what destroys the near-orthogonality argument. There is no capacity theorem for a delta-rule store with learned correlated addresses, and phase-1 H1 (at P=8, far below either ceiling) could not test it. This is the first experiment the next phase should run, and the anti-Hebbian / separation-loss regulariser is the obvious control arm.
- What is the right coding level f for LANGUAGE memory, and does it differ by function? The fly's ~5% is an evolved compromise for odour discrimination. Task-dependent coding-level theory exists but has never been applied to next-token prediction, one-shot in-context binding or novelty detection separately [VERIFIED-repo line 1130]. A sweep of f over {0.1%, 0.5%, 2%, 5%, 10%} at fixed n on MQAR-style recall is cheap and would be a genuinely new measurement.
- At what memory size n does the top-k address search itself become the compute bottleneck, and does a product-key or ANN approximation to the top-k preserve the capacity advantage? The whole matched-compute claim depends on the address search being O(k log n) or better. This is an engineering question with a clean answer and it should be settled before any large run.
- Does block-code VSA binding (Frady, Kleyko & Sommer 2023) buy anything over RoPE for positional or role binding inside a memory row? Concretely: can one memory row hold R role-tagged values recoverable by unbinding, and does that multiply effective capacity by R without extra state? This is testable in isolation on a multi-value MQAR variant in hours, with no language model involved, and it is the one piece of the hyperdimensional literature that attention does not already contain.
- Is there a capacity theorem for a memory written by the delta rule with a precision-gated, data-dependent rate? The existing analyses assume a fixed learning rate or full convergence. The gate the project uses is beta_t = sigmoid(a log(pi_t ||e_t||^2/n) + b), and knowing its capacity behaviour under change points would turn an empirical result into a theoretical one — which is where a genuine scientific contribution could live.
- Do multiple compartments with distinct decay rates sharing one address code beat a single store with the same total parameters? This is the clearest architectural feature the mushroom body has that Gated DeltaNet does not, it costs nothing to test, and it has never been measured for language as far as I can tell.
- How do the recall-capacity gains interact with the standard zero-shot suite? The honest prediction is no perplexity change and no regression. If a perplexity gain appears, it is more likely a confound (extra parameters, extra state) than a discovery, and the pre-registration should say so in advance.
- Is the correct framing for the claim 'a better memory LAYER' (competing with Product Key Memory, UltraMem, Memory Layers at Scale on factual QA) or 'a better test-time-learning RULE' (competing with Gated DeltaNet, Titans, LaCT, TTT on long-horizon retention)? These have different baselines, different benchmarks and different novelty arguments, and the project cannot credibly claim both at 20M-125M parameters on one GPU. Choosing one is the most consequential open decision.
- Can the 100k-adversarial-key failure of fixed sparse hashing on language-model states be fixed by whitening alone, or does it need learned, separation-regularised addresses? The single data point available is weak and unreviewed; reproducing it properly with a whitening ablation would be a small, self-contained, publishable negative or positive result either way.

### Relevance to language models

"Direct answer to the brief's question — would a KC-style sparse binary code with VSA algebra give a language model anything attention does not already give? Mostly no, but there is one real yes, and it is narrow, quantitative and testable.\n\nThe noes first, because they are what kills most versions of this design. Exponential storage capacity: attention already has it (Ramsauer 2020 proves the softmax Hopfield update IS attention). Content-addressable retrieval by similarity: attention already has it, and softmax attention with normalised keys IS a Kanerva SDM read with a learned radius (Bricken & Pehlevan 2021). Binding and unbinding of role-filler pairs: linear attention IS the VSA outer-product memory, and the delta rule that fixes its capacity limit is already in DeltaNet and Gated DeltaNet, which are the project's own controls (Schlag 2021; Yang 2024, 2025). Exact long-range retrieval: provably out of reach for any fixed-state memory (Jelassi 2024). Recall at matched state bytes: bounded by state size, so no win is available there (Arora 2024). A design that argues from any of these five is arguing for something the baseline already has.\n\nThe one yes: a sparse address decouples capacity from per-token compute, and a dense fast-weight store cannot. A dense delta-rule store is rank-limited to d_key, which is also its per-token cost. A k-of-n store touches k rows but has rank ceiling n. At matched per-token compute (d_key = k) the sparse store therefore has an n/k times larger capacity ceiling, paid for in state bytes. The project has already measured this on its own: 0.82 versus 0.17 recall at 256 stored pairs, 0.70 versus 0.07 at 512, with k = 100 in both arms. That is a factor of four to ten at equal compute, and the crosstalk arithmetic (expected overlap k^2/n) predicts the knee where it was measured.\n\nWhat this means practically. The claim available here is 'sparse addressing converts state into recall more efficiently than widening a dense recurrent state, at constant per-token compute' — an economics claim about a tradeoff curve, not a capability claim. It is falsifiable, it has a mechanism, it has a matched control (Gated DeltaNet at equal FLOPs, and a second run at equal state bytes where the theory says the effect must vanish), and the project already has the numpy evidence that the effect is real in isolation.\n\nThe warning that must accompany it: this idea is not unexplored in ML. Product Key Memory (2019), PEER (2024), UltraMem (ICLR 2025) and Meta's Memory Layers at Scale (2025) are all sparse top-k key-value memories that work at scale, and the community reached them without the fly. So the novelty cannot be 'sparse hashed memory in a language model'. It has to be in the parts those systems do not have and the mushroom body does: (i) one-shot gradient-free writes at test time with a precision-inferred rate, (ii) several compartments sharing one address code but decaying at different rates, (iii) a global inhibitory unit holding the coding level constant so a fixed hash survives distribution shift, and (iv) selective replay into slow weights. Of those, (ii) and (iii) are cheap, anatomical, and as far as I can tell unmeasured for language — which makes them the best value per unit of GPU time the project has.\n\nFinally, the benchmark discipline. The capacity theory tells you where to look before you look: pick P around the dense control's ceiling. Phase 1's H1 tested 8 pairs against a d_key = 65 store — five times below the point where any difference could appear — and returned null, as it had to. Test at P in {8, 40, 200, 1000}, on MQAR plus a silent re-binding variant plus BABILong/RULER, and treat perplexity strictly as a no-regression check. Expect no perplexity gain; a perplexity gain would be evidence of a confound."


## Active inference and predictive processing after the FlyAI null: what has an algorithmic edge, what is only a description language

*Key: `active-inference-revisited`.*

### Summary

Brutally: active inference is, at the level of published evidence, a description language. Every mechanism in its vocabulary that has produced a measurable gain has an independently derived, better-benchmarked machine-learning twin, and in no case did the free-energy framing produce an algorithm that beat the twin. Variational free-energy descent maps to energy-based and looped transformers; precision maps to learned gain and temperature control; expected free energy maps to Bayesian experimental design plus KL-regularised RL, which is provably variational inference (Levine 2018; Korbak et al. 2022), so every RLHF'd model already minimises EFE's pragmatic term. There is exactly one exception, and it is worth the pivot: active inference insists that prediction errors be inverse-variance weighted and that the variance be inferred online. Every scaled ML twin uses unweighted errors - EBT's energy is an unweighted scalar, Titans' surprise is a raw gradient norm, standard predictive-coding implementations fix precision to the identity. Mathematically this matters: a Gamma prior on precision marginalises to a Student-t likelihood, and free-energy descent under it becomes IRLS, whose Hessian depends on the state - so iteration is genuinely non-redundant, unlike the quadratic objective FlyAI iterated on. FlyAI's v0.2 replaced exactly that Gamma tracker with a learned constant temperature, which made the null structurally inevitable; docs/aif/DESIGN.md section 1.1 recorded the quadratic problem before any run. The null is also uninformative on three further counts: 135k parameters (Mixture-of-Recursions only pays above 360M; EBT's smallest text run is 44M), unmatched controls (the 'wide' control had 1.94x params and 1.32x FLOPs), and AIF-H2 through H6 - precision gating, arousal, Lyapunov regulation, the Bayesian wind tunnel, EFE decoding - never ran at all. Three rules should govern the next phase: iterate only on an objective whose Hessian depends on the state; learn the update operator rather than deriving it; and evaluate under measured distribution shift at matched train-plus-inference FLOPs, because the amortisation gap is an out-of-distribution gap that joint training has already closed in-distribution. Drop the free-energy branding; keep precision, keep turn-level information gain, and pre-register phi and shift-slope as the metrics.

### Established (with sources)

- ELBO decomposition and the amortisation gap are well-defined and measurable. log p(x) = ELBO(q_phi) + KL(q_phi||p(z|x)); the inference gap splits into an approximation gap (log p(x) - ELBO(q*), q* the best member of the variational family) and an amortisation gap (ELBO(q*) - ELBO(q_phi) >= 0). Cremer, Li & Duvenaud, 'Inference Suboptimality in Variational Autoencoders', ICML 2018 (arXiv:1801.03558) showed the amortisation gap is frequently the LARGER of the two, and that it grows on harder and out-of-distribution datapoints. (Exact per-dataset nat values not re-verified this session: academic hosts blocked; treat the specific magnitudes as uncertain, the qualitative decomposition as established.)
- Closing the amortisation gap requires a LEARNED iterative update, not a hand-derived free-energy gradient. Marino, Yue & Mandt, 'Iterative Amortized Inference', ICML 2018 (arXiv:1807.09356) train an inference network that consumes the current estimate AND the gradient/error signal and emits an update; this beats both one-shot amortised inference and plain gradient-based SVI at matched iteration counts. Every subsequent system that wins with iteration (EBT, Huginn, Ouro, TTT) also learns its update operator. FlyAI ran hand-derived preconditioned gradient/Newton steps, which is the arm Marino's paper shows is the weak one.
- Joint training makes the generative model conform to the amortised encoder, which is why adding test-time iteration to a jointly-trained model gains almost nothing. The training objective is max_theta max_phi E_x[ELBO]; its optimum sits where the amortisation gap is already small, so the residual an extra K steps can eat has been minimised by training. FlyAI trained WITH K=3 in the loop, so the K=3 vs K=0 comparison was a comparison against a model that had already adapted. (Mathematically established from the objective; the empirical statement that gains are largest when the encoder is frozen or the distribution shifts is supported by Cremer 2018, Kim et al. 2018 and EBT's OOD result.)
- Semi-amortised inference for TEXT was tried and the honest result is: tiny likelihood gain, large qualitative gain (no posterior collapse), ~5x cost. Kim, Wiseman, Miller, Sontag & Rush, 'Semi-Amortized Variational Autoencoders', ICML 2018 (arXiv:1802.02550): a few SVI steps on top of the encoder output raise the KL term from ~0 to ~7 nats on Yahoo with a sub-nat NLL improvement. (Exact numbers recalled, not re-verified: uncertain.)
- The amortisation gap is the proximate cause of posterior collapse in text VAEs, not the KL term per se. He, Spokoyny, Neubig & Berg-Kirkpatrick, 'Lagging Inference Networks and Posterior Collapse in Variational Autoencoders', ICLR 2019 (arXiv:1901.05534): the inference network lags the generative model early in training; aggressively optimising the encoder fixes collapse without changing the objective. Original report: Bowman et al., 'Generating Sentences from a Continuous Space', CoNLL 2016 (arXiv:1511.06349).
- Predictive coding as a LEARNING rule has been tried on real text and loses on the autoregressive objective. Salvatori et al. (iPC), arXiv:2212.00720 / ICLR 2024, on One Billion Word (200k train / 10k dev, 10 seeds): BERT-style masked LM perplexity iPC 106.19+/-10.54 (10/10 seeds converged) vs BP 120.02+/-13.19 (7/10) vs vanilla PC 523.08+/-12.3 (3/10); conditional (causal) LM iPC 142.54+/-4.23 vs BP 113.32+/-0.36 vs PC 206.34+/-6.46. Gaussian PC on a transformer LM gives ppl 590.08+/-12.60 vs BP 162.64+/-0.76, recovering to 175.90+/-1.74 only with a categorical forward-KL energy (Pinchetti, Salvatori et al., NeurIPS 2022, arXiv:2211.03481).
- Theory now says PC converges to backprop in exactly the regimes where it is stable at scale. For linear residual networks the width- and depth-stable feature-learning parameterisations of PC coincide with BP's, and the equilibrated PC energy converges to the BP loss when width >> depth ('On the Infinite Width and Depth Limits of Predictive Coding Networks', arXiv:2602.07697, 2026, taken from the repo's own sweep — I could not re-verify it, treat the citation as moderately confident). Consistent with Innocenti, Achour & Buckley, 'muPC: Scaling Predictive Coding to 100+ Layer Networks', NeurIPS 2025 (arXiv:2505.13124) and with Zahid, Guo & Fountas (arXiv:2304.02658), who show exact-BP PC variants are lower-bounded in cost by BP.
- Iterative refinement in ACTIVATION space does work at scale, but only when the operator is learned and the compute accounting is honest. Energy-Based Transformers (Gladstone et al., arXiv:2507.02092, ICLR 2026 oral): up to 35% higher scaling rate than Transformer++ and +29% more benefit from inference-time iteration on language, at 3.3-6.6x training FLOPs, <=800M params. Recurrent depth: Huginn-3.5B (Geiping et al., arXiv:2502.05171) ARC-C 27.99 -> 38.23, HellaSwag 43.46 -> 65.21, MMLU 23.39 -> 31.38 going from r=4 to r=32 loops. Looped LMs: Ouro 1.4B/2.6B on 7.7T tokens (arXiv:2510.25741) match up to 12B models but at 2.63-6.75x inference FLOPs/token.
- Masked diffusion LMs are NOT iterative variational inference and should not be cited as evidence that iteration beats amortisation. Absorbing-state masked diffusion is an order-agnostic autoregressive factorisation; the denoiser at every step is a fully amortised network, and the 'iteration' is ancestral sampling from a learned joint, not a descent on a free energy over a latent. LLaDA-8B (Nie et al., arXiv:2502.09992) is trained from scratch on 2.3T tokens and is competitive with LLaMA3-8B; Dream-7B (Ye et al., arXiv:2508.15487) is initialised from an AR checkpoint (Qwen2.5-7B). Their real, measured edges are (a) any-order/parallel decoding and reversal-style tasks and (b) data efficiency in data-constrained, compute-abundant regimes (Prabhudesai et al., 'Diffusion Beats Autoregressive in Data-Constrained Settings', arXiv:2507.15857, which derives a closed-form critical-compute threshold).
- Expected free energy has produced measurable gains ONLY where it reduces to Bayesian experimental design / expected information gain in an action loop, never at the token level. Uncertainty of Thoughts (Hu et al., NeurIPS 2024): +57.8% average task-completion success over direct prompting on 20 Questions / medical diagnosis / troubleshooting across GPT-3.5, GPT-4, Llama-2-70B, Mistral-Large, Gemini-1.0-pro. Information-gain clarification (arXiv:2606.03135, ICML 2026): +3.7% task success for +0.3 interaction steps across five backbones on a clarification-augmented tau-Bench. ClarifyGPT: GPT-4 MBPP-sanitized pass@1 70.96% -> 80.80%. A GitHub-wide code search performed in the repo's own sweep (Sept 2026) found ZERO repositories combining expected_free_energy with transformers or vLLM.
- The pragmatic half of expected free energy is already deployed and is called RLHF. KL-regularised RL is exactly variational inference with the pretrained LM as prior and the reward as a log-likelihood (Todorov 2006; Kappen 2005; Rawlik, Toussaint & Vijayakumar 2012; Levine, 'Reinforcement Learning and Control as Probabilistic Inference', arXiv:1805.00909, 2018; Korbak, Perez & Buckley, 'RL with KL penalties is better seen as Bayesian inference', EMNLP Findings 2022, arXiv:2205.11275). So every instruction-tuned LM already minimises the pragmatic term; the only unclaimed part of EFE is the epistemic term, and that is Bayesian experimental design.
- Precision-as-attention (Feldman & Friston, 'Attention, uncertainty, and free-energy', Frontiers in Human Neuroscience 4:215, 2010; Parr & Friston, 'Working memory, attention, and salience in active inference', Scientific Reports 7:14678, 2017, with three precisions zeta sensory / omega transition / gamma policy) has an independently derived ML twin that does scale — but it was invented without the FEP and its winning form is a learned gate, not an inferred variance. Gated Attention (Qiu et al., NeurIPS 2025 best paper, arXiv:2505.06708): a query-dependent sigmoid gate on head outputs, validated across 30 variants of 1.7B dense and 15B MoE models on 3.5T tokens, shipped in Qwen3-Next. Related: Scalable-Softmax s*log(n) logit scaling (Nakanishi, arXiv:2501.19399, 2025); the softmax dispersion theorem (Velickovic et al., arXiv:2410.01104, 2024) proving max attention weight -> 0 as n -> infinity for bounded logits; attention entropy collapse and sigma-Reparam (Zhai et al., ICML 2023, arXiv:2303.06296).
- JEPA-style predictive latents are established for vision/video and thin for text. LeCun, 'A Path Towards Autonomous Machine Intelligence' (2022, OpenReview position paper); I-JEPA (Assran et al., CVPR 2023, arXiv:2301.08243); V-JEPA 2 (Assran et al., 2025, arXiv:2506.09985), a ~1B-parameter video model reporting state-of-the-art motion understanding and zero-shot robot planning via V-JEPA 2-AC. For language the transfer is weak: LLM-JEPA (Huang, LeCun & Balestriero, arXiv:2509.14252, 2025) adds an embedding-space predictive term with modest, dataset-dependent gains. The member of this family that DOES pay for text is multi-token prediction: Gloeckle et al., ICML 2024 (arXiv:2404.19737), 13B model solves 12% more HumanEval and 17% more MBPP problems; adopted as MTP in DeepSeek-V3.
- Multi-timescale hierarchical prediction is supported by human language neuroscience, but subtractive error neurons are contested. Caucheteux, Gramfort & King, Nature Human Behaviour 7:430-441 (2023): brain responses to speech are best explained by hierarchically organised long-range predictions spanning ~8 words ahead, and adding multi-timescale predictions to LMs improves brain mapping. Against: Lowet & Uchida (Current Biology 2024, via the repo's sweep) find mouse V1 'prediction errors' look like stimulus-specific adaptation/amplification rather than subtraction. Do not treat explicit subtractive error units as an anatomical fact.
- In the fly specifically, the one circuit with a formally Bayesian account is the central-complex ring, not the mushroom body: the EPG/Delta7 heading system has been modelled as a circular Kalman filter in which bump amplitude encodes certainty (Kutschireiter, Basnak, Wilson & Drugowitsch, 'Bayesian inference in ring attractor networks', PNAS 2023 - citation recalled, not re-verified this session, treat as moderately confident). FlyBrainLLM's Phase-1 ring result (0.24 vs 0.67 accuracy) tested the EXTRACTED 148-neuron wiring, not the circular-Kalman ALGORITHM; the algorithm was never tested. What IS established and non-Bayesian in the fly: divisive normalisation in the antennal lobe (Olsen, Bhandawat & Wilson, Neuron 2010) - gain control, not prediction error; and MBON->DAN feedback giving an RPE-shaped teaching signal in the mushroom body.
- The FlyAI AIF-H1 null is weaker evidence than it looks, for three specific reasons recorded in the repo's own files. (i) Scale: 135,232 parameters on TinyShakespeare is far below any scale at which iterative-refinement gains have ever been reported - EBT's smallest text run is 44M on 32B RedPajama-v2 tokens, Huginn is 3.5B, and Mixture-of-Recursions (Bae et al., NeurIPS 2025, arXiv:2507.10524) only matches vanilla Transformers above 360M. (ii) Controls: docs/aif/VERIFICATION.md records that flyai_K0_wide was over-provisioned (262,080 params, 1.88 MFLOPs/token) against flyai_K3 (135,232 params, 1.42 MFLOPs/token), so no FLOP-matched control existed in either direction. (iii) Mechanism: docs/aif/DESIGN.md section 1.1 records that the block's free energy is exactly quadratic in the fast latent, and v0.2 change #4 replaced the categorical head's Gamma-tracked precision with a LEARNED CONSTANT temperature - which removed the only residual-dependent term that could have made iteration non-redundant. The experiment was therefore near-guaranteed to be null on structural grounds.
- AIF-H2 through AIF-H6 were never run (RESULTS.md records '_not run_' for the precision write-gate under non-stationarity, arousal, Lyapunov regulation, the Bayesian wind tunnel and EFE decoding). The precision and EFE hypotheses - the two parts of active inference the literature marks as genuinely unexplored for LMs - are UNTESTED, not refuted.

### Principles and their translation

| principle | biology / mathematics | translation to a sequence model | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Iteration is only non-redundant when the free energy is non-quadratic in the latent. A Gaussian-linear level makes K gradient steps provably equivalent to one affine map. | For F(s) = (1/2) sum_l pi_l //s - mu_l//^2 with FIXED pi_l, dF/ds = sum_l pi_l (s - mu_l), so the unique minimiser is s* = (sum_l pi_l mu_l)/(sum_l pi_l): a single precision-weighted average. Any number of descent steps converges to a value a linear head could have emitted directly. This is exactly what docs/aif/DESIGN.md section 1.1 discovered and then, correctly, replaced with the closed form - after which there was nothing left for K to do. | Never put an iteration loop on a quadratic objective. Before building any iterative module, compute the Hessian of its objective w.r.t. the iterated variable and check it is not constant. Pre-register this as a gate: if d2F/ds2 does not depend on s, the module is deleted before any run. | established |
| THE ONE THING ACTIVE INFERENCE OFFERS THAT WAS NOT EXPLOITED: residual-dependent precision turns free-energy descent into IRLS on a Student-t likelihood, i.e. a robust, heavy-tail-aware predictor. This is a genuine algorithmic difference, not a renaming. | Put a Gamma(nu/2, nu/(2 pi_0)) prior on the precision pi and marginalise it: the Gaussian scale mixture is exactly a Student-t likelihood with nu degrees of freedom. The EM/variational fixed point alternates pi_t = (nu + n)/(nu/pi_0 + //e_t//^2) and a reweighted least-squares step on s - this is iteratively reweighted least squares. The Hessian now depends on s, so iteration is genuinely non-redundant. Friston's 'Variational free energy and the Laplace approximation' (NeuroImage 34:220-234, 2007) treats precision hyperparameters exactly this way; the repo's AXIOMS.md AX3 wrote the Gamma update down, and DESIGN.md v0.2 change #4 then removed it from the only loop that ran. | A language-model head whose token-level residual is weighted by an ONLINE-INFERRED precision is a robust (Student-t) objective, and the K-step loop is IRLS, not gradient descent. Concretely: keep the Gamma tracker (a_t = rho a_{t-1} + n/2, b_t = rho b_{t-1} + //e_t//^2/2, pi_t = a_t/b_t) on the residual that actually feeds the loop, and compare against (i) a fixed learned temperature and (ii) a learned input-dependent gate, at matched FLOPs. The claim to pre-register: the inferred-precision arm wins on heavy-tailed / shifted / non-stationary text and ties in-distribution. Nobody has run this - the repo's own sweep records 'No iterative-inference LM uses precision-weighted (inverse-variance) prediction errors' and 'Titans uses raw gradient norm as surprise; whether a properly variance-normalised surprise improves memorisation/forgetting is unexplored'. | promising |
| The iterative update must be a LEARNED operator, not a derived gradient. This is the single most reliable discriminator between iterative-inference systems that work and ones that do not. | Marino, Yue & Mandt (ICML 2018, arXiv:1807.09356) give the mechanism: a learned update f(mu_t, dF/dmu_t) can encode curvature, step size and problem structure that a fixed-step descent cannot, and can converge in 2-5 steps where SVI needs hundreds. Biologically this is the claim that a cortical/central-complex microcircuit implements a learned inference operator, not a numerically exact gradient. | Replace 'K steps of preconditioned descent on F' with 'K applications of a small learned block that receives (current estimate, precision-weighted error, log-precision) and emits a delta'. This is what EBT, Huginn, Ouro and Mixture-of-Recursions all do under other names. Budget the cost honestly: the recurrence-equivalence exponent is phi = 0.46 (arXiv:2604.21106), i.e. one recurrence buys less than half a unique block at matched training compute. | established |
| The payoff of test-time iteration is an OUT-OF-DISTRIBUTION and ADAPTATION payoff, not an in-distribution likelihood payoff. This defines the only regime in which a defensible claim is available. | Cremer et al. 2018 show the amortisation gap grows on harder/atypical datapoints; the joint-training argument says the in-distribution gap has already been trained away. EBT reports a roughly linear relationship between OOD shift magnitude and the gain from 'thinking longer' (arXiv:2507.02092). iP-VAE (Vafaii, Galor & Yates, NeurIPS 2025, arXiv:2410.19315) reports stronger OOD generalisation than hybrid iterative-amortised VAEs. Test-time training on ARC gives 53.0% (8B) and 61.9% ensembled, up to 6x over fine-tuned baselines (Akyurek et al., arXiv:2411.07279) - all of it a shift/adaptation gain. | Pre-register the primary metric on HELD-OUT-DOMAIN text at matched train+inference FLOPs, with in-distribution perplexity as a secondary metric that is allowed to tie or lose slightly. State the hypothesis as 'gain scales with a measured shift statistic (e.g. KL between train and eval unigram/embedding distributions, or per-token energy)'. A positive slope is a novel, publishable, falsifiable claim even if absolute perplexity is not SOTA. | promising |
| Precision is attention, and the open seam is uncertainty-conditioning, not the gate itself. Do not reinvent the gate; make it a function of inferred uncertainty and beat the query-conditioned gate. | Feldman & Friston 2010 identify attention with optimisation of the precision of sensory prediction errors; Parr & Friston 2017 split precision into sensory (zeta), transition (omega) and policy (gamma). In the fly, neuromodulators are the substrate: DANs gate mushroom-body plasticity and octopamine/serotonin set global gain (Doya's 2002 mapping of four neuromodulators to four RL meta-parameters is the cleanest statement of the analogy). | Take Gated Attention (Qiu et al., NeurIPS 2025, arXiv:2505.06708) EXACTLY as the baseline and control - a best-paper mechanism deployed in Qwen3-Next, so beating it is a real claim - and change one thing: make the sigmoid gate a function of an inferred precision (running inverse variance of the head's residual, or attention entropy, or the Gamma posterior above) instead of, or in addition to, the query. The repo's own sweep names this as an untouched gap: 'No work makes the Gated-Attention sigmoid gate a function of inferred uncertainty rather than the query alone.' Guard against entropy collapse with a sigma-Reparam-style spectral bound (Zhai et al., ICML 2023) or a hyperprior on log-precision; guard against length dispersion with s*log(n) scaling (Nakanishi 2025). | promising |
| Expected free energy is Bayesian experimental design with a preference prior. Its gains are real, are at the TURN/ACTION level, and must be cost-normalised. | EFE G(pi) = -E[ln p(o/C)] (pragmatic/risk) - E[I(s;o/pi)] (epistemic/ambiguity), with policy posterior p(pi) = sigma(-gamma G(pi)). In deterministic settings the epistemic term reduces exactly to expected information gain, E_y[KL(p(theta/y,x) // p(theta))] - the classical Lindley/Chaloner-Verdinelli BED criterion. Nothing in the FEP is needed to derive it. | Score ask / retrieve / tool-call / stop actions by EIG minus token cost, not tokens by EFE. Baselines that must be beaten: UoT (+57.8% over direct prompting), CA-BED (+21.8% for +1.8 turns), DEER confidence early-exit (CoT length -19.1% to -80.1% with accuracy +0.3 to +5.0 pts), and plain entropy thresholds. The genuinely open engineering problem is an AMORTISED EFE head that predicts the epistemic term from hidden state instead of rolling out (Millidge 2020 style), validated against rollout EIG - the repo's sweep records that no such head exists for LMs. | established |
| Halting should be read off the DYNAMICS, not learned as a gate; and every iteration should be supervised. | A learned exit distribution that also weights per-depth losses entangles exit selection with trajectory formation, so the gradient for 'when to stop' fights the gradient for 'what to compute' (arXiv:2607.20519, 2026). Dynamics-based criteria - second-order difference of step size (arXiv:2509.23314), or per-step displacement small relative to the decoder margin (arXiv:2608.18222) - are decoupled and provably depth-safe. | Train with fixed-prior depth supervision (deep supervision at every iteration - independent ARC Prize analysis of HRM found this, not the hierarchy, was the driver; a 2-layer Tiny Recursive Model, Jolicoeur-Martineau arXiv:2510.04871, generalises better than HRM). Halt at inference on a displacement criterion. FlyAI's adaptive-K never fired (K_mean = 3.00 in every run), which is precisely the predicted failure of a learned gate. | established |
| Weight-space iteration and activation-space iteration are different mechanisms, and unifying them under ONE precision-weighted objective is the clearest unclaimed piece of territory. | Activation iteration refines a belief within a token; weight iteration writes a memory that persists across tokens. In the fly these are anatomically distinct: recurrent central-complex dynamics (belief) versus dopamine-gated KC->MBON synaptic depression (memory). A single free energy over (state, fast weights) with a shared inferred precision is the natural formal object and does not exist in the literature. | One objective F(s, M) = precision-weighted prediction error, descended in s for K steps per token AND in M once per token with the SAME precision as the write gate. Baselines: Gated DeltaNet / Titans (arXiv:2501.00663) / ATLAS (arXiv:2505.23735) for the weight half, Huginn/Ouro for the activation half. The repo's sweep states plainly: 'No system unifies activation-space iteration with weight-space iteration under a single objective; whether the two forms of test-time compute are complementary or redundant is unknown.' | promising |
| Hierarchical generative models for text pay off when the hierarchy is over SEGMENTS with learned boundaries, not when it is a continuous Gaussian latent. | Continuous-latent VAE LMs collapse because a sufficiently strong autoregressive decoder can ignore z at negligible ELBO cost (Bowman 2016; Chen et al. 'Variational Lossy Autoencoder', ICLR 2017, arXiv:1611.02731 gives the bits-back argument for why). Discrete or structural latents do not have this failure mode. Multi-timescale prediction has direct brain support (Caucheteux 2023, ~8 words ahead). | The working versions for text are: multi-token prediction heads (Gloeckle et al., ICML 2024, arXiv:2404.19737); H-Net dynamic chunking (Hwang, Wang & Gu, arXiv:2507.07955, 2025), which learns segment boundaries end-to-end and outperforms BPE transformers; Byte Latent Transformer (Pagnoni et al., arXiv:2412.09871, 2024) with entropy-based patching - note that the patching criterion is literally a surprise threshold, i.e. precision-driven segmentation. Do NOT build a Gaussian-latent VAE-LM; the field's own verdict is that these remain small-scale and beat only minimal baselines. | established |
| Predictive coding is worth keeping as an INFERENCE-TIME filter, never as the learning rule. | Temporal predictive coding with local Hebbian rules approximates Kalman filtering for linear-Gaussian dynamics and extends to nonlinear sequences (Millidge, Tang, Osanlouy, Harper & Bogacz, PLOS Computational Biology 20(4):e1011183, 2024). As a learning rule it either loses to BP on autoregressive text or converges to BP in the stable regimes. | Use PC only for the online/test-time pathway: warm-start latents from the previous token (temporal amortisation halves inference steps), run a small learned update, and train everything by backprop. Report gradient cosine to BP, inference steps per update, wall-clock vs a BP twin, and the per-layer energy distribution - the PCX/PC-ALM diagnostic set. | established |
| Any-order factorisation is the iterative-refinement branch with an actual scaled result, and it buys DATA efficiency, not inference-time intelligence. | Masked/absorbing-state diffusion optimises an ELBO that is a weighted sum of masked-token cross-entropies over all masking ratios, which equals an expectation over random generation orders. More compute per token is spent because each token is predicted many times under different contexts, which is a regulariser and an augmentation, not a posterior refinement. | If the constraint is a fixed corpus and abundant compute (which is the realistic situation for a small research group), a masked-diffusion objective is the principled way to convert compute into likelihood: Prabhudesai et al. (arXiv:2507.15857) derive the critical compute threshold beyond which diffusion overtakes AR in data-constrained settings. This is an honest, defined regime in which a state-of-the-art claim is achievable. It has nothing to do with active inference; do not dress it up as such. | established |

### Numbers an engineer can use

- Amortisation gap: defined as ELBO(q*) - ELBO(q_phi) >= 0 nats; reported as frequently exceeding the approximation gap in trained VAEs and as growing on harder/OOD datapoints (Cremer, Li & Duvenaud, ICML 2018, arXiv:1801.03558). Exact per-dataset nat values not verified at the time of writing.
- Predictive coding on One Billion Word, BERT-style masked LM, 200k train / 10k dev, 10 seeds: iPC ppl 106.19 +/- 10.54 (10/10 seeds converged), BP 120.02 +/- 13.19 (7/10), vanilla PC 523.08 +/- 12.3 (3/10). Conditional (causal) LM: iPC 142.54 +/- 4.23 (7/10), BP 113.32 +/- 0.36 (10/10), PC 206.34 +/- 6.46 (10/10). Source: Salvatori et al., arXiv:2212.00720 (ICLR 2024).
- PC on a transformer LM with Gaussian output energy: test ppl 590.08 +/- 12.60 vs BP 162.64 +/- 0.76; with a forward-KL (categorical) energy, 175.90 +/- 1.74. Source: Pinchetti, Salvatori et al., NeurIPS 2022, arXiv:2211.03481. Implication: any token-level PC head MUST use a categorical energy.
- Energy-Based Transformers: up to 35% higher scaling rate than Transformer++ w.r.t. data, batch size, parameters, FLOPs and depth (log-log slope); +29% more benefit from inference-time iteration on language than Transformer++, which shows none; training cost 3.3x-6.6x the FLOPs of a standard Transformer; largest model 800M; smallest text run 44M on 32B RedPajama-v2 tokens. Source: Gladstone et al., arXiv:2507.02092.
- Huginn-3.5B recurrent depth (3.5B params, 800B tokens), r=4 -> r=32 loops: ARC-Challenge 27.99 -> 38.23, HellaSwag 43.46 -> 65.21, MMLU 23.39 -> 31.38; GSM8K improves only marginally. Latency scales linearly with loop count. Source: Geiping et al., arXiv:2502.05171.
- Ouro (LoopLM) 1.4B/2.6B on 7.7T tokens, 4 recurrent steps, vs Qwen3-4B: BBH 71.02 vs 70.95, GSM8K 78.92 vs 72.86, MATH500 82.40 vs 59.60 - but inference FLOPs/token are 6.75x Qwen3-1.7B and 2.63x Qwen3-4B, so the comparison is parameter-matched, not FLOP-matched. Source: arXiv:2510.25741.
- Iso-depth scaling law for looped LMs: recurrence-equivalence exponent phi = 0.46 over r in {1,2,4,8} and ~50x training compute (phi = 1 would mean a recurrence is worth a unique block). A 410M looped model at r=4 matches a 580M dense model but costs as much to train as a 1B dense model. Truncated backprop through loops lowers phi to 0.38; hyperconnections raise it to 0.65. Source: arXiv:2604.21106 (from the repo's sweep; not independently verified).
- Mixture-of-Recursions (135M-1.7B, recursion 3, ~1/3 unique params): matches or exceeds vanilla Transformers at equal training FLOPs only for models ABOVE 360M, across isoFLOP budgets 2e18, 5e18, 16.5e18; MoR-4 gives up to 2.06x throughput with continuous depth-wise batching. Source: Bae et al., NeurIPS 2025, arXiv:2507.10524. This is the sharpest available statement of the minimum scale at which activation iteration starts paying.
- LLaDA-8B: masked diffusion LM trained from scratch on 2.3T tokens, reported competitive with LLaMA3-8B (which used ~15T tokens). Source: Nie et al., arXiv:2502.09992; 2.3T figure verified from github.com/ML-GSAI/LLaDA. Individual benchmark deltas NOT verified (README shows only charts). Follow-ups listed on that repo: LLaDA 1.5 (VRPO preference alignment, May 2025), LLaDA-MoE-7B-A1B (~1B active params, described as surpassing LLaDA 1.5 and comparable to Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct, Sept 2025), iLLaDA-8B (June 2026).
- Dream-7B: arXiv:2508.15487 (verified from github.com/DreamLM/Dream). Initialised from an autoregressive checkpoint (Qwen2.5-7B) - benchmark numbers and token count not retrievable at the time of writing; treat specific figures as uncertain.
- Test-time weight learning: Titans (Behrouz et al., arXiv:2501.00663) 170M/340M/400M/760M on 15B-30B FineWeb-Edu tokens, surprise = gradient of the memory loss plus momentum and decay. ATLAS (arXiv:2505.23735) 340M-1.3B on 15B-100B tokens keeps >80% BABILong accuracy at 10M context where Titans degrades. TTT-E2E (arXiv:2512.23675) 3B on 164B tokens: loss scales with context like full attention, 2.7x faster inference at 128K, 3.4x slower training at 8K. Titans replication caveat: a probe at embedding dim 128 with N=10/25/50 facts found 100% training-loss convergence but only 0-40% free-form and 45-85% forced-choice retrieval (arXiv:2603.17781).
- Test-time training on ARC: 53.0% with an 8B LM, 61.9% ensembled with program synthesis, up to 6x over fine-tuned baselines - matching average human performance. Source: Akyurek et al., arXiv:2411.07279.
- Expected-information-gain gains in LM agents: UoT +57.8% average task-completion success over direct prompting (20 Questions / medical diagnosis / troubleshooting, five model families; NeurIPS 2024). CA-BED +21.8% success for +1.8 turns. ClarifyGPT: GPT-4 MBPP-sanitized pass@1 70.96% -> 80.80%. Information-gain clarification: +3.7% success for +0.3 steps across five backbones (arXiv:2606.03135). DEER confidence early exit (threshold 0.95): CoT length -19.1% to -80.1% with accuracy +0.3 to +5.0 points across 11 reasoning models.
- Multi-token prediction on code: a 13B model trained with 4 future-token heads solves 12% more HumanEval and 17% more MBPP problems than the next-token baseline. Source: Gloeckle et al., ICML 2024, arXiv:2404.19737.
- Precision/gain control at scale: Gated Attention validated across 30 variants of 15B-parameter MoE and 1.7B dense models trained on 3.5T tokens; NeurIPS 2025 best paper; deployed in Qwen3-Next-80B-A3B. Source: Qiu et al., arXiv:2505.06708. Softmax dispersion theorem: for bounded logits the maximum attention coefficient -> 0 as the number of attended items n -> infinity, for ANY trained circuit (Velickovic et al., arXiv:2410.01104). Attention entropy lower bound decreases exponentially with the spectral norm of the attention logits (Zhai et al., ICML 2023, arXiv:2303.06296).
- Neuromodulated plasticity, the honest historical baseline: Backpropamine (Miconi et al., ICLR 2019) reached Penn Treebank test ppl 61.44 vs 62.48 for a larger-parameter LSTM - about 1.7 ppl, and the lineage produced no scaling results afterwards. Any 'dopamine-gated fast weights' proposal must beat this precedent's trajectory, not just its number.
