- FlyAI AIF-H1 as run (repo RESULTS.md, commit 64a1bf3, 4 CPU cores): flyai_K3 val 2.7998 +/- 0.0106 bits/char (135,232 params, K_mean 2.9994); flyai_K0 2.8258 +/- 0.0352 (135,232 params); flyai_K0_wide 2.7931 +/- 0.0107 (262,080 params). K3 vs K0 = +0.0259 bpc = +0.7 seed-SD; K3 vs K0_wide = -0.0068 bpc = -0.6 seed-SD. Verified FLOPs: 1.42 (K3) vs 1.88 (K0_wide) MFLOPs/token - i.e. the 'wide' control had 1.94x the parameters and 1.32x the FLOPs, so neither control was matched.
- Fly biology numbers relevant to the precision story (from the repo's connectome work and cited primary sources): antennal-lobe divisive normalisation with exponent n ~ 1.5 compresses a 57x ORN drive range to 6.6x in PNs (Olsen, Bhandawat & Wilson, Neuron 2010). Mushroom body: 1,927 KCs, 34 MBON types, 158 DANs across 15-16 compartments; uniglomerular olfactory PNs supply 63.6% of KC input; ~5% KC coding level. MaleCNS v1.0: 166,700 neurons, 10.5M edges, 30.8% inhibitory.
- Predictive coding compute reality: even the optimised PCX JAX implementation costs 5.5 s/epoch vs 3.2 s for backprop on CIFAR-100 VGG-5 (A100), and 29 s vs 10 s on a 161M-parameter AlexNet; PC only approaches BP speed when inference steps ~ depth (Pinchetti et al., ICLR 2025, arXiv:2407.01163). PCNs degrade beyond ~5-7 layers without depth fixes.
- Data-constrained diffusion crossover: masked diffusion overtakes autoregressive training only beyond a closed-form critical compute threshold, the regime informally summarised as needing roughly an order of magnitude (~16x) more compute for the same data. Source: Prabhudesai et al., arXiv:2507.15857. Treat the '16x' as a framing number, not a measured constant.

### Dead ends

- Hand-derived free-energy gradient descent on a latent whose objective is quadratic. Evidence: the closed-form minimiser s* = sum_l pi_l mu_l / sum_l pi_l makes K steps equivalent to one affine map; the repo's own DESIGN.md section 1.1 established this before the runs, and the AIF-H1 null (+0.7 seed-SD, CI excludes 0 but below the 2-SD bar) is the predicted outcome. Do not rebuild this.
- Predictive coding as the LEARNING rule for an autoregressive language model. Evidence: iPC loses to BP on the causal objective (142.54 vs 113.32 ppl) even at BERT-small scale; Gaussian PC on a transformer LM gives 590 vs 163 ppl; theory says stable PC parameterisations coincide with BP's and equilibrated PC gradients converge to BP's; exact-BP PC variants are lower-bounded in cost by BP (arXiv:2304.02658). No PC decoder-only LM has been published since 2024.
- Continuous-Gaussian-latent VAE language models under an ELBO/VFE objective. Evidence: posterior collapse documented since Bowman et al. 2016 with only partial fixes (KL annealing, free bits, aggressive encoder training); bits-back argument (Chen et al., ICLR 2017) explains why a strong AR decoder can ignore z at negligible cost; 2025 GP-VAE LMs beat only 'minimal' transformer baselines on WikiText-2 in short-context regimes. This is the single most reliably null branch in the whole space.
- Token-level expected-free-energy decoding with enumerated policies or rollouts. Evidence: a GitHub-wide code search in Sept 2026 found zero repositories combining expected_free_energy with transformers or vLLM; rollout-based EIG costs N candidates x J depth x |possibility set| forward passes per token; deep active inference scaled only to small control tasks with explicit transition models (Tschantz et al. 2020; Millidge 2020). One project (pratyabhijna-world-model) coupled an EFE world model to a frozen 120B LLM via token biases and reported an honest NEGATIVE effect size of -0.47 on English text.
- Learned halting gates that also weight per-depth losses. Evidence: they entangle exit selection with trajectory formation and underperform fixed-prior depth supervision on modular arithmetic/parity and on Ouro-1.4B/2.6B checkpoints (arXiv:2607.20519); CALM-style confidence early exit was worse than a static-exit baseline on every summarisation dataset tested (arXiv:2509.05915); AdaPonderLM's learned policy saves only ~10% inference compute. FlyAI's adaptive-K never fired (K_mean = 3.00 in every run) - the same failure.
- Arousal or global precision implemented as a scalar multiplier of the free energy. Evidence: the repo's own design panel found it nearly inert because the preconditioned step divides by gamma*P + 1, so gamma cancels for P >> 1 and the adaptive-K test is scale-invariant - the experiment would have compared the model with itself. Any global gain must enter a term it does not cancel from (a write gate, a temperature, a plasticity rate).
- 'Active inference' as a wrapper around a frozen LLM (pymdp-style discrete agents with hand-specified A/B/C/D matrices). Evidence: Multi-LLM AIF (arXiv:2412.10425) reports only qualitative emergence with no baseline; Orchestrator (arXiv:2509.05651) uses toy mazes; the npj Digital Medicine 2025 'active inference' therapist paper computes no free energy at all. None touches the LM's objective. Do not cite these as evidence for anything.
- Bio-inspired mechanism stacking without per-mechanism ablations. Evidence: bio_inspired_nanochat (2026) added vesicle-depletion fatigue, fast/slow weights, Hebbian consolidation, neuromodulator scalars, MoE lifecycle and uncertainty-gated decoding to nanochat and reported, at 2 layers x 64 dims on CPU, fast-weights dECE ~ +0.03, split/merge regressing loss on 8/8 seeds, zero lifecycle events and 4-18x slowdown; the author's own verdict is that the mechanisms are 'built, not shown to help'. This is the failure mode FlyBrainLLM Phase 2 is closest to.
- Sub-1M-parameter models as evidence about iterative refinement. Evidence: Mixture-of-Recursions only matches vanilla Transformers above 360M parameters; EBT's smallest text run is 44M on 32B tokens; Huginn is 3.5B. A 135k-parameter model on TinyShakespeare cannot falsify - or support - any of these mechanisms. Phase 3 must either run at >=100M or restrict its claims to synthetic tasks where the mechanism is isolated and the scale is irrelevant by construction.
- Treating explicit subtractive prediction-error neurons as an anatomical fact. Evidence: mouse V1 data fit stimulus-specific adaptation and amplification better than subtraction (Lowet & Uchida, Current Biology 2024); pre-onset 'pre-activation' encoding results have been contested in re-analysis. In Drosophila specifically, no subtractive error population has been demonstrated; what exists is divisive normalisation (gain control) and RPE-shaped MBON->DAN feedback.
- Dopamine-as-precision as a design axiom. Evidence: it rests on discrete-state simulations and a single fMRI study, has never been realised in a trained ML model with baselines, and is behaviourally near-indistinguishable from reward-prediction-error accounts (FitzGerald et al. 2015). It can be an ablation arm; it cannot be a premise.
- Expecting adaptive per-token depth to give length or complexity extrapolation. Evidence: ANIRA (ICML 2026, arXiv:2602.08864) shows compute allocation does track token complexity without difficulty supervision, yet models still fail to extrapolate to unseen input sizes; 'overthinking' actively degrades predictions in recurrent-depth models (arXiv:2604.07822).
- Naive weight-tied Universal Transformers / ACT / PonderNet for language modelling. Evidence: never competitive on parameter-dominated LM tasks (the parameter-compute ratio problem) until MoEUT added MoE capacity, and even then the gain over standard Transformers on BLiMP/PIQA was slight (Csordas et al., arXiv:2405.16039).

### Open questions

- Does a residual-dependent, online-inferred precision (Gamma posterior -> Student-t likelihood -> IRLS) beat (a) a fixed learned temperature and (b) a learned input-dependent gate, at matched parameters and FLOPs, on heavy-tailed or distribution-shifted text? This is the one place where 'we did not exploit active inference' is literally true rather than a consolation, and FlyAI removed the term (DESIGN.md v0.2 change #4) before running the loop. Cheap to test at 100M scale.
- Does a variance-normalised surprise gate beat Titans' raw gradient-norm surprise for a test-time memory, specifically under NON-STATIONARITY (re-binding, topic switches, streaming distribution shift)? The repo's Phase-1 H1 found a learned gate beat the error gate on stationary MQAR (0.9966 vs 0.9814) - but the claimed regime was never tested, and AIF-H2 was never run.
- Does making the Gated-Attention sigmoid a function of inferred uncertainty (running inverse residual variance, attention entropy, or a Gamma-tracked precision) beat the query-conditioned gate at >=1B scale? The baseline is a NeurIPS 2025 best paper shipped in Qwen3-Next, so a positive result is a real claim and a negative result is still publishable.
- Does the benefit of learned iterative refinement scale linearly with a MEASURED distribution-shift statistic, at matched train+inference FLOPs, on natural text? EBT reports this relationship for vision-style shifts; nobody has measured it on held-out-domain language with a pre-registered shift metric. A positive slope with a null at zero shift would be a clean, novel, honest contribution.
- Are activation-space iteration and weight-space iteration complementary or redundant? No system has unified them under a single objective. If they are complementary, F(s, M) with a shared precision is a genuinely new architecture; if redundant, that is itself a useful negative result at a scale where people care.
- Do structural biological priors (Dale's law, 30.8% inhibitory, connectome-derived sparsity) change the recurrence-equivalence exponent phi (currently 0.46), the fixed-point stability, or the settling regime of an iterated operator? This is the ONLY formulation in which the connectome could still matter after the Phase-1 null, because it is a question about dynamics, not about wiring carrying information. Pre-register phi as the metric.
- Is the fly ring attractor's circular-Kalman ALGORITHM (amplitude = certainty, von Mises natural parameters, evidence-weighted bump update) useful as a latent state module, independent of the extracted wiring that failed in Phase 1 (0.24 vs 0.67)? The algorithm was never tested; only the anatomy was.
- Can the epistemic term of EFE be AMORTISED - a small head predicting expected information gain from hidden state - and validated against rollout EIG on held-out data? This is the concrete missing piece between the conceptual AIF-LM literature and the benchmarked BED literature, and it is affordable.
- Under what corpus-size and compute budget does a masked-diffusion objective overtake autoregressive training for a fixed research-scale corpus? Prabhudesai et al. give a closed-form threshold; nobody has measured where it sits for a ~10-50B-token corpus at 100M-1B parameters. This is a defined regime where a state-of-the-art claim is attainable without any biological story.
- Does segment-level hierarchy with learned boundaries (H-Net dynamic chunking, entropy-based patching in Byte Latent Transformer) plus multi-token prediction reproduce the ~8-word-ahead multi-timescale prediction structure that best explains brain responses to speech (Caucheteux 2023)? If so, the 'hierarchical generative model' axiom survives the pivot in a form that has scaled results behind it.
- Is there any regime in which a categorical forward-KL PC head beats a standard softmax head at matched compute? Pinchetti et al. showed FKL recovers most of the gap to BP (175.9 vs 162.6 ppl) but nobody has checked whether the surviving difference buys anything - e.g. calibration, OOD detection, or robustness - that justifies the loss.

### Relevance to language models

Directly relevant, but almost entirely as a set of constraints on what NOT to build. For a language model the conditional distribution p(o_t | o_{<t}) IS the model's output; there is no latent posterior to refine unless one is deliberately constructed and is genuinely under-determined. That is why iterative variational inference has repeatedly been null for LMs (iPC loses to BP on the causal objective 142.5 vs 113.3 ppl; no PC decoder-only LM has been published; no EFE decoder exists in any public codebase) while the iterative mechanisms that DO work at LM scale - EBT energy descent, Huginn/Ouro recurrent depth, Mixture-of-Recursions, masked diffusion, test-time training - all learn their update operator and none weights its errors by an inferred precision. The three AIF ideas with a live path into a language model are: (1) precision as an inferred, residual-dependent quantity, which converts the free energy into a Student-t objective and the K-loop into IRLS - the only mathematically non-redundant form of iteration available, and the exact term FlyAI deleted; (2) the same precision as the gate on a test-time memory write and on attention head outputs, where the strongest existing baselines (Gated Attention at 15B/3.5T tokens; Titans' raw-gradient surprise) conspicuously use unweighted signals; (3) expected free energy at the turn level, which is Bayesian experimental design and already delivers +3.7% to +57.8% on information-seeking tasks. Everything else in the AIF vocabulary maps onto something the field built without it: hierarchical generative model -> segment-level latents and multi-token prediction; pragmatic value -> KL-regularised RLHF, which is provably variational inference; action-perception loop -> tool use and retrieval. The regime in which a defensible claim remains available for this project is distribution shift and non-stationarity at matched compute, at >=100M parameters - not in-distribution perplexity, and not at 135k parameters on TinyShakespeare.


## The 2026 small/medium-scale SOTA landscape and what would count as a claimable result for a fly-derived memory/learning design

*Key: `sota-landscape-2026`.*

### Summary

First, a warning the next designer must read: primary research sources could not be reached when this note was written, so everything below is from background knowledge (as of May 2026) plus the repository's own sweep. Nothing from June-September 2026 is covered, and the low-confidence items are flagged. Verify before publishing.

The 2026 landscape at 125M-3B has one shape: hybrids, 3:1 to 8:1 linear-or-memory layers to softmax attention. No pure sub-quadratic model is SOTA anywhere. Gated DeltaNet (ICLR 2025, arXiv 2412.06464) is the correct primary control; Mamba-2, DeltaNet, RWKV-7 and mLSTM are the secondary set; Titans, ATLAS, TTT and LaCT are the test-time-memory frontier. Absolute SOTA at this scale is a data claim (2T-36T tokens) and is unreachable on one 16 GB GPU, so the only honest claim is a matched-token, matched-state, single-variable comparison reported as a Pareto frontier.

Which axis can a fly-derived design plausibly win? Not perplexity per FLOP - concede that in writing. The defensible axis is capacity and retention per bit of recurrent state, which is the axis the field already uses (Zoology, ICLR 2024; Based, ICML 2024) and the axis the fly was optimised on. Two pieces of established mathematics the repo has not yet used carry the case: Dambre et al. (Sci Rep 2:514, 2012) proves total capacity equals state dimension, making per-bit efficiency the only well-posed claim; Benna & Fusi (Nat Neurosci 19:1697-1706, 2016) proves a coupled geometric-timescale chain gives memory lifetime linear in N with power-law SNR decay, versus sqrt(N) and exponential. No test-time-learning LM implements that chain. That is the novelty.

Minimum credible experiment: 60M-125M parameters, 3-8B FineWeb-Edu tokens, 3:1 hybrid, four arms (proposed, Gated DeltaNet, Mamba-2, DeltaNet) at two or three matched state sizes, three seeds, LR fixed by pilot - roughly 120-1,000 GPU-hours. Report MQAR at P in {64,128,256,512} (never 8), the SWDE/FDA/SQuAD recall suite, S-NIAH extrapolation, a retention-vs-age curve, and lm-eval as a no-regression check.

Three risks to fix before building: FLOP-matching is not GPU-wall-clock matching for sparse gathers (use block sparsity, benchmark first); derive fan-in from Litwin-Kumar rather than copying 7; and test the inferred-precision gate only out of distribution, or P3 will return null for exactly phase 1's reason.

### Established (with sources)

- METHOD CAVEAT, READ FIRST. Primary research sources could not be reached when this note was written. Everything below is from background knowledge (to May 2026) or from docs/aif/LITERATURE.md. NOTHING from June-September 2026 is covered. Confidence is tagged per item; every number tagged 'approx' must be re-verified against the paper before it enters a write-up.
- No pure sub-quadratic model is state of the art at any scale in 2025-2026. Every frontier efficient model is a HYBRID: a majority of linear/recurrent layers plus a minority of softmax-attention layers, typically 3:1 to 8:1. Evidence: Waleffe et al., 'An Empirical Study of Mamba-based Language Models' (NVIDIA, arXiv 2406.07887, 2024) shows at 8B/3.5T tokens that a Mamba-2 + 4-attention + MLP hybrid beats BOTH pure Mamba-2 and a pure transformer; Lieber et al., 'Jamba' (arXiv 2403.19887, 2024); Ren et al., 'Samba: Simple Hybrid State Space Models' (arXiv 2406.07522, 2024). CONFIDENCE high. CONSEQUENCE for this project: a fly-derived memory must be a layer inside a hybrid, never a replacement for attention.
- The accepted axis for judging a new recurrent/memory layer is the RECALL-vs-RECURRENT-STATE-SIZE Pareto frontier, not perplexity alone. Arora et al., 'Zoology: Measuring and Improving Recall in Efficient Language Models' (ICLR 2024, arXiv 2312.04927) introduced MQAR and showed associative-recall failures explain the large majority of the perplexity gap between attention and gated-convolution models. Arora et al., 'Simple linear attention language models balance the recall-throughput tradeoff' (ICML 2024, arXiv 2402.18668, 'Based') made the tradeoff explicit and gave the real-world recall suite (SWDE, FDA, SQuAD, TriviaQA, NaturalQuestions, DROP) that reviewers now expect. Arora et al., 'Just Read Twice' (COLM 2024, arXiv 2407.05483) is the follow-up. CONFIDENCE high. This is the single most important framing for the next phase: the project's own numpy capacity result (docs/phase3/MEMORY_CAPACITY.md) is a point on exactly this frontier and nobody in the repo has connected it to this literature by name.
- The DeltaNet lineage is the current best-in-class linear layer at 125M-3B and is the correct primary control. Schlag, Irie, Schmidhuber, 'Linear Transformers Are Secretly Fast Weight Programmers' (ICML 2021, arXiv 2102.11174) established the delta rule for fast weights; Yang, Wang, Zhang, Kim, Shen, Kim, 'Parallelizing Linear Transformers with the Delta Rule over Sequence Length' (NeurIPS 2024, arXiv 2406.06484) made it trainable at scale via a WY/chunkwise representation; Yang, Kautz, Hatamizadeh, 'Gated Delta Networks: Improving Mamba2 with Delta Rule' (ICLR 2025, arXiv 2412.06464) added a scalar forget gate and is the model the repo already uses as its twin. Yang et al., 'Gated Linear Attention' (ICML 2024, arXiv 2312.06635) is the other standard baseline. CONFIDENCE high.
- Mamba lineage: Gu & Dao, 'Mamba' (arXiv 2312.00752, COLM 2024); Dao & Gu, 'Transformers are SSMs: ... State Space Duality' (ICML 2024, arXiv 2405.21060, Mamba-2). MAMBA-3 exists as a 2026 work (I believe an ICLR 2026 submission titled roughly 'Mamba-3: Improved Sequential Modeling using State Space Principles') whose claimed changes are a trapezoidal rather than Euler discretisation, a complex/rotational state update improving state tracking, and a multi-input-multi-output variant raising decode arithmetic intensity. CONFIDENCE medium on existence and mechanism, LOW on any specific number: I could not retrieve it and have no verified benchmark table. Do not cite Mamba-3 numbers without fetching the paper.
- RWKV-7 'Goose' (Peng et al., arXiv 2503.14456, 2025) generalises the delta rule to vector-valued in-context learning rates and a relaxed value-replacement rule. Its important claim is THEORETICAL: RWKV-7 can recognise any regular language and perform S5 state tracking, which places it beyond TC0 and therefore strictly above both softmax attention and diagonal SSMs under standard complexity assumptions. Predecessors: Peng et al., 'RWKV: Reinventing RNNs for the Transformer Era' (EMNLP Findings 2023, arXiv 2305.13048); 'Eagle and Finch' (COLM 2024, arXiv 2404.05892). CONFIDENCE high on the architecture and the expressivity claim; medium on the '3B multilingual SOTA at its scale' marketing claim. RWKV-8 'Heron' was in development in 2026 - I have no verified results.
- xLSTM: Beck et al., 'xLSTM: Extended Long Short-Term Memory' (NeurIPS 2024, arXiv 2405.04517) combines sLSTM (scalar memory with memory mixing, non-parallelisable) and mLSTM (matrix memory with a covariance/outer-product update, parallelisable). Beck et al., 'xLSTM 7B: A Recurrent LLM for Fast and Efficient Inference' (arXiv 2503.13427, 2025): 7B on ~2.3T DCLM tokens, competitive with Llama-2/3 7B and Mamba 7B on lm-eval, with the fastest reported 7B inference throughput at the time. CONFIDENCE high on existence, medium on the exact lm-eval deltas.
- Test-time-learning memory is the live 2025-2026 frontier and is exactly the mushroom-body analogue. Sun et al., 'Learning to (Learn at Test Time)' (arXiv 2407.04620, 2024, TTT-Linear/TTT-MLP); Behrouz, Zhong, Mirrokni, 'Titans: Learning to Memorize at Test Time' (arXiv 2501.00663, 2025) - deep neural memory with a surprise signal (gradient of the memory loss) plus momentum plus weight decay as forgetting, variants MAC/MAG/MAL; Behrouz et al., 'ATLAS: Learning to Optimally Memorize the Context at Test Time' (arXiv 2505.23735, 2025) - Omega rule over a sliding window, Muon-style memory optimiser, polynomial feature maps; Behrouz et al., 'It's All Connected' (arXiv 2504.13173, 2025, MIRAS) which frames the whole design space as four choices (memory architecture, attentional-bias objective, retention gate, optimiser) and explicitly leaves most of it unexplored. 'Test-Time Training Done Right' (LaCT, arXiv 2505.23884, ICLR 2026 per the repo sweep) does large-chunk TTT at 2K-1M chunk size and reports about 70 percent A100 utilisation. CONFIDENCE high on the papers; the repo's own sweep records an independent reimplementation ('Titans Revisited', arXiv 2510.09551) reporting that Titans does NOT always beat established baselines and that the gain is concentrated in the neural-memory component.
- Two hard theoretical limits the next design must respect. (a) Jelassi, Brandfonbrener, Kakade, Malach, 'Repeat After Me: Transformers are Better than State Space Models at Copying' (ICML 2024, arXiv 2402.01032): a fixed-size recurrent state provably cannot copy strings longer than its state capacity, while a transformer can; this is an information-theoretic bound, not a training artefact. (b) Merrill, Petty, Sabharwal, 'The Illusion of State in State-Space Models' (ICML 2024, arXiv 2404.08819): S4/Mamba-style SSMs lie in TC0 and cannot do genuine state tracking. Partial fixes exist: Grazzi et al., 'Unlocking State-Tracking in Linear RNNs Through Negative Eigenvalues' (ICLR 2025, arXiv 2411.12537) and Siems et al., 'DeltaProduct' (arXiv 2502.10297, 2025, multiple Householder delta steps per token). CONFIDENCE high. CONSEQUENCE: any claim of 'lossless memory' from a constant-size state is false a priori; the fly-derived claim must be about capacity PER BIT of state and about graceful degradation, not losslessness.
- Absolute SOTA at 125M-3B in 2026 is a DATA claim, not an architecture claim, and is therefore unreachable for this project. Modern small models are overtrained by two to three orders of magnitude relative to Chinchilla-optimal (Hoffmann et al., NeurIPS 2022, arXiv 2203.15556, ~20 tokens/param): Llama-3.2-1B ~9T tokens (~9,000 tokens/param), SmolLM3-3B ~11T tokens, the Qwen3 family ~36T tokens, Gemma-3-1B ~2T, OLMo-2-1B ~4T. CONFIDENCE high on the pattern, medium on each individual token count. CONSEQUENCE: the only honest claim available is a matched-data, matched-token, matched-compute, single-variable architecture comparison, reported as a Pareto frontier.
- MoE is the standard way to buy capacity per active FLOP at small active-parameter budgets: Muennighoff et al., 'OLMoE-1B-7B' (ICLR 2025, arXiv 2409.02060) - 7B total / 1B active on 5T tokens, outperforming dense models of similar ACTIVE size; Qwen3-30B-A3B and Qwen3-Next-80B-A3B; IBM Granite 4.0 (2025). CONFIDENCE high for OLMoE, medium for the 2025 releases. CONSEQUENCE: a 'SOTA at 3B active' claim would require MoE, which is out of budget on one 16 GB GPU; the defined regime must be dense.
- Long-context evaluation has converged on a small set of suites. Hsieh et al., 'RULER' (COLM 2024, arXiv 2404.06654) - 13 synthetic tasks in 4 families (NIAH variants, multi-hop variable tracing, aggregation, QA), with 'effective context length' defined as the longest length at which a model still beats the Llama-2-7B 4K baseline; its headline finding is that most models claiming 32K-128K fail far below the claimed length. Kuratov et al., 'BABILong' (NeurIPS 2024 D&B, arXiv 2406.10149) - bAbI tasks embedded in PG19 text up to 10M tokens; GPT-4 and RAG use only ~10-20 percent of the available context effectively, while fine-tuned recurrent-memory models (RMT, ARMT: Rodkin et al., arXiv 2407.04841) reach 1M-50M tokens on the synthetic tasks. Bai et al., 'LongBench v2' (ACL 2025, arXiv 2412.15204) - 503 multiple-choice items over 8k-2M words; human experts score 53.7 percent under a 15-minute limit, the best model at release (o1-preview) 57.7 percent, direct-answer models ~50 percent. Yen et al., 'HELMET' (ICLR 2025, arXiv 2410.02694). Modarressi et al., 'NoLiMa' (ICML 2025, arXiv 2502.05167) removes literal lexical matches from NIAH and reports that 11 of 12 tested models fall below 50 percent of their own short-context baseline by 32k. CONFIDENCE high on suite identity and methodology, medium on individual model scores.
- In-context-learning evaluation beyond MQAR: Bertsch et al., 'In-Context Learning with Long-Context Models' (arXiv 2405.00200, 2024) uses many-shot ICL on TREC, Banking-77, Clinic-150, NLU with hundreds to thousands of demonstrations; Agarwal et al., 'Many-Shot In-Context Learning' (NeurIPS 2024, arXiv 2404.11018). For in-context learning of genuinely NEW skills the community currently uses ARC-AGI: Akyurek et al., 'The Surprising Effectiveness of Test-Time Training for Abstract Reasoning' (arXiv 2411.07279, 2024) reports ~53 percent on the ARC public validation set with an 8B model plus test-time training, roughly a 6x improvement over the fine-tuned base. CONFIDENCE high.
- Continual learning at LM scale currently WORKS ONLY BY REHEARSAL, which is the open door. Ibrahim et al., 'Simple and Scalable Strategies to Continually Pre-train LLMs' (COLM 2024, arXiv 2403.08763) shows that learning-rate re-warming plus replay of roughly 5 percent of the old data recovers full-retraining performance. That is rehearsal, not forgetting-free learning. The lifelong knowledge-editing line (Hartvigsen et al., 'GRACE', NeurIPS 2023, arXiv 2211.11031; Wang et al., 'WISE', NeurIPS 2024, arXiv 2405.14768; Meng et al. ROME/MEMIT 2022-2023) degrades after hundreds to a few thousand sequential edits. CONFIDENCE high. This is the clearest genuinely open problem that maps onto fly biology (sleep-dependent consolidation).
- The connectome-as-ML-prior question has a published POSITIVE counterexample that sharpens why phase 1 was null: Lappalainen et al., 'Connectome-constrained networks predict neural activity across the fly visual system' (Nature 634:1132-1140, 2024) shows connectome constraints materially improve prediction of MEASURED NEURAL ACTIVITY; and Shiu, Sterne et al., 'A Drosophila computational brain model reveals sensorimotor processing' (Nature 634:210-219, 2024) shows a whole-brain leaky-integrate-and-fire simulation reproduces real sensorimotor behaviour (e.g. sugar-evoked proboscis extension). Connectome structure transfers to the fly's own tasks and to predicting the fly's own biology; it does not transfer to text. Phase 1's null is consistent with the whole literature, not anomalous. Anatomy sources: Scheffer et al. (eLife 9:e57443, 2020, hemibrain); Dorkenwald et al. (Nature 634:124-138, 2024, FlyWire); Schlegel et al. (Nature 634:139-152, 2024). CONFIDENCE high.
- The single strongest piece of established mathematics that the repo has NOT yet used: Benna & Fusi, 'Computational principles of synaptic memory consolidation' (Nature Neuroscience 19:1697-1706, 2016). A synapse modelled as a chain of coupled variables with geometrically increasing time constants achieves memory lifetime scaling LINEARLY in the number of synapses N, versus sqrt(N) for the best bistable-cascade synapse and log(N) for a simple bounded synapse, with signal-to-noise ratio decaying as a power law (~1/sqrt(t)) instead of exponentially. The predecessor is Fusi, Drew & Abbott, 'Cascade models of synaptically stored memories' (Neuron 45:599-611, 2005). CONFIDENCE high (these are well-known, heavily cited results). This is a theorem-grade justification for the repo's P5/P6 principles (log-spaced compartments plus consolidation) and, to my knowledge as of May 2026, NO test-time-learning language model implements a Benna-Fusi consolidation chain. That gap is the most defensible novelty in the whole pivot.
- The second piece of established mathematics the repo has not used: Dambre, Verstraeten, Schrauwen & Massar, 'Information processing capacity of dynamical systems' (Scientific Reports 2:514, 2012). The total information-processing capacity of any fading-memory dynamical system with N state variables is EXACTLY N, distributed across linear memory and nonlinear transformations - a conservation law. CONFIDENCE high. This is the rigorous reason the recall-vs-state-size Pareto exists, the reason reservoirs cannot be backbones (Jaeger, ESN, GMD Report 148, 2001; Maass, Natschlaeger, Markram, Neural Computation 14:2531-2560, 2002), and the reason 'more memory' must come either from more state or from writing into slow weights. Stating this bound up front makes the next phase's claim precise: the claim can only ever be about capacity PER STATE VARIABLE, never about exceeding N.
- Fly-specific theory that already has theorem status. (a) Dasgupta, Stevens & Navlakha, 'A neural algorithm for a fundamental computing problem' (Science 358:793-796, 2017): the PN->KC circuit is a locality-sensitive hash - sparse binary random projection that EXPANDS dimensionality (~50 glomeruli to ~2,000 Kenyon cells) with small fan-in and a winner-take-all top ~5 percent; it provably beats classical LSH for nearest-neighbour search in the low-dimensional-input regime. (b) Litwin-Kumar, Harris, Axel, Sompolinsky & Abbott, 'Optimal Degrees of Synaptic Connectivity' (Neuron 93:1153-1164, 2017): the claw number K that maximises the dimensionality of the KC representation under noise is K_opt ~ 6-10 for the fly's parameters, matching the measured ~6-7 claws; crucially K_opt is a FUNCTION of input dimension and noise, it is not a universal constant. (c) Kanerva, 'Sparse Distributed Memory' (MIT Press, 1988) for the capacity theory. (d) Bricken & Pehlevan, 'Attention Approximates Sparse Distributed Memory' (NeurIPS 2021, arXiv 2111.05498) shows softmax attention's read is an SDM read - i.e. attention and the mushroom body are the same operation with different address distributions. CONFIDENCE high. CONSEQUENCE: do NOT copy fan-in 7; DERIVE K_opt from the Litwin-Kumar formula at the model's actual key dimension and noise level, and report the derivation. That alone is a more defensible scientific move than anything in phases 1-2.
- Mushroom-body plasticity biology that grounds the 'learning rate is inferred' axiom. Aso et al. (eLife 3:e04577, 2014) - 15 compartments, ~21 dopaminergic neuron types, ~34 MBON types, each compartment an independently modulated plasticity unit; Aso & Rubin (eLife 5:e16135, 2016) - dopaminergic neurons both write and update memories with compartment-specific time courses; Cohn, Morantte & Ruta (Cell 163:1742-1755, 2015) - state-dependent dopaminergic modulation of KC->MBON transmission; Handler et al. (Cell 178:60-75, 2019) - DopR1/DopR2 give bidirectional plasticity depending on the timing of dopamine relative to KC activity; Hige et al. (Neuron 88:985-998, 2015); Bennett, Philippides & Nowotny, 'Learning with reinforcement prediction errors in a model of the Drosophila mushroom body' (Nature Communications 12:2569, 2021) - the DAN-MBON recurrent loop computes a TD-like prediction error; Jiang & Litwin-Kumar (PLoS Computational Biology, 2021) - heterogeneous dopamine signalling. Consolidation: Isabel, Pascual & Preat (Science 304:1024-1027, 2004) on ARM vs LTM; Dag et al. (eLife 8:e42786, 2019) - neuronal reactivation during post-learning sleep consolidates LTM in Drosophila; Haynes, Christmann & Griffith (eLife 4:e03868, 2015) - dorsal fan-shaped body sleep neurons. Sparse coding measurements: Turner, Bazhenov & Laurent (J Neurophysiol 99:734-746, 2008); Honegger, Campbell & Turner (J Neurosci 31:11772-11785, 2011). CONFIDENCE high.

### Principles and their translation

| principle | biology / mathematics | translation to a sequence model | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Frame the entire next phase as a point on the recall-vs-recurrent-state Pareto frontier, not as a perplexity claim. The deliverable is a frontier plot with the new architecture dominating Gated DeltaNet / Mamba-2 / DeltaNet at two or more matched state sizes. | Dambre et al. (Sci Rep 2:514, 2012) proves total information-processing capacity of an N-state fading-memory system is exactly N, so capacity per state variable is the only well-posed efficiency axis. Jelassi et al. (ICML 2024, arXiv 2402.01032) proves a fixed state cannot copy beyond its capacity, so 'lossless' claims are a priori false. | Fix total recurrent bytes per layer at 3 levels (e.g. 32 KB / 128 KB / 512 KB fp16). Train each arm to the same token count with the same optimiser and a per-arm LR sweep. Plot MQAR accuracy and real-world recall (SWDE/FDA/SQuAD) against state bytes. Claim = strict dominance at >=2 of 3 state sizes by >2 seed-SD with bootstrap CI excluding 0, PLUS no lm-eval regression. This is the exact form reviewers accept (Arora et al. ICLR 2024 / ICML 2024). | established |
| The sparse high-dimensional address is a LARGE-STATE, LOW-ACTIVE-FRACTION design. Sell it as decode-bandwidth efficiency, not FLOP efficiency, and verify the gather is coalesced before building anything. | Fly hashing (Dasgupta et al., Science 2017) expands ~50 -> ~2,000 with ~6 inputs per cell and 5 percent k-WTA; sparse k-of-n codes have expected pairwise overlap k^2/n, giving near-orthogonal addresses. Kanerva (1988) supplies the SDM capacity theory. Litwin-Kumar et al. (Neuron 2017) gives K_opt as a derived function of input dimension and noise, not the constant 7. | CRITICAL ENGINEERING RISK the repo has not written down: docs/phase3/MEMORY_CAPACITY.md matches compute by FLOPs (d_key = k active rows). On a GPU that is NOT wall-clock matched - a k=100-of-2048 gather/scatter has terrible tensor-core utilisation compared with a dense 128x128 matmul, and could be 5-20x slower in practice. The honest framing is bandwidth at DECODE: reading k=100 rows of a 2048x64 state moves ~6.4k values per layer, versus ~16k for a dense 128x128 Gated DeltaNet state - a genuine ~2.5x bandwidth win IF the rows are contiguous. MITIGATION: use BLOCK-sparse addressing (blocks of 64-128 rows, k_blocks active) so it maps to tensor cores. Derive K_opt from the Litwin-Kumar formula at the model's real key dimension rather than copying 7, and report that derivation - it converts an arbitrary constant into a prediction. Benchmark tokens/s before committing, not after. | promising |
| Implement a Benna-Fusi consolidation chain as the memory's state, not a bank of independent decaying compartments. This is the most defensible novelty available to the project. | Benna & Fusi (Nature Neuroscience 19:1697-1706, 2016): a chain of coupled synaptic variables u_1..u_m with geometrically increasing time constants and bidirectional coupling (du_k/dt proportional to (u_{k-1} - u_k) - (u_k - u_{k+1}), with level-dependent conductances) yields memory lifetime scaling as N rather than sqrt(N), and power-law rather than exponential SNR decay. Fusi, Drew & Abbott (Neuron 45:599-611, 2005) is the cascade predecessor. Biological correlate: ARM vs LTM stages (Isabel et al., Science 2004) and sleep-dependent reactivation (Dag et al., eLife 2019). | Replace the repo's four independent compartments (decay 4/16/64/256) with a COUPLED chain, which is the version with the proof. The plastic fast weights become m coupled matrices; writes enter u_1, coupling diffuses information toward slower levels, reads take a weighted sum. To my knowledge as of May 2026, no test-time-learning LM (TTT, Titans, ATLAS, LaCT, Gated DeltaNet, Kimi Linear) implements this. The claimable result: retention of a fact written once at position p, measured at p + 10^3 to p + 10^6 tokens, against Gated DeltaNet and Titans twins - a retention-vs-age curve that is power-law where the controls are exponential. That plot alone is a paper. | promising |
| The inferred-precision write rate can only beat a learned gate OUT OF DISTRIBUTION. Design the experiment so the learned gate is tested on non-stationarity statistics it never saw in training - otherwise phase 1's objection recurs verbatim. | Dopamine sets plasticity magnitude and sign as a function of prediction error and timing (Handler et al., Cell 2019; Cohn et al., Cell 2015; Bennett et al., Nat Commun 2021 for the TD-error reading). Mathematically the optimal write rate under a random-walk prior on the stored value is the Kalman gain, k_t = P_t/(P_t + R), with P updated from the observed innovation - i.e. learning rate = inferred precision ratio. See Aitchison, 'Bayesian filtering unifies adaptive and non-adaptive neural network optimization methods' (NeurIPS 2020, arXiv 1807.07540) for the ML statement (CONFIDENCE medium on exact venue). | The repo's phase-1 lesson is correct: a learned gate with the same inputs contains any fixed rule as a special case and backprop finds it IN DISTRIBUTION. The only escape is generalisation. Train all arms at drift rate lambda_train; evaluate at lambda_test in {0.1 lambda, 10 lambda} and at observation noise levels absent from training. Pre-register that the claim is generalisation of the gate, not in-distribution accuracy. This is the single most important methodological correction I can offer: without it, P3 in docs/phase3/EXPERIMENT_PLAN.md will return null for exactly the reason phase 1 did. Secondary value: MIRAS (arXiv 2504.13173) explicitly leaves the retention-gate design space open, so a principled gate with a regret bound is a recognised gap. | promising |
| Ship the memory as a drop-in layer inside a standard hybrid decoder (3:1 memory:sliding-window-attention), changing exactly one thing versus the control. | Not biology - it is the 2025-26 empirical consensus (Waleffe et al. arXiv 2406.07887; Jamba; Samba; and, with medium confidence, Qwen3-Next and Kimi Linear both at 3:1). | Single-variable swap is what makes a small-scale result readable. Identical tokenizer, data order, optimiser, depth, width, parameter count; only the mixer in the non-attention layers differs. Report the control's numbers from YOUR OWN runs, never from the control's paper - cross-paper number comparison is the fastest way to get a small-scale claim dismissed. | established |
| Choose the continual/streaming-adaptation regime as the second claim, because the incumbent solution there is rehearsal and rehearsal is precisely what an offline consolidation mechanism replaces. | Sleep-dependent consolidation in Drosophila: memory-specific neuronal reactivation during post-learning sleep consolidates LTM (Dag et al., eLife 8:e42786, 2019); dFB sleep neurons gate consolidation (Haynes et al., eLife 4:e03868, 2015). Mathematically this is generative replay from a fast store into slow weights, and Benna-Fusi gives the capacity argument for why staging helps. | Protocol: continually pre-train on a temporally ordered or domain-shifting stream; measure (i) forward transfer on the new domain, (ii) retained perplexity on held-out old domains, (iii) tokens of replay required. The accepted incumbent is Ibrahim et al. (COLM 2024, arXiv 2403.08763): LR re-warming plus ~5 percent replay. A claim of matching that at 1 percent or 0 percent stored replay, using self-generated replay from the fast store, is a recognisable result. RISK: continual-learning benchmarks are fragmented and reviewers discount small-scale CL claims more than they discount small-scale recall claims. Rank this second, not first. | speculative |
| Kill the energy claim and the spiking branch explicitly, in writing, with reasons - and get credit for the honesty. | Many fly neurons of interest are GRADED, not spiking (large monopolar cells in the lamina; much of central-complex signalling is graded/oscillatory), so a spiking implementation is not even the more faithful biology. The fly's efficiency derives from analog computation, 3D wiring and molecular integration, none of which survives a GPU. | There is no accepted energy benchmark for language models; an energy claim requires neuromorphic silicon (Loihi 2, SpiNNaker2, IBM NorthPole) and nobody has run a competitive LM on one. SNN LMs remain far behind: SpikeGPT (Zhu et al., arXiv 2302.13939, 2023) is ~260M parameters; SpikingBrain-7B (arXiv 2509.05276, 2025 - CONFIDENCE LOW, I could not verify it) claims training on under 2 percent of typical data with large long-context speedups on non-NVIDIA hardware, but I have no verified independent evaluation. Recommendation: state 'not claimable on available hardware' and move on. Reporting this as a reasoned exclusion strengthens the paper. | established |

### Numbers an engineer can use

- Hybrid ratios in 2025-26 frontier models: Jamba 1 attention : 7 Mamba; Nemotron-H roughly 8 percent attention layers; Samba 1:1 Mamba:sliding-window-attention; Qwen3-Next and Kimi Linear both reported at 3 linear : 1 attention (CONFIDENCE medium for the last two). Use 3:1 as the default for the project's hybrid.
- Training FLOPs per token, transformer: 6N for parameters plus 12 * n_layer * d_model * L_ctx for attention. Worked example at 1.3B (n_layer 24, d_model 2048): 6N = 7.8e9; attention term at L=4096 = 12*24*2048*4096 = 2.42e9, i.e. 31 percent overhead; at L=32768 = 1.93e10, i.e. 248 percent - 2.5x the parameter term. CONSEQUENCE: at 2k-4k context a linear layer buys almost no training FLOPs; the real prize is decode memory bandwidth. CONFIDENCE high (arithmetic).
- Decode-time state: a transformer reads 2 * n_layer * L * d_kv * bytes of KV cache per token. For an 8B-class model with GQA (n_layer 32, d_kv 1024, fp16) that is ~131 KB per token of context, i.e. ~4.3 GB at 32k context. A linear/recurrent model reads a CONSTANT state, typically ~1-6 MB total at 1.3B. That is roughly a 1000x difference at 32k. This, not FLOPs, is the efficiency argument to make. CONFIDENCE high on the formula, medium on the per-model constants.
- Tokens-per-parameter in 2026 small models, versus Chinchilla-optimal 20: Llama-3.2-1B ~9T tokens (~9,000 tok/param, ~450x Chinchilla); SmolLM3-3B ~11T; Qwen3 family pretraining ~36T; Gemma-3-1B ~2T; OLMo-2-1B ~4T. CONFIDENCE medium on individual counts, high on the pattern. CONSEQUENCE: at 125M params on <10B tokens the project is ~1000x below the data budget of any released small model, so absolute benchmark numbers will look bad and must never be compared to published model cards.
- Standard linear-attention paper recipe and approximate results (the setting the project should mirror): 1.3B parameters, 100B FineWeb-Edu tokens, 2k-4k context. Approximate WikiText perplexity / LAMBADA perplexity / lm-eval zero-shot average over ARC-e, ARC-c, HellaSwag, PIQA, WinoGrande, LAMBADA: Transformer++ ~16.9 / ~13.4 / ~51; Mamba-2 ~16.6 / ~12.6 / ~51; Gated DeltaNet ~16.4 / ~12.2 / ~52; Gated DeltaNet-H2 hybrid ~53. CONFIDENCE LOW-MEDIUM on these specific values - I am reconstructing the GDN paper table from memory and could not fetch it. Re-verify against arXiv 2412.06464 before use. The ORDERING (hybrid > GDN > Mamba-2 ~ Transformer++) is what I am confident about.
- Smaller-scale calibration: at 340M-400M parameters on 15-30B tokens (the Titans/Gated-DeltaNet small setting), lm-eval zero-shot averages land around 40-44 percent; at 125-160M on 5-10B tokens expect roughly 35-40 percent. CONFIDENCE medium. At 125M/8B tokens do NOT expect MMLU above chance (25 percent); exclude MMLU from the no-regression suite.
- MQAR (Zoology, ICLR 2024, arXiv 2312.04927) is the cheap discriminative task: vocabulary 8k-32k, key-value pairs from 8 up to 512, sequence lengths 64 to 2048. The repo's phase-1 H1 ran at 8 pairs, which is far inside the trivial regime for a d_key=64 dense delta memory (capacity ~d_key). The repo's own numpy study (docs/phase3/MEMORY_CAPACITY.md) shows the interesting regime starts at P >= 2*d_key: at P=128 with 64 active rows, sparse delta 0.90 vs dense delta 0.25. RUN MQAR AT P in {64, 128, 256, 512}, never at 8.
- Real-world recall suite to use verbatim from Based (ICML 2024, arXiv 2402.18668) and Just Read Twice (arXiv 2407.05483): SWDE, FDA, SQuAD, TriviaQA, NaturalQuestions, DROP, scored as information-extraction accuracy. These are cheap zero-shot evaluations on an already-trained model and are what reviewers expect alongside MQAR.
- RULER (arXiv 2404.06654): baseline threshold is Llama-2-7B at 4K scoring 85.6; 'effective context' is the longest length still above it. Reported effective lengths were far below claimed ones (e.g. models advertising 128K typically effective at 32K; GPT-4 around 64K). CONFIDENCE medium on individual numbers. For a 125M model trained at 2k, the only meaningful RULER subset is S-NIAH-1/2/3 at 1k/2k/4k/8k, used as a LENGTH-EXTRAPOLATION test (train 2k, test 4k-8k).
- BABILong (NeurIPS 2024 D&B, arXiv 2406.10149): LLMs including GPT-4 use effectively only ~10-20 percent of the provided context; fine-tuned recurrent-memory transformers (RMT, and ARMT at arXiv 2407.04841) reach 1M and up to ~50M tokens on the synthetic bAbI tasks. The repo's sweep records ATLAS holding >80 percent at 10M context where Titans degrades. CONFIDENCE medium. BABILong qa1-qa5 at 4k-128k is the right long-horizon retention test for this project and is cheap to run on an already-trained model.
- LongBench v2 (arXiv 2412.15204): 503 multiple-choice items, 8k-2M words, 6 categories; human experts 53.7 percent under a 15-minute limit; best model at release (o1-preview) 57.7 percent; direct-answer models ~50 percent, i.e. barely above the 25 percent chance floor for 4-way items but far below competence. NOT usable at 125M - list it only as the eventual scale-up target.
- NoLiMa (ICML 2025, arXiv 2502.05167): removing literal lexical overlap from needle-in-a-haystack causes 11 of 12 tested models to fall below 50 percent of their own short-context baseline by 32k context. CONFIDENCE medium-high. This is the sharpest published statement that long-context 'success' is largely lexical matching, and it is the benchmark most likely to reward a genuine associative memory.
- Test-time training on ARC (arXiv 2411.07279, 2024): ~53 percent on the ARC public validation set with an 8B model plus TTT, roughly 6x the fine-tuned base. CONFIDENCE medium-high. This is the reference point for 'in-context learning of new skills' and is out of reach at 125M.
- Continual pre-training incumbent (COLM 2024, arXiv 2403.08763): learning-rate re-warming plus replay of ~5 percent of the original data matches full retraining. Any continual-learning claim from this project must be stated as 'matches this at X percent replay' with X well below 5.
- Realistic compute budget on one 16 GB GPU. At 125M parameters and 8B tokens, training FLOPs = 6*1.25e8*8e9 = 6.0e18. At ~60 TFLOP/s effective bf16 (a 4090-class card at realistic MFU) that is ~28 hours per run. A 4-arm x 3-state-size x 3-seed grid = 36 runs ~ 1,000 GPU-hours ~ 6 weeks: TOO MUCH. Scaled-down feasible unit: 60M parameters x 3B tokens = 1.1e18 FLOPs ~ 5 hours per run; 4 arms x 2 state sizes x 3 seeds = 24 runs ~ 120 GPU-hours ~ 5 days. That IS feasible and is the honest maximum. Pick the learning rate once per arm with a 300M-token pilot rather than sweeping inside the grid.
- Fly energy figures, for rhetoric only, never for a benchmark claim: human brain ~20 W for ~8.6e10 neurons, i.e. ~2.3e-10 W per neuron; scaling to ~1.4e5 fly neurons gives an ORDER-OF-MAGNITUDE ESTIMATE of ~10-30 microwatts for the fly brain. CONFIDENCE LOW - this is an extrapolation, not a measurement, and must be labelled as such. An H100 SXM draws ~700 W. There is no accepted joules-per-token benchmark for language models, so no energy claim is defensible.

### Dead ends

- Absolute lm-eval or MMLU SOTA at 125M-3B. Unreachable: the incumbents are trained on 2T-36T tokens (Llama-3.2-1B ~9T, SmolLM3-3B ~11T, Qwen3 family ~36T) and the project's budget is under 10B. Any table comparing the project's model to a published model card will be read as either naive or dishonest. Compare only against arms YOU trained on identical data.
- Perplexity-per-FLOP against a well-tuned transformer or against Gated DeltaNet on generic text. No biologically derived architecture has ever won this and the repo's own REFLECTION.md already concedes it. At 2k-4k context the attention FLOP term is only ~31 percent of the parameter term, so there is barely any FLOP headroom to win.
- Pure recurrent / pure linear models as the whole model. Ruled out empirically (Waleffe et al., arXiv 2406.07887: hybrid beats pure Mamba-2 AND pure transformer at 8B/3.5T) and theoretically (Jelassi et al., ICML 2024, arXiv 2402.01032: a fixed-size state provably cannot copy beyond its capacity; Merrill et al., ICML 2024, arXiv 2404.08819: SSMs sit in TC0 and cannot state-track).
- Reservoir computing as a backbone. Dambre et al. (Sci Rep 2:514, 2012) caps total processing capacity at the state dimension N, and there is no competitive reservoir language model at any scale. Echo state networks (Jaeger, GMD Report 148, 2001) and liquid state machines (Maass et al., Neural Computation 14:2531-2560, 2002) remain useful only for low-dimensional dynamical-systems tasks (see also next-generation RC: Gauthier et al., Nature Communications 12:5564, 2021). Keep random expansion as an ADDRESS SPACE, which is what the mushroom body actually is; discard the reservoir-as-backbone idea.
- Backprop-free learning for the slow weights (Forward-Forward, equilibrium propagation, predictive coding as a backprop substitute). Bartunov et al., 'Assessing the scalability of biologically-motivated deep learning algorithms' (NeurIPS 2018, arXiv 1807.04587) shows the gap widens with scale; Hinton, 'The Forward-Forward Algorithm' (arXiv 2212.13345, 2022) has not been scaled past small vision; the repo's own literature sweep records that canonical state-based predictive coding suffers exponential signal decay past ~5-7 layers. Use local rules ONLY for the fast weights, where they are natural and where the fly actually uses them.
- Spiking neural networks as the model. Quality gap is large (SpikeGPT, arXiv 2302.13939, is ~260M parameters); the energy claim requires neuromorphic hardware nobody has run a competitive LM on; and it is not even the more faithful biology, since many fly neurons of interest signal in graded fashion rather than by spikes. SpikingBrain-7B (arXiv 2509.05276, 2025) may complicate this - CONFIDENCE LOW, unverified, fetch before relying on it.
- Contrastive learning as a generative-modelling objective for the backbone. Contrastive objectives (SimCSE, Gao et al., EMNLP 2021) produce embeddings, not next-token distributions; noise-contrastive estimation (Gutmann & Hyvarinen 2010; Mnih & Teh, ICML 2012) is an obsolete softmax approximation; contrastive DECODING (Li et al., ACL 2023; DoLa, Chuang et al., ICLR 2024) is an inference-time trick over an already-trained model, not an architecture. Contrastive learning has a legitimate but narrow role here: shaping the memory's ADDRESS space so that distinct items map to low-overlap sparse codes - i.e. as a regulariser on the hash, not as the training objective.
- Per-token iterative inference on top of an amortised forward pass (phase 2's AIF-H1). Already null in-repo at +0.7 seed-SD, and the theory predicts it: the amortisation gap closes with training, so the inner loop buys nothing a trained network cannot do in one pass. DO NOT REVISIT. The exception worth keeping is the repo's own conclusion - inferred precision as a LEARNING RATE on the fast weights, which is a different mechanism entirely.
- The connectome as a wiring prior. Closed by phase 1 and consistent with the external literature: connectome structure transfers to the fly's own sensorimotor tasks and to predicting the fly's own neural activity (Lappalainen et al., Nature 634:1132-1140, 2024; Shiu et al., Nature 634:210-219, 2024), not to text. Report this as a finding, cite the positive counterexamples to show the null is well-understood rather than a failure.
- Fixed biological gates versus learned gates evaluated IN DISTRIBUTION. Structurally unwinnable: the learned gate's hypothesis class contains the fixed rule. The repo already learned this at phase-1 H1. The ONLY version worth running is out-of-distribution generalisation of the gate (see principle 4). If the next phase reruns P3 in-distribution it will get another null for a reason that is already known.
- Claiming 'lossless long-term memory beyond the window'. Information-theoretically impossible from a constant-size state (Jelassi et al. 2024). The claimable version is GRACEFUL degradation: power-law rather than exponential retention decay, which is exactly what Benna & Fusi (Nature Neuroscience 2016) proves a coupled multi-timescale chain delivers. Phrase the claim that way from the start.
- Energy efficiency as a headline. No accepted joules-per-token benchmark for language models exists, and the project has no neuromorphic hardware. State the exclusion and its reason; do not attempt the claim.

### Open questions

- Post-cutoff gap: I could verify nothing from June-September 2026. Before the design is frozen, someone with working network access must fetch and check (a) the Mamba-3 paper and its benchmark table, (b) Kimi Linear / Kimi Delta Attention (I believe arXiv 2510.26692, Oct 2025 - verify id, and the claimed 75 percent KV-cache reduction, ~6x decode at 1M, RULER-128k numbers), (c) Qwen3-Next-80B-A3B's architecture report, (d) whether RWKV-8 'Heron' has published results, (e) the exact lm-eval table in Gated DeltaNet (arXiv 2412.06464) since my reconstructed numbers are low-confidence, (f) SpikingBrain-7B (arXiv 2509.05276), (g) whether any 2026 work has already put a Benna-Fusi consolidation chain into a sequence model - this is the novelty claim and it must be checked, not assumed.
- Does the sparse-addressing capacity advantage survive LEARNED keys? The repo's numpy study uses random keys. If the model learns its key projection, backprop will spread keys toward orthogonality on its own, potentially erasing the advantage of random sparse hashing. This is the most likely way P1 turns null, and it should be tested first, cheaply, with a learned dense expansion of the same width as the explicit control the plan already specifies.
- What is K_opt (the fly's 'claw number') at the model's actual key dimension? Litwin-Kumar et al. (Neuron 2017) gives K_opt as a function of input dimensionality and noise, yielding ~6-10 for the fly's ~50 glomeruli. At d_key = 256-768 the formula predicts a different, larger value. Deriving and then empirically confirming K_opt would be a small but genuinely novel scientific result, and is far stronger than copying 7.
- Is block-sparse addressing (blocks of 64-128 rows) enough to recover tensor-core throughput without destroying the near-orthogonality that makes sparse codes work? Expected overlap of two k-of-n codes is k^2/n for unstructured codes; block structure raises correlation. Nobody has, to my knowledge, measured this tradeoff for a test-time memory. Needs a measurement before the GPU phase.
- Is there a regret bound for the inferred-precision (Kalman-gain) write rule in the delta-rule fast-weight setting? MIRAS (arXiv 2504.13173) frames the retention gate as an open design choice but I am not aware of any published regret analysis. A proof here would be a standalone theory contribution independent of whether the experiments win.
- Which benchmark actually rewards consolidation rather than a bigger state? BABILong and RULER both admit 'bigger state' solutions. A retention-vs-age curve (fact written once at position p, queried at p + 10^3..10^6) at MATCHED total state bytes is the discriminating measurement, and I am not aware of a standard benchmark that reports it. The project may have to define it - which is a contribution if the protocol is clean and released, and a weakness if reviewers read it as a bespoke benchmark chosen to win.
- Do the fly's compartment time constants correspond to anything at the scale of a 125M model's token stream? The MB's compartments differ over seconds to days of an animal's life; the repo's 4/16/64/256 mapping to token counts is an assumption with no biological warrant. Benna-Fusi at least tells you the chain should be geometric and how many levels m you need for a given horizon (m ~ log of the horizon ratio); use that rather than picking constants.
- Is a small-scale (60M-125M) Pareto result actually publishable in 2026, or has the bar moved to 1.3B/100B tokens? My honest read is that the recall/Pareto framing is the one place where a controlled sub-350M result still gets engaged with - Zoology itself ran at that scale - but I could not check 2026 reviewing norms. Worth asking someone who reviewed for ICLR 2026.
- Would a clean, well-controlled NEGATIVE result on the whole programme be publishable? I believe yes (the NeurIPS 'I Can't Believe It's Not Better' workshop and similar venues exist for exactly this, and the project's pre-registration discipline is unusually strong), but this should be confirmed, because it materially changes the risk calculus: it means the project has a publishable outcome either way.

### Relevance to language models

Direct and specific. The 2025-26 frontier at 125M-3B has converged on one shape - a hybrid of a fast test-time-learning memory layer with a minority of softmax-attention layers - and the open design space inside that memory layer is precisely the mushroom body's design space. MIRAS (arXiv 2504.13173) names the four open choices as memory architecture, attentional-bias objective, retention gate and optimiser; the fly supplies a principled candidate for three of them (sparse high-dimensional addressing, a dopamine-gated three-factor write whose rate is an inferred precision, and multi-timescale retention with offline consolidation). The bridge is already proved in both directions: Bricken & Pehlevan (NeurIPS 2021, arXiv 2111.05498) show softmax attention's read IS a sparse-distributed-memory read, and Schlag, Irie & Schmidhuber (ICML 2021, arXiv 2102.11174) show linear attention IS a fast-weight memory with a delta write. Gated DeltaNet, Titans, TTT and LaCT are therefore not competitors from another tradition - they are undermodelled mushroom bodies, each with a single-timescale state, a hand-designed or learned gate, and a dense address. The two things the fly has that none of them has are (i) a coupled multi-timescale consolidation chain with a proved lifetime-vs-capacity advantage (Benna & Fusi, Nature Neuroscience 19:1697-1706, 2016 - linear-in-N lifetime and power-law SNR decay versus sqrt(N) and exponential), and (ii) a write rate that is inferred online rather than trained. Neither has been tried in a language model as far as I can determine as of May 2026. The realistic ceiling is not perplexity per FLOP; it is capacity per bit of recurrent state and retention per bit as a function of age, which is exactly the axis the field already uses (Arora et al., ICLR 2024 and ICML 2024) and exactly the axis on which the fly was optimised. That coincidence - a 1.4e5-neuron controller built for one-shot learning under a hard state budget, meeting a field that has just discovered its own hard state budget - is the scientific case for the pivot, and it is a real one.
